The condition is `ControlledSinceUpkeep >= UpkeepsBegun - 1` (`game.CameUnderControlSinceLastUpkeepForEffect`), read as the upkeep begins and again on resolution, so a permanent that left, came back as a new object or changed hands in response is not charged; counting upkeeps rather than turns means a skipped upkeep is no "last upkeep" and a permanent that entered in the untap step is charged once. `effects.Echo`, `EchoPayment` and `EchoX` build the trigger (stamped `game.KeywordEcho`) with the cost read on resolution (Volcano Hellion's life total), and paying emits `EventEchoPaid` (`effects.WhenEchoIsPaid`, Shah of Naar Isle). The upkeep pay-unless prompt learned two payments besides mana (owner decision 3): `game.PayAction` "discard N cards" and "sacrifice N permanents" of a `PermanentQuery`, plain data on the prompt. The payer names the cards on the "yes" (`card_ids`, `ResolvePayUnlessWithCards`), validated before the prompt is dequeued; an unpayable "yes" is a decline (CR 118.3). `effects.CumulativeUpkeepPaying` multiplies the count by the age counters and pays them all or none (CR 702.24a). The view ships `pay_cards` (action, count, options; a discard's options go to the chooser only) and a derived `echo_due` marker on the permanent; the enumerator offers each way to pay plus the decline, and the bot pays when it can, giving up its least valuable cards. Two additive snapshot fields under v7; no closure routes. **Cards:** 50 echo cards (Acridian through Yavimaya Granger, including Deepcavern Imp, Rakdos Headliner and Skizzik Surger) and the cumulative upkeeps Polar Kraken, Vexing Sphinx and Phyrexian Soulgorger, all Full. Volcano Hellion's echo works, but its "amount of damage of your choice" waits on a resolution-time number prompt.

- **A creature that can't be regenerated this turn** (#1887, [ADR 0108](decisions/0108-turn-scoped-effects-object-history-and-damage-shields.md) §2, PR 1) — "<that creature> can't be regenerated this turn" (CR 701.19c) is a `ScopedEffect` rules-gate kind, `cantBeRegenerated` (`game.ModCantBeRegenerated`, reader `rule`), pinned to the permanent until cleanup by `Game.CantBeRegeneratedThisTurnForEffect` (CR 400.7, 514.2). There is one regeneration gate, `regenerationRefusedLocked` (`game/regeneration.go`), and it reads both the destroy instruction's own rider (`DestroyOptions.CantBeRegenerated`, Wrath of God) and the turn's mark. The shield built-in asks it in its AppliesTo, so a refused shield is not applied and not spent; a catalog static regeneration ("If this creature would be destroyed, regenerate it", CR 701.19b) is `effects.RegenerateIfThisWouldBeDestroyed`, which asks it through `Game.RegenerationAllowedForEffect`, and `TestEveryRegenerationAsksTheGate` reads every replacement in the catalog and the engine and fails one that regenerates without asking. The card side is `CantBeRegeneratedThisTurn` and the dealt-damage continuation `CantBeRegeneratedIfDealtDamage` ("A creature dealt damage this way", Incinerate, Flamebreak). Whippoorwill's "When the creature dies this turn, exile the creature" is an event-delayed trigger pinned to the object (`effects.ExileWhenItDiesThisTurn`, keys `dies/the-listed-object` and `dies/exile-from-graveyard`), so the creature dies, its dies triggers see it, and the card is then exiled from the graveyard if it is still there (CR 603.7c). The view shows a NO REGEN chip (`CardView.cant_be_regenerated`). **Cards** (all Full): Whippoorwill, Incinerate, Disintegrate, Carbonize, Scorching Lava, Engulfing Flames, Rage of Purphoros, Gravebind, Hurr Jackal, Furnace Brood, Flamebreak, Jaya Ballard, Task Mage, Orcish Healer, Lim-Dûl's Cohort. **Moved to other rows:** Bone Shaman and Runesword (#1946), Clergy of the Holy Nimbus and Knight of the Holy Nimbus (#1947).

- **Exile instead if it would die this turn** (#1886, [ADR 0108](decisions/0108-turn-scoped-effects-object-history-and-damage-shields.md) §1, PR 1) — "if it would die this turn, exile it instead" (CR 614.1a, 700.4) is a `ScopedEffect` replacement kind, `exileIfWouldDie` (`game.ModExileIfWouldDie`, reader `replacement`, reads nothing), on a move from the battlefield to a graveyard and nothing else: a bounce, an exile or a tuck is untouched, and a sacrifice or zero toughness is a death and is replaced. `Game.ExileIfItWouldDieThisTurnForEffect` pins it to one object until cleanup (CR 400.7, 514.2), so a flickered creature comes back free of it; `Game.ExileIfCreaturesWouldDieThisTurnForEffect` writes it over a live set (CR 611.2c) with the new `ScopeCreatures` ("If a creature would die this turn", Flaying Tendrils) or `ScopeOpponentsCreatures` (Malicious Eclipse), reading the dying permanent's type and controller as it would die. Two records on one dying creature are one modification, so no CR 616 question is asked. The card side (`effects/exile_if_dies.go`) follows the printed wording: `ExileIfItWouldDieThisTurn` for "if that creature would die" and "if it's a creature" (the spell's effect, registered whatever the damage did — the Disintegrate ruling), and `ExileIfDealtDamageWouldDie` for "a creature dealt damage this way", a continuation on the new per-recipient damage walk `game.DealDamageEachEachThenForEffect` that marks only a recipient dealt more than 0. The view shows an EXILED IF IT DIES chip on a marked permanent (`CardView.exiled_if_it_dies`) and a banner line for a scoped record (`GameView.exile_if_creatures_die`). A v7 corpus fixture holds every shape. **Cards** (all Full): Demonfire, Lava Coil, Disintegrate, Anger of the Gods, Flaying Tendrils, Malicious Malfunction, Malicious Eclipse, Bot Bashing Time, Fanged Flames, Feed the Flames, Flame-Blessed Bolt, Magma Spray, Obliterating Bolt, Puncturing Blow, Reduce to Ashes, Scorching Dragonfire, Scorchmark, Elspeth's Smite, Annihilating Fire, Incendiary Flow, Pillar of Flame, Touch of the Void, Yamabushi's Flame, Carbonize, Yamabushi's Storm, Bleed Dry, Ob Nixilis's Cruelty, Combustion Technique, Blitz of the Thunder-Raptor, Narset's Rebuke, Burn the Accursed, Smite the Deathless, Burn from Within, Betrayer's Bargain, Wilt in the Heat, Bouncer's Beatdown, Red Sun's Zenith, Scorching Lava, Necrotic Wound, Torch the Tower, Spikefield Hazard (the spell face), Agate Assault, Suplex, Pinecone Strike, Brutal Expulsion, Gnashing of Teeth, Crush the Weak, Underworld Fires, Gallifrey Falls // No More, Serpentine Spike, Nine-Ringed Bo, Unnatural Aggression, Mawloc, Cry of the Carnarium, The War Doctor. **Moved to other rows:** Fiery Annihilation (#1943), Chandra, Awakened Inferno (#1944), Faunsbane Troll (#1945), Runesword (#1946), Draconic Intervention (#1948).

- **Cards in graveyards can't be targeted** (#1885, [ADR 0109](decisions/0109-rule-gates-land-types-mana-and-cost-components.md) §6, PR 6) — the targeting check read only an object's own shroud, hexproof and protection, and returned "legal" at once for anything off the battlefield, so a rule about a whole ZONE had nowhere to live. `Spec.TargetingRestrictions` (`game.TargetingRestriction{Label, Zones, Forbids(TargetingQuery), ActiveWhen}`) is read live off the battlefield through `CatalogAbilityKey` and asked by `canBeTargetedByLocked` before its battlefield guard, so the two targeting choke points carry it: the legal-target walk the view and the legal-move enumerator read, and the announce (CR 601.2c) and resolution (CR 608.2b) checks. A spell aimed at a graveyard card when a restriction arrives loses that target. A cost, or a choice that is not a target, is never refused (CR 115.10a). `GameView.graveyard_target_bans` names each live restriction for the graveyard viewer's banner. **Cards:** Ground Seal, Silent Gravestone, Underworld Cerberus, Dennick, Pious Apprentice // Dennick, Pious Apparition, and Tomik, Distinguished Advokist, whose other sentence is the land-play gate of ADR 0109 PR 5 (all Full).

- **What a creature did during your last turn** (#1882, CR 502.3 / CR 508.1c / CR 508.4 / CR 400.7 / CR 500.7, [ADR 0108 §6](decisions/0108-turn-scoped-effects-object-history-and-damage-shields.md)) — Goblin Rock Sled's and Tangle Kelp's "doesn't untap … if it attacked during your last turn", Giant Turtle's "can't attack if it attacked during your last turn", Avenge's "costs {2} less if a player attacked you during their last turn" and O-Kagachi's "if that player attacked you during their last turn". `TurnTally.Attacks` is replaced as each turn begins, so the fact has to live on the player: **`Player.LastTurnAttacks`** is the attack records (attacker, object epoch, defender) of the last turn that player took. `recordLastTurnAttacksLocked` copies the ending turn's tally onto its active player at the top of `beginNextTurnLocked`, before the turn and the tally are replaced, so "your last turn" is the most recent turn **you** took: an extra turn replaces the record (CR 500.7), a seat that never begins a turn (CR 800.4k) leaves its record alone, and an empty record is written too. Only declarations are recorded (CR 508.4) and each is keyed by object (CR 400.7). **`Game.AttackedDuringControllersLastTurn(card)`** reads the card's current controller's record; **`AttackedYouDuringTheirLastTurn(player, you)`** and **`AnyPlayerAttackedYouDuringTheirLastTurn(you)`** read a player's, where an attack on a planeswalker or battle you control is not an attack on you. The card side is `doesntUntapIfItAttackedDuringYourLastTurn` (an `UntapStepRestriction`, self or attached), `CantAttackIfItAttackedDuringYourLastTurn` (a layer-6 `CantAttack` static whose condition changes only as a turn begins, where the layer cache is already invalidated), `APlayerAttackedYouDuringTheirLastTurn` (a cost predicate) and `TargetNonlandPermanentOfThePlayerHit` (a `TargetsFrom` clause read off the trigger's event; O-Kagachi re-checks its intervening "if" as the ability resolves, CR 603.4). One additive snapshot field, `lastTurnAttacks`, recorded in the shape file and classified `carried`. Goblin Rock Sled, Giant Turtle, Tangle Kelp, Avenge and O-Kagachi ship `full`. **Still open and a different seam**: Weathered Sentinels' "can attack players who attacked you during their last turn as though it didn't have defender", a per-defending-player waiver of defender, now on its own row.

- **A land that becomes a basic land type for a while** (#1881, [ADR 0109](decisions/0109-rule-gates-land-types-mana-and-cost-components.md) §1, PR 1) — CR 305.7 from a resolved effect is a `ScopedEffect` kind, `setBasicLandTypes` (`game.ModSetBasicLandTypes`, layer 4, a CR 613.1f removal, reads `Mod.Subtypes`, one or more basic land types; registration and restore refuse anything else). The land's land types are replaced and its other subtypes stay (CR 205.1a, `Characteristic.SetLandSubtypes`, over `game.LandTypes`, CR 205.3i's seventeen), it loses the abilities its rules text gives it and keeps every layer-6 grant, and it taps for the new colour through the intrinsic CR 305.6 path. "In addition to its other types" stays `addSubtypes` (CR 205.1b). The static form `effects.SetsBasicLandType` now replaces land types only, so Magus of the Moon leaves Dryad Arbor its Dryad. Card side (`effects/land_types.go`): `LandBecomes`, `ChooseBasicLandTypeThen` (an option pick at resolution over the five basic land types, a narrower list, or all seventeen), the ability bodies `TargetLandBecomesUntilEOT`, `…WhileSourceRemains`, `…Indefinitely`, `…UntilItsControllersNextTurn` and `TargetLandYouControlGainsChosenTypeUntilEOT`, and the Aura form `ChooseBasicLandTypeAsEnters` / `EnchantedLandIsTheChosenType`. A group's set is fixed as it begins (CR 611.2c). The view stamps `CardView.land_type_effects` and the client draws a land-type badge ("Island until end of turn — Tidal Warrior"). A v7 corpus fixture holds the kind in four durations. **Cards** (all Full): Deepwood Elder, Dream Thrush, Dreamwinder, Floodchaser, Grixis Illusionist, Jinx, Kavu Recluse, Kukemssa Serpent, Moonbow Illusionist, Mystic Compass, Pixie Illusionist, Reef Shaman, Sea Snidd, Shimmering Mirage, Slimy Kavu, Streambed Aquitects, Tidal Warrior, Tideshaper Mystic, Tundra Kavu, Unstable Frontier, Zombie Trailblazer, Elsewhere Flask, Terraformer, Nightcreep, Vision Charm, Navigator's Compass, Energybending, Gaea's Liege, Graceful Antelope, Tide Shaper, Orcish Farmer, Cyclopean Giant, Thelonite Monk; and the static land-type cards (owner decision 4) Spreading Seas, Sea's Claim, Evil Presence, Tainted Well, Lingering Mirage, Contaminated Ground, Lush Growth, Convincing Mirage, Phantasmal Terrain, Blood Moon, Harbinger of the Seas, Illusionary Terrain.

- **A creature that can't attack unless the defending player controls something** (#1879, CR 508.1c / CR 508.5 / CR 508.5a / CR 509.1b, [ADR 0107 §2](decisions/0107-state-triggers-rebound-disturb-and-damage-prevention.md)) — Sea Serpent's "can't attack unless defending player controls an Island", and every other condition on the defending player the Commander-legal cards print, as plain-data clauses on ADR 0106 §2's per-target restriction. `AttackTargetRestriction` carries `DefenderMustControl []game.PermanentQuery` (an any-of list of closed permanent filters, `game/permanent_query.go`: card types, subtypes, supertypes, colours, a keyword, "enchanted"), `DefenderMustBePoisoned` (CR 122.1f), `DefenderMustBeMonarch` (CR 725.1), `DefenderGraveyardAtLeast` and `ControllerMustControlMore` ("you control more creatures than defending player", the "you" being the attacking creature's controller). Every clause set must hold. Each is written by an ordinary layer-6 self static (`effects.CantAttackUnlessDefendingPlayerControls`, `…IsPoisoned`, `…IsTheMonarch`, `…HasCardsInGraveyard`, `CantAttackUnlessYouControlMoreThanDefendingPlayer`) and goes with the creature's abilities (CR 613.1f). `canAttackTargetWithLocked` works out each target's defending player with `defendingPlayerForAttackLocked` (CR 508.5: the player, a planeswalker's controller, a battle's protector) and refuses the target when that player fails a clause, read live at declaration (an Island that leaves later changes nothing, CR 506.4a). So in Commander the creature may attack each opponent who meets the condition, their planeswalkers and the battles they protect, and no one else, worked out per creature (CR 508.5a). Both verbs refuse with the reason ("Sea Serpent can't attack Bob: Bob controls no Island.", "…: Bob isn't poisoned."), the CR 508.1d search counts only the targets left, and the enumerator's per-attacker list and the ADR 0105 digest's `attack_targets` follow. The card view's `attack_target_restrictions` gains one row per opponent who fails, with the reason in full (`reason`), and the chip's tooltip says it. A resolved effect grants the Island form with a new `ScopedEffect` mod kind, `cantAttackUnlessDefenderControls` (reads `Mod.Queries`): Veiled Serpent becomes "a 4/4 Serpent creature with 'This creature can't attack unless defending player controls an Island.'" A restriction a creature imposes on itself is its own ability, so a later layer-6 "loses all abilities" drops it and an earlier one does not (CR 613.1f, 613.7). The "can't block unless" halves are CR 509.1b block rules, `effects.CantBlockUnless` with `YouControlMoreThanTheAttackingPlayer` (the attacking player is the active player, CR 506.2) or `YouHaveCardsInHandAtLeast`. Every field is additive in the snapshot under schema v7; no closure routes. **Cards:** Armored Galleon, Chained Throatseeker, Crown-Hunter Hireling, Deep-Sea Serpent, Ethereal Whiskergill, Goblin Goon, Godhunter Octopus, Hammerhead Shark, Lurking Green Dragon, Mogg Toady, Monstrous Hound, Red Cliffs Armada, Sea Monster, Sealock Monster, Serpent of the Endless Sea, Slipstream Eel, Steam Frigate, Vantress Gargoyle, Veiled Serpent, Vodalian Serpent, Whimwader, Wu Warship and Zhou Yu, Chief Commander, all Full; the 13 serpents that also sacrifice themselves "when you control no Islands" landed with state triggers (#1858). Dreamwinder, Floodchaser and Kukemssa Serpent wait on a land becoming a basic land type until end of turn (#1881), and Goblin Rock Sled on what a creature did during your last turn (#1882).

- **The card discarded to pay, and the world rule** (#1862, [ADR 0109](decisions/0109-rule-gates-land-types-mana-and-cost-components.md) §8, PR 7) — the activated-ability payer now records every card a discard component moved on `PaidCost.Discarded` (cycling's own card first), as the spell path does, and an effect reads it with `Context.DiscardedCard()` / `DiscardedManaValue()` (CR 400.7j). `copiedPaidCost` carries `Discarded`, `Exiled`, `Sacrificed` and `ReturnedAttacking`, so a CR 707.10 copy of an ability uses the objects the original paid with. The world rule (CR 704.5k) is a state-based action read off `Card.EntryOrdinal`, a game counter taken once per move onto the battlefield and shared by every card of one simultaneous entry: of two or more world permanents, all but the most recent entrant go to their owners' graveyards, and all of them on a tie. `EntryOrdinal` is an additive snapshot field; an older file orders its world permanents by entry stamp. **Cards:** Land's Edge, Hisoka, Minamo Sensei, Mercurial Chemister, Moria Scavenger, Necromancer's Stockpile, Slumbering Tora, Volrath the Fallen, and the world enchantments Living Plane, Gravity Sphere, Mystic Decree, Serra Aviary, Chaosphere, Nether Void, In the Eye of Chaos, Bazaar of Wonders, Eye of Singularity, Storm World, Pillar Tombs of Aku and Teferi's Realm (Full); Concordant Crossroads, Caverns of Despair and Forsaken Wastes lose their world-rule caveat. **Moved to other rows:** ten older world enchantments, each one shape short (#1997, `world-enchantment-leftovers`).

- **State triggers about each of several objects** (#1858, CR 603.8 / 400.7, [ADR 0107 §1](decisions/0107-state-triggers-rebound-disturb-and-damage-prevention.md)) — Bomb Squad's "Whenever a creature has four or more fuse counters on it, remove all fuse counters from it and destroy it". The state is a fact about one creature, so the ability triggers once for each creature in it. A row whose `State` key names a `game.StateEachCondition` (`RegisterStateEachCondition`, in the same package-level registry and key namespace as `RegisterStateCondition`) is asked once per battlefield permanent. Each instance it queues names that permanent as its trigger context's `Object` (ID and epoch) with no event, so `TriggerContext.Fired` stays false and the effect reads the creature through `ctx.TriggeringPermanent()`, by last-known information once it has gone (CR 608.2h). The CR 603.8 latch reads the same `Object`, on the trigger queue, the stack, the resolving slot and every open announcement prompt: an instance about one creature does not latch the ability for another, a creature that left and came back is a new object (CR 400.7), and two Bomb Squads each trigger for one creature, as the card's ruling says. The latch stays derived: the object is already snapshot data on every queued and stacked item, a new v7 corpus board (`state_each_trigger_on_stack`) holds one, and no closure route, ceiling or shape key changed. Helpers: `effects.WhenStateEach` and `WheneverACreatureHasAtLeast`. **Cards** (1, Full): Bomb Squad. The seam has no cards left waiting.

- **Targets bounded by X or by the counters removed** (#1842, [ADR 0109](decisions/0109-rule-gates-land-types-mana-and-cost-components.md) §9, PR 7) — the announced-X target bound is now one bound with a statistic (`ManaValueAtMostX`, `ManaValueEqualsX`, `PowerAtMostX`, `ToughnessAtMostX`) and an input: the announced X (a mana {X}, a variable sacrifice or tap), or, with `BoundByCountersRemoved`, the counters the activation's cost removed. `game.AnnouncedBound` binds the clause at announce, after the payment is validated and before the targets (CR 602.2b, 601.2c), and again from the stack item for the CR 608.2b re-check; nothing new is stored. `effects.Register` refuses a bound with nothing to bind it to. The view ships each candidate's power, toughness or mana value with the bound's flags, the client narrows by the X or the counter payment, and the enumerator filters every (payment, targets) pair through `TargetsWithinBoundForEffect`. It also stamps `mana_value_equals_x` now, so the client no longer offers Lazav a superset. **Cards:** Simic Manipulator, Quillmane Baku, Killing Glare, Minamo Sightbender, Aryel, Knight of Windgrace, Finale of Eternity and Entrancing Lyre (Full); Ruthless Technomancer gains its reanimation ability. **Moved to other rows:** Gang Up (assist, #1857).

- **Exile instead of your graveyard this turn** (#1823, [ADR 0108](decisions/0108-turn-scoped-effects-object-history-and-damage-shields.md) §4, PR 3) — "if a card would be put into your graveyard from anywhere this turn, exile that card instead" (CR 614.1a) is a `ScopedEffect` kind, `exileInsteadOfYourGraveyard` (`game.ModExileInsteadOfYourGraveyard`, a replacement-reader kind, scope game, reading `Player`), written by `Game.ExileInsteadOfYourGraveyardThisTurnForEffect` until end of turn. It applies to every zone move and every discard into that player's graveyard from any zone (a discard is its own replacement window, so the kind watches both), costs and effects alike, and the spell that made it, which goes to the graveyard after its effect starts. A token is not a card and is left alone. The graveyard a card goes to is its owner's, so a creature you stole and lost still dies into its owner's graveyard. The affected object's controller orders it against other replacements (CR 616.1). `effects.GraveyardPlayThisTurn` (`YawgmothsWillThisTurn()`) is the paired card-side helper: it writes the replacement and a stored `ScopeStanding` cast permission over the controller's graveyard together, so the "play lands and cast spells from your graveyard" half can never ship without the replacement that keeps it from being stronger than printed; `SpellsOnly` is Forgotten Cellar's spells-without-lands variant. Shipped on Yawgmoth's Will, Gaea's Will (suspend 4—{G}), Magus of the Will and Walk-In Closet // Forgotten Cellar (the left door is Glacierwood Siege's land permission over the graveyard, the right door's unlock trigger runs the helper).

- **The total mana spent, read as the permanent enters** (#1735, CR 601.2h, [ADR 0068 amendment 2026-10-02](decisions/0068-the-mana-spent-on-a-spell.md)) — closes the "total spent at entry" item of the "Mana that does something when it's spent" row. The record was never missing: `StackItem.Paid.Mana` already held every mana the payment spent (generic, coloured, X, kicker and other additional mana, the commander tax and other increases, after reductions; not Phyrexian life, convoke, waterbend or delve, and nothing for a free cast), and `ManaSpent.Total()` counts it. What was missing was the read inside the CR 614 window. `game.CastCounts.ManaSpent` is that total, set by `castCountsFor` from the one record, so a waived payment (strict mana off) reads 0 and a CR 707.10 copy reads a real 0. `Game.EntryCastCountsForEffect(ev)` hands the whole `CastCounts` to a hook that runs inside the window and is given the event: it reads the resolving item the event already carries across a paused entry, and it answers the zero value for an entry that was not a cast (reanimation, flicker, token). The counter-clause seeder and the accessor share `castCountsLocked`. `CopySelector.Candidates` keeps its signature; `effects.EntersAsCopyOfFromCast` hands the counts to a candidate filter. No wire, snapshot or bot change: the figure is read from the payment record, which already survived a clone and a snapshot round trip. **Cards:** Mockingbird copies a creature with mana value up to the amount spent, as a flying Bird, and keeps only the strict-mana caveat every mana-spent reader carries. Mana Sculpt's caveat claimed the engine kept no record of the mana spent; it now refunds that amount with a Wizard. **Now one `EntryCountersFromCast` clause away, not catalogued:** Verazol, the Split Current, Kurbis, Harvest Celebrant, Gyrus, Waker of Corpses, Marath, Will of the Wild, Dyadrine, Synthesis Amalgam. **Still open on the row:** granted readers (#1552), where this accessor is the read half of Lux Artillery's granted sunburst.

- **Exiling a card until something happens** (#1729, CR 610.3, CR 725.4, CR 800.4a) — the "until" return is CR 610.3's second one-shot effect, created immediately after the event, and no longer a triggered ability on the stack. `game.UntilReturn` is the record, kept in `Game.DelayedTriggers` with `Until` set so it clones, snapshots and shows on the wire with everything else still owed; `effects.ExileUntil` is the one card-side primitive. An event-keyed "until" (Palace Jailer's "until an opponent becomes the monarch") is made due by the `untilReturns` listener; "until this leaves the battlefield" (Ossification, Hostage Taker, Sheltered by Ghosts) is due once its object is no longer on the battlefield, a structural test, so a source that leaves the game with its owner counts though that emits no zone-change event (ADR 0060). `resolveUntilReturnsLocked` performs every due return together (CR 610.3d) before the state-based actions and before any player receives priority ("Nothing happens between the two events, including state-based actions" — the Hostage Taker ruling). Because it is not a trigger, the record does not leave the game with its controller (CR 800.4a/d), so Palace Jailer's creature comes back when its controller leaves wearing the crown and CR 725.4 hands it to an opponent, and Hostage Taker's card comes back when its owner leaves. CR 610.3a/b is `UntilEventHappenedForEffect`, asked before the exile, so an Ossification removed in response no longer exiles its target for good, and an opponent crowned in response to Palace Jailer's trigger means nothing is exiled. Snapshot: three additive `delayedTriggers[]` keys (`until`, `untilLeaves`, `due`) and a new body key, `until/return-to-battlefield`, which an older binary refuses. The old leave triggers stay as legacy rows that fire only for a card exiled before this change. **Cards:** Palace Jailer drops its caveat and is Full.

- **Casting one spell from among several cards** (#1729, CR 611.2c) — `game.CastPermission.CastsLeft` is how many spells a permission may still open, zero being no limit. A cast the permission is the reason for spends one (`consumeLimitedGrantLocked`, `castUsesGrantLocked`), and the permission ends at zero; a cast the card's own text allows, such as a printed flashback, spends nothing, which is Locke's ruling. A card can now hold two stored permissions and the caster chooses: `CastPermissionForClaimLocked` picks the permission whose `AltCostKey` the cast claims, and `CastOffersForLocked` lists the second permission's offer, so the view and the bot enumerator offer it too. Snapshot: one additive `castPermissions[]` key, `castsLeft`. **Cards:** Court of Locthwain (Full) — the card it exiles is playable while exiled with mana of any type, and on a monarch turn one spell from among the cards exiled with it is free, claimed as an alternative cost so a paid cast leaves the free one for another card; Locke, Treasure Hunter (Caveats: a milled card a replacement exiled instead is not covered).

- **A land that loses all its land types** (#1604, [ADR 0109](decisions/0109-rule-gates-land-types-mana-and-cost-components.md) §2, PR 3) — "loses all land types" is a layer-4 type change (CR 205.3i, 613.1d). The new `ScopedEffect` kind `loseLandTypes` (`game.LoseLandTypesMod`, reads nothing) and its static form `effects.LosesAllLandTypes` both call `Characteristic.LoseLandTypes`, which removes every one of CR 205.3i's seventeen land types and keeps every other subtype (CR 205.1a), so a Dryad Arbor is still a Dryad. It is not CR 305.7: it removes no ability by itself. The basic land types' intrinsic mana abilities go with the types (CR 305.6). Every printed use goes on to say "and abilities and has …", which is the same effect's layer-6 half: `game.LoseAllAbilitiesMod` and a grant in the same record, or the static `effects.LosesAllAbilitiesAndHas`, with the removal applied before the grant. The land-type chip lists a `loseLandTypes` record with what that effect took and gave (`No land types, no abilities, "{T}: Add {C}." for as long as it has a blight counter on it — Ultima, Origin of Oblivion`). Alpine Moon's chosen name became the first chosen name a static ability reads, so choosing one now bumps the layer version, and it is read by `game.PermanentHasName` (the name of the face that is up, CR 712.8f) because "a nonbasic land card name" is judged by the named face (CR 201.4a, 201.4d). A v7 corpus fixture holds the new kind. **Cards**: Ultima, Origin of Oblivion (one record timed by `WhilePinnedHasCounter{blight}`, and its "add an additional {C}" triggered mana ability), Lithoform Blight and Alpine Moon.

- **Granted loyalty abilities** (#1604, CR 606.3 / 606.6 / 114.2 / 707.10, [ADR 0109](decisions/0109-rule-gates-land-types-mana-and-cost-components.md) §2, owner decision 2, PR 4) — ADR 0093 Decision 10's loyalty exclusion is lifted. A grant bundle's row with a `LoyaltyCost` is an ordinary granted activated row on the planeswalker it lands on, so CR 606.3's "no player has previously activated a loyalty ability of that permanent that turn" and CR 606.6's counter check are the walker's: the granted row and the walker's own rows share one activation a turn, in either order, and a granted −N needs N loyalty counters. `ActivateCatalogAbility` stamps the row's grantor on the stack item (`StackItem.GrantedBy`, read by `effects.Context.Grantor`), and `effects.CreateEmblem` files the emblem under the grantor's catalog key, because "you get an emblem with …" in a granted ability is the grantor's text (CR 114.2). The grantor is carried by a snapshot (`grantedBy`, omitted when there is none, so an older binary refuses only a file holding a granted ability on the stack) and by a CR 707.10 copy, so a copied −12 is still the Talent's emblem. A restore point names the ability by its `grant:` ref, as every granted activated ability. `EventActivateAbility` now carries a `Loyalty` bit, read by `effects.WheneverYouActivateALoyaltyAbilityOfEnchanted` ("whenever you activate a loyalty ability of enchanted planeswalker"). **Cards**, all Full: Teferi's Talent (its −12 makes the Talent's own emblem, Teferi, Temporal Archmage's text under the Talent's name), Elspeth's Talent, Liliana's Talent, Rowan's Talent (Rings of Brighthearth's copy, without the {2}) and Vivien's Talent.

- **Discarding your hand as a cost** (#1600, CR 118.3 / CR 602.2b / CR 602.5e / CR 605.3a, [ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md)'s 2026-10-02 amendment) — `game.DiscardCost` demanded a fixed count of at least one, so "Discard your hand" had no shape. It is now the component's `Hand` form, built by `effects.DiscardYourHand()` and owned by both `AbilityCost.DiscardCards` and `ManaAbilityCost.DiscardCards`: `validateDiscardCostLocked` reads the activator's whole hand at payment, refuses `discard_ids` for it, and pays an empty hand as nothing (CR 118.3). The cards leave through the one discard door with cause cost, so discard triggers and madness see them, Library of Leng (effects only) does not, and on a mana ability the triggers go on the stack after the mana is in the pool. No options are stamped, so the client opens no picker. `effects.Register` refuses a hand clause with a count, a predicate or "at random", and beside any other component that spends a hand card. "Activate only as an instant" on a mana ability is `effects.OnlyAsAnInstant()`, a Condition over `Game.InstantWindowOpenForEffect` (holds priority, owes no prompt, no prompt stopping the table, mulligans shut) — the legal enumerator's own reading of "may act now", so a mana ability that may otherwise be activated mid-cast or mid-resolution (CR 605.3a) cannot be; the view greys it through `condition_unmet`, and the auto-tapper, which runs mid-cast, refuses every discard component. `legal.MoveCost.Hand` tells a policy how many cards the move throws away, and the heuristic prices it at `Weights.Hand` per card. **Cards:** Lion's Eye Diamond, Diamond Lion, Null Brooch and Slate of Ancestry (Full). **Not covered:** "As an additional cost to cast this spell, … discard your hand" (Kaervek's Spite, which also sacrifices every permanent you control); Bomat Courier, Kyren Archive, Connecting the Dots, Reverberating Summons, Subira, Tulzidi Caravanner, Tarrian's Journal and Flamewar print the cost beside other mechanics and are not catalogued.

- **Spending mana as though it were any color** (#1600, CR 609.4b, [ADR 0066](decisions/0066-granted-cast-and-play-permissions.md)'s 2026-10-02 amendment) — the one-spell form (a cast permission's `AnyColor` / `AnyType`, folded in the cast pricer, #1573) now has a player form: `Spec.AnyColorSpend`, a `game.AnyColorSpendStatic` for "you" or "players", optionally narrowed by `Covers` over the payment's spend context, read live off the battlefield. `costAsPaidByLocked` (`game/spend_any_color.go`) reads it at every PAYMENT — a cast and its auto-tap, a CR 602 activation / special action / attack tax (`payAbilityManaCostLocked`), a mana ability's own mana cost, a pay-unless (`payCostLocked`) — and `internal/legal`'s one affordability probe and the auto-tap preview read it through the same function, so the view's `legal_actions`, the bot's moves and the payment agree. No pricer is touched, so the price shown stays printed. The cost is WIDENED rather than folded: coloured requirements become `ColorRequirement.AnyMana` with their printed options kept, `{C}` is left alone (CR 106.1b), and both solvers pay a widened symbol with its printed colour when they can and as generic otherwise, after convoke / waterbend / delve and before the Phyrexian strike. Mana restrictions still bind. **Cards:** Chromatic Orrery and Oath of Nissa (Full); Mycosynth Lattice (its spend line; caveat left for "everything is colorless"). **Not covered:** "spend WHITE mana as though it were any color" with "other mana only as colorless" (Celestial Dawn), and grants over one permanent's own abilities (Manascape Refractor); no catalogued card waits on either.

- **Granted mana-spent readers, and sunburst as a keyword** (#1552, [ADR 0109](decisions/0109-rule-gates-land-types-mana-and-cost-components.md) §11, PR 10) — closes the "Mana that does something when it's spent" row. Four pieces, all reading the one payment record (`StackItem.Paid.Mana`, CR 400.7d). `Game.EntrySpentForEffect(ev)` hands the entering spell's whole spend, as a `game.ManaSpent`, to a replacement on ANY permanent inside the CR 614 entry window: the known zero for an entry that was not a cast, unknown for a waived payment. Sunburst (CR 702.44) joins `canonicalKeywords` as a cumulative keyword, and `applySunburstLocked` counts its instances on the permanent as it would enter, from ADR 0109 PR 9's CR 614.12 entry look-ahead (owner decision 1: printed, deck-imported, or given to the spell, and none under an ability-removing effect such as Dress Down), seeding a +1/+1 counter per colour on an object entering as a printed creature and a charge counter otherwise; `effects.SunburstCounters` is gone and Etched Oracle moved to the keyword. `GrantKeywordsToSpellForEffect` takes a `Duration`, and the re-pin to the permanent (`repinSpellControlLocked`, `adoptSpellControlLocked`) keeps it, so "it gains haste until end of turn" on a spell ends at cleanup on the creature (CR 400.7a, 611.2a). Spend riders gained `spell_gains` (`effects.SpentSpellGains`: a keyword grant to the spell made at the spend, one per production; an effect's mana can carry riders too, `AddManaOptions.Riders`) and a counted enters-with-counters rider (`ManaSpendRider.Count`, `RegisterManaRiderCount`, read as the creature enters). Restore refuses a rider kind or count key the binary cannot read, wherever a token is carried (`snapshot_mana_riders.go`). Satoru's condition reads the whole event batch as a CR 603.4 intervening if. **Cards:** Lux Artillery, Coin of Mastery, Satoru, the Infiltrator and Opal Palace keep only the strict-mana caveat; new are Kalain, Reclusive Painter, Generator Servant, Carnelian Orb of Dragonkind, Tyvar Kell (Full), Freestrider Commando, Solar Array, Domri, Chaos Bringer (its +1's mana gives a creature spell riot) and the printed sunburst cards Suntouched Myr, Skyreach Manta, Pentad Prism, Clearwater Goblet, Opaline Bracers, Sawtooth Thresher, Lunar Avenger, Heliophial, Engineered Explosives, Suncrusher, Solarion, Baton of Courage, Infused Arrows, Spinal Parasite and Arcbound Wanderer (modular's dies trigger with its last-known counters). **Still waiting:** Primeval Spawn on free casts with a total mana value budget (#2017).

- **Players can't gain life** (#1880, [ADR 0107](decisions/0107-state-triggers-rebound-disturb-and-damage-prevention.md) §5 decision 6, owner decision 5, PR 6) — CR 119.7 is a rule-modifying effect (CR 613.11) read at one gate, `playerCantGainLifeLocked`, which every life gain asks BEFORE the CR 614 life window opens: a forbidden gain does not happen, no "if you would gain life" replacement sees it (CR 119.10), and no `EventChangeLife` fires for a "whenever you gain life" trigger. It has three sources: **battlefield statics** (`Spec.CantGainLife`, a `game.CantGainLifeStatic` for "players", "your opponents" or "enchanted player", read live and keyed by `CatalogAbilityKey`); **turn grants** (`ModCantGainLife`, every player or the record controller's opponents, ended at cleanup, CR 514.2); and **the rest of the game** (the same kind naming one player, with an indefinite duration, CR 611.2a). Lifelink damage is still dealt; only its gain is stopped (CR 702.15b). An exchange of life totals that would raise a forbidden total does not happen at all, nor one involving a locked total (CR 119.7, 119.8). "Gains no life instead" (Sulfuric Vortex, Flames of the Blood Hand) is a replacement, not this gate: a catalog replacement, or the new `ModGainNoLife` record. The player view carries `cant_gain_life`, shown on the seat as a NO GAIN badge. **Cards:** Skullcrack, Call In a Professional, Sunspine Lynx, Archfiend of Despair, Atarka's Command, Erebos, God of the Dead, Giant Cindermaw, Grievous Wound, Gríma Wormtongue, Havoc Festival, Knight of Dusk's Shadow, Quakebringer, Rampaging Ferocidon, Stigma Lasher, The Lord of Pain, Tibalt, Rakish Instigator and Sulfuric Vortex (Full); Leyline of Punishment (Caveats: no opening-hand start), Forsaken Wastes (Caveats: no world rule), Roiling Vortex (Caveats: needs strict mana for its no-mana trigger) and Screaming Nemesis (its can't-gain-life caveat dropped; its simultaneous-damage caveat stays). **Moved to other rows:** Witch Hunt (random targets, #1815), Everlasting Torment (#1889).

- **Several cards returned from exile enter together** (#1872, CR 603.6a / CR 101.4 / CR 111.8) — the exile half of #1867. The catalog's multi-card exile returns looped over the single-card exile door, so the first card was announced before the second moved: of two returned Soul Wardens only the first saw the other enter. `effects.ReturnFromExileTogether{Targets, Controller, Tapped, Then}` is `ReturnFromGraveyardTogether` for exile, over one shared body: it skips a card no longer in exile (CR 603.7c), a token (CR 111.8) or a nonpermanent, and tells `Then` the NEW object IDs (CR 400.7). The batch door now defaults "under its owner's control" to the CARD's owner, because exile is a shared zone owned by nobody and an exiled card's CR 614 window used to see no controller at all. The single-card exile door leaves a token in exile, so an immediate blink of a token returns nothing. **Converted:** Living Death (the last step is one entry for every player), the delayed blink's return (Waterbender's Restoration, The Eternal Wanderer, Charming Prince, Palace Jailer), the "until this leaves the battlefield" return (Hostage Taker, Ossification, Sheltered by Ghosts) and Oblivion Sower, which now also lets its controller choose any number of the lands as printed and refuses a face-down card (CR 406.3a), and goes `full`. Teval, the Balanced Scale and Blossoming Tortoise return their land with "tapped" on the entry event, closing the "enters untapped and is tapped a moment later" caveat. No snapshot field, effect key or closure route was added. Cosmic Intervention is deliberately unchanged: its ruling returns each card with its own delayed trigger, one at a time.

- **Several cards returned from a graveyard enter together** (#1867, CR 603.6a / CR 614.12 / CR 101.4) — every catalog card that put more than one card from a graveyard onto the battlefield did it with a loop over the single-card reanimation door, so the first card was announced before the second moved: of two returned Soul Wardens only the first saw the other enter. `Game.PutOntoBattlefieldTogetherThenForEffect` (#1322, #1324) already ran one simultaneous entry, but refused `ZoneGraveyard`; it takes graveyard cards now, each keeping its instance ID as the single-card door does. `effects.ReturnFromGraveyardTogether{Targets, Controller, Tapped, Then}` is the catalog's door over it: it skips a card no longer in a graveyard, a token or a nonpermanent instead of refusing the batch, carries the effect's own "tapped" on each entry event, and runs the rest of the effect in `Then` once the last entry question is answered. No snapshot field and no closure route was added. **22 cards routed through it:** Reveillark, Pull, Lich-Knights' Conquest, Smile at Death, Victimize, Eerie Ultimatum, Agadeem's Awakening, Behold the Sinister Six!, Mantle of the Ancients, Exhume (whose players now choose one at a time in APNAP order, CR 101.4b, before anything enters), Replenish, Brilliant Restoration, Open the Vaults, Raise the Past, Funeral Room // Awakening Hall, Rise of the Dark Realms, Splendid Reclamation, Will of the Sultai, Aftermath Analyst, World Shaper, Lumra, Bellow of the Woods and Zombie Apocalypse. Pull goes `full`, and Reveillark is `full` honestly. The batch's tapped clause also closed the "enters untapped and is tapped a moment later" caveat on Aftermath Analyst, World Shaper, Lumra and Zombie Apocalypse, which go `full`. **Still open:** loops that return several cards from EXILE one at a time (Living Death, the multi-card flickers), which the same door already accepts.

- **The next damage from a source** (#1860, [ADR 0107](decisions/0107-state-triggers-rebound-disturb-and-damage-prevention.md) §6, owner decisions 4 and 6, PR 7) — CR 615.8's one-use shield is `ModPreventNextFromSource`, a scoped prevention kind of plain data, so a table holding one is a restore point. It pins its source as an object (CR 400.7) — a chosen permanent spell covers the permanent it becomes (CR 609.7a), and a chosen permanent that has left covers its damage as it last existed — protects a player (optionally with that player's creatures), a pinned permanent or anything, rechecks the card's property against the source's last-known information as the damage would be dealt (CR 615.9; a failed recheck neither prevents nor uses the shield up, CR 609.7b), and names a follow-up body. It prevents the next INSTANCE of damage: one source dealing damage to several things at once is several engine events, so the shield stays live for the rest of the event batch it was spent in. **"The damage prevented this way"** (CR 615.5) is the instance's total: each event the shield prevents adds to one owed follow-up (`Game.preventionFollowUps`, plain data, in the snapshot), which runs once, with the total, the source's controller and its colours, when the instance is over — at the next priority boundary, before the state-based actions, or as play moves on to a new batch — so a trampler's split damage under Awe Strike is one life gain of 5. Under damage that can't be prevented (CR 615.12) the shield is kept and the follow-up runs once with zero. **`choose_source`** is a new card-set pick offering everything CR 609.7a allows — permanents, spells, face-up command-zone cards, and the objects stack items, waiting replacement and prevention records and waiting delayed triggers refer to, in public zones; the client captions each candidate with its controller and zone, the heuristic picks an opponent's spell on the stack and then the opponent's source with the most power, and the move label names each source's controller for the model tiers. The stale notes are fixed: the charged shield is CR 615.7's, and `effects/prevention.go` no longer says nobody prints the Circle form. **Cards** (41, all Full): the seven Circles of Protection, the seven Runes of Protection, Circle of Solace, Story Circle, Prismatic Circle, Greater Realm of Preservation, Haazda Shield Mate, Invulnerability, Kithkin Armor, Martyr's Cause, Mercenaries, Pentagram of the Ages, Pilgrim of Justice, Pilgrim of Virtue, Righteous Aura, Samite Blessing, Sanctum Guardian, Charm Peddler, Circle of Despair and Dazzling Reflection, and with the follow-up Awe Strike, Bone Mask, Cho-Arrim Alchemist, Deflecting Palm, Honorable Passage, Intervention Pact, New Way Forward, Reverse Damage and Shadowbane. **Moved to other rows:** Penance and Seasoned Tactician (#1902, their costs), Rhystic Circle (#1903), Desperate Gambit (#1890), Immortal Coil and Nine Lives (#1906, a static's follow-up). The not-one-use and redirecting shields against a chosen source are #1904 and #1905. **2026-10-02:** [ADR 0108](decisions/0108-turn-scoped-effects-object-history-and-damage-shields.md) PR 0 replaced the event batch with the damage instance (one damage instruction, or one combat damage step), so two instructions in one resolution are two instances and the shield prevents only the first, and the follow-up runs as its instance ends.

- **State triggers** (#1858, CR 603.8, [ADR 0107 §1](decisions/0107-state-triggers-rebound-disturb-and-damage-prevention.md)) — "When you control no Islands, sacrifice this creature", "When there are five or more plot counters on this enchantment". `TriggeredAbility.State` names a condition over the board, the source and its controller, filed in a package-level registry (`RegisterStateCondition`) that no `Game` reaches, so the row holds a key and no func; a row that declares one watches no event, and Register refuses it with an unregistered key, `Watches`, a `Build`, no `Effect` or another zone, so its stack item is always keyed (`catalog/triggered`) and a table with one waiting is a restore point. Per owner decision 1 the engine asks every battlefield permanent's state triggers after every event it emits and in each pass of the CR 704.3 loop, so a state that lasts a moment inside a resolution still triggers, as CR 603.8's own example says; combat damage (CR 510.2) and a state-based-action sweep, which the rules see as one event and the engine emits in pieces, hold the per-event check until they finish. The CR 603.8 latch is derived, never stored: the ability is latched while the trigger queue, an open announcement prompt, the stack or the still-open resolving slot holds an item of the same row from the same object (CR 400.7), and a copy of the ability does not latch it. The registry records which catalog keys carry a state trigger, so the walk is a map lookup per permanent and the layer cache is caught up only when a candidate is on the battlefield. Helpers: `WhenYouControlNo`, `WhenYouControlNoOther`, `WhenThereAreNo` and `WhenYouControlAtLeast` over ADR 0107's shared `game.PermanentQuery`, `WhenThisHasAtLeast` and `WhenThisHasNo` for counters, and `WhenState`. No closure route and no snapshot field: the key lives only on catalog rows, which the running binary rebuilds on restore. **Cards** (43, all Full): Afiya Grove, Barbarian Outcast, Covetous Dragon, Dark Depths, Darksteel Reactor, Deadly Designs, Drop of Honey, Emperor Crocodile, Endangered Armodon, Endrek Sahr, Master Breeder, Force Bubble, Garruk Relentless, Homarid, Last Laugh, Lurebound Scarecrow, Lurking Jackals, Mana Vortex, Mazemind Tome, Opal Avenger, Plague Boiler, Porphyry Nodes, Serendib Djinn, Skeleton Ship, Synod Centurion, Task Mage Assembly, Tethered Griffin, The Millennium Calendar, Tidal Influence, Transcendence and Veiled Crocodile, and with ADR 0107 §2 the serpents Bog Serpent, Dandân, Giant Shark (its block trigger reads ADR 0107 PR 5's `DealtDamageThisTurn`), Gorilla Pack, Island Fish Jasconius, Manta Ray, Marjhan, Merchant Ship, Pirate Ship, Ronom Serpent, Sea Serpent, Slipstream Serpent and Vodalian Knights. Bomb Squad's state trigger about each creature followed (see "State triggers about each of several objects"). **Moved to other rows:** Rune-Tail on flip cards (#1892), Phylactery Lich on an as-enters counter on another permanent (#1893), Seasinger on a two-condition control duration (#1894), City in a Bottle on a land-play restriction (#1895), Immortal Coil on "damage prevented this way" and Nine Lives on a prevention effect's CR 615.12 additional effect (both #1860) and Kukemssa Serpent on a land type for a duration (#1881).

- **Disturb** (#1855, CR 702.146, [ADR 0107 §4](decisions/0107-state-triggers-rebound-disturb-and-damage-prevention.md#4-disturb-1855)) — an alternative cost that casts a card's back face out of its owner's graveyard. `AlternativeCost.CastsFace` names the face, and `effects.Disturb(cost)` is the graveyard-bound offer with `CastsFace: 1`. The claim settles the face (`faceForClaimLocked`): the card goes on the stack back face up (CR 712.11a) and enters that way (CR 702.146b), a caller may send face 0 or 1, and the claim is refused on any card that is not a `transform` card with that face. The offer and the zone it opens are judged off the front face, which prints them, and every other announce gate off the back (CR 712.11d, 712.8c), so a disturbed Aura targets with its own enchant clause; the mana value is the front face's, on the stack and the battlefield (CR 712.8c, 712.8e). The back face's "If [this] would be put into a graveyard from anywhere, exile it instead" is `DisturbedExile(name)` (`GraveyardBecomesExile{SelfOnly: true}`) on the `"<oracle_id>#1"` entry, read only while that face is up (CR 712.8a). `disturb` joins `canonicalKeywords`. The bot enumerator and the view turn the card over through `AlternativeCost.CastFaceOf` before reading the target clause, the timing and the cast gate, so the graveyard menu needs no client change. A double-faced card's keywords are now per face (`game.Face.Keywords`, stamped by the importer from Scryfall's union and materialised by `SetFace`), so a back face no longer keeps its front face's keywords; the field is additive in snapshot v7, and `dorothea/sacrifice-at-end-of-combat` is a new on-disk delayed-trigger body key. **Cards:** Malevolent Hermit // Benevolent Geist (the row's Waiting card), Baithook Angler, Beloved Beggar, Binding Geist, Brine Comber, Chaplain of Alms, Covert Cutpurse, Covetous Castaway, Devoted Grafkeeper, Distracting Geist, Drogskol Infantry, Faithbound Judge, Galedrifter, Gutter Skulker, Katilda, Dawnhart Martyr, Kindly Ancestor, Lantern Bearer, Lunarch Veteran, Mirrorhall Mimic, Mischievous Catgeist, Mourning Patrol, Overwhelmed Archivist and Twinblade Geist, all Full; Dorothea, Vengeful Victim, Full on the front and with a caveat on the back. **Still open:** Dennick, Pious Apprentice waits on "cards in graveyards can't be targeted" (#1885).

- **Giving a spell rebound** (#1854, CR 613.1f / 702.88 / 616.1 / 400.7a, [ADR 0107 §3](decisions/0107-state-triggers-rebound-disturb-and-damage-prevention.md#3-rebound-1854) decision 5, owner decision 3, Delivery PR 4) — a spell can gain an ability on the stack. The stack step of the layer pass is now layers 2 and 6, keywords only: after `stackControlPassLocked` settles each spell's controller, `stackKeywordPassLocked` (`game/spell_keywords.go`) applies, in timestamp order, every ScopedEffect pinned to the spell with an `addKeywords` mod and every battlefield static declared `StaticAbility.AffectsSpells`, and stamps the result on `Card.stackGranted`. `HasKeyword` and `Effective()` read it beside the printed keywords, so PR 3's `spellRebounds` picks a granted rebound up with no change, and the stack view shows it. `stackPinProblem` admits `addKeywords` beside `setController`, and now also runs at restore, so a newer binary's stack-pin mod is refused (`ErrUnknownEffectKey`) rather than restored as a record that does nothing; the PR 3 binary does not run that check, so a rollback to it restores a spell given rebound without it (weaker, never stronger). A static over spells is never gathered by the battlefield pass, a cast bumps the layer version while one is out, `resolveTopOfStackLocked` recomputes before it copies the resolving card, and `effects.Register` refuses one outside layer 6, on an emblem or from a declared zone. A keyword given to a permanent spell follows it onto the permanent (CR 400.7a: `inheritSpellControlLocked` re-pins the record even when nothing stole the spell). **CR 616.1:** rebound, buyback and an Adventure's exile all replace "put it into its owner's graveyard as it resolves", so when two apply the spell's controller chooses with an `option_pick` while the card waits on the stack (`game/resolution_exits.go`); the buyback-and-Adventure pair, settled until now by a stated judgement call for buyback, is the controller's choice too. Flashback still wins outright (CR 702.34a), and a flashed-back spell was never cast from a hand. Card helpers: `effects.SpellsYouControlHave`, `effects.ThatSpellGains`, `effects.YouCastFromYourHand`, and `game.ObjectAttackedThisTurn` for an intervening "if ~ attacked this turn" rechecked from last-known information. **Cards:** Cast Through Time and Taigam, Ojutai Master, both Full. **Moved off the row:** Narset Transcendent waits on its emblem's "can't cast" (#1899, `emblem-cast-restrictions`), and Ojer Pakpatiq, Deepest Epoch // Temple of Cyclical Time on returning a card to the battlefield transformed from a graveyard (#1900, `return-transformed`).

- **Rebound** (#1854, CR 702.88, [ADR 0107 §3](decisions/0107-state-triggers-rebound-disturb-and-damage-prevention.md#3-rebound-1854), Delivery PR 3) — printed rebound. `rebound` joins `canonicalKeywords`, and the stack's graveyard route (`routeStackCardToGraveyardLocked`) gains a fourth arm after flashback: a spell that RESOLVED, has rebound (`HasKeyword`, so a deck-imported card works with no catalog entry; several instances are one, CR 702.88c), is not a copy, and was cast from its controller's hand (`StackItem.CastFromZone`) is exiled instead of going to the graveyard. A countered or fizzled rebound spell, or one cast from anywhere but a hand, goes to the graveyard as usual (CR 608.2n). The route's continuation schedules a delayed trigger for the spell's controller (CR 603.7d) at their next upkeep (`At: StepUpkeep`, `ControllerTurnOnly`), naming the exiled object in `Params.Object` so a card that left exile in the meantime is offered nothing (CR 603.7c, 400.7). Its body is the new on-disk key `rebound/cast`: as it resolves it asks suspend's `may_cast` prompt (`may_cast_keyword: "rebound"`, CR 603.5), and "yes" grants a per-object `{0}` cast with flash timing (CR 608.2g, 702.88b) whose window closes on its holder's next pass, leaving the card in exile (`CastPermission.LapseOnPass: "exile"`, a new lapse value). The bot needs nothing new: the enumerator offers both answers and then the free cast, and the heuristic takes the offer. Three v7 corpus boards hold it on disk: the trigger queued, the trigger on the stack, and the grant. **Cards:** Artful Maneuver, Blessed Reincarnation, Blossoming Calm, Center Soul, Consuming Vapors, Distortion Strike, Emerge Unscathed, Ephemerate, Faithless Salvaging, Fantastic Elasticity, Fevered Suspicion, Flame On!, Great Teacher's Decree, Into the Time Vortex, Invisible Force Field, It's Clobberin' Time!, Nomads' Assembly, Ojutai's Breath, Ojutai's Summons, Prey's Vengeance, Profound Journey, Quantum Misalignment, Recurring Insight, Sight Beyond Sight, Staggershock, Surreal Memoir, Survival Cache, Taigam's Strike, Terramorph, Transpose, Trumpeting Herd, Virulent Swipe and Void Squall, all Full. **Still open:** World at War waits on the extra-combats row (`AnchorNthMainPhase`, `DelayedTrigger.OnPhaseID`), and a spell GIVEN rebound (Taigam, Ojutai Master; Narset Transcendent; Ojer Pakpatiq; Cast Through Time) waits on the new granted-rebound row, ADR 0107 PR 4.

- **Damage that can't be prevented** (#1853, [ADR 0107](decisions/0107-state-triggers-rebound-disturb-and-damage-prevention.md) §5, PR 6) — CR 615.12 is a rule-modifying effect (CR 613.11) read at one gate, `damageUnpreventableLocked`, while the CR 614 window settles a damage event. It asks four sources in order: **the damage's own rider** (`DealDamage.CantBePrevented` on an ability or a target-dependent clause; `Spec.SpellDamageCantBePrevented`, a `game.SpellCondition` judged as the spell deals its damage, for a spell — Combust always, Banefire with X of 5 or more, Arrow Storm with raid); **the source's own static** ("Damage that would be dealt by this creature can't be prevented", read as the event opens and from last-known information once the source has left, CR 608.2h); **battlefield statics** (`Spec.DamageCantBePrevented`: all damage, combat damage, or combat damage dealt by creatures you control, read live and keyed by `CatalogAbilityKey`); and **turn grants** (`ModDamageCantBePrevented`, game-wide, or pinned to one creature, ended at cleanup, CR 514.2). Every prevention effect now declares `ReplacementEffect.Prevention` — protection's built-in (CR 702.16e), Fog's and Mending Hands' scoped shields and the catalog's three — and `TestDamageReplacementsDeclareWhetherTheyPrevent` reads every damage replacement's source and fails one that cancels or cuts the damage without saying what it is. While the gate says yes, the apply-loop settles each prevention effect once without running it (CR 615.12a): the damage is dealt, a charged shield keeps its charge, protection stops nothing, and no CR 616 prompt is asked about a shield that will do nothing. Lava Burst's "or dealt instead to another permanent or player" is the same treatment for `RedirectsDamage` replacements (`ModDamageCantBeRedirected` for a pinned grant). A spell's conditional "this spell can't be countered" (`Spec.CantBeCounteredIf`) shipped beside it for Banefire. The view shows a stack chip for a marked spell and a game-banner line for a live turn grant. **Cards:** Skullcrack, Call In a Professional, Banefire, Stomp, Combust, Pinpoint Avalanche, Arrow Storm, Lightning Surge, Urza's Rage, Lava Burst, Fear, Fire, Foes!, Flames of the Blood Hand, Flaring Pain, Impractical Joke, Wild Slash, Unstable Footing, Excruciator, Malignus, Sunspine Lynx, Frenzied Baloth, Pyrewood Gearhulk and Questing Beast (Full), and Leyline of Punishment (Caveats: no opening-hand start). **Moved to other rows:** Demonfire (#1886), Whippoorwill (#1887), Volcano Hellion (#1888), Everlasting Torment (#1889), Insult // Injury and Isengard Unleashed (#1890), Alchemist's Gambit (extra turns), Spider-Punk (riot).

- **Targets that must come from a single graveyard** (#1807, CR 601.2c / CR 608.2b / CR 115.7d-e, [ADR 0106 §5](decisions/0106-five-small-seams-from-the-s50-rechecks.md)) — "up to four target cards from a single graveyard" needed the opposite of #1559's set rule: every pick must SHARE a key rather than differ on one. `TargetSpec.Same *game.TargetSameness{Label, Key}` is that sibling, keyed on the card's owner (`game.TargetShareOwner`; a card is only ever in its owner's graveyard, CR 400.3) and built with `effects.FromASingleGraveyard()` or `UpToCardsFromASingleGraveyard`. The key is a closed enum, not a func, so the ADR 0041 closure ratchet gains no route and the snapshot is untouched: the clause is rebuilt from the catalog row on restore. The readers that judge `Different` each gained a "share" branch: the announce gate refuses a set with two keys (`those targets must come from a single graveyard`); the CR 608.2b re-check judges the surviving picks, with `setRuleConflictLocked`'s weaker reading; `fillableCountLocked` answers the largest key group, so an exact four (Pestilent Cauldron) is not offered unless one graveyard holds four; the one-slot retarget offer narrows to the graveyard the staying slots are in, while the gate judges only the final set, so moving every slot to another graveyard at once is legal (CR 115.7e); the enumerator builds sets inside one group; and the view ships `same: {label, keys}` beside `different`, which the client picker uses to grey every candidate outside the first pick's graveyard. **21 cards:** Digsite Conservator, Decompose, Scarab Feast, Rapid Decay, Shred Memory (with a new `Transmute` constructor, CR 702.53), Carrion Beetles, Rag Dealer, Famished Ghoul, Griffnaut Tracker, Arashin Sunshield, Gravegouger, Soul-Shackled Zombie, Ebony Charm, Rats' Feast, Serene Remembrance, Unlicensed Hearse, Waste Management, Kaya, Orzhov Usurper, Cease // Desist, Pestilent Cauldron // Restorative Burst, all `full`, and Push // Pull, whose Pull half returns its two creatures one after the other (`caveats`). **Still open**, on the row's Waiting list: Martyr of Bones, Qutrub Forayer, Feral Deathgorger // Dusk Sight and Spellweaver Helix, held for their other text.

- **Making other spells uncounterable** (#1806, [ADR 0106](decisions/0106-five-small-seams-from-the-s50-rechecks.md) §4, PRs 2 and 3) — every form of "can't be countered" is a rule-modifying effect (CR 613.11), read at one gate, `spellCantBeCounteredLocked`, which every counter verb and the stack chip ask. It has five sources, asked in order: the spell's printed rider; the mana that paid for it (#1547); **marks on the stack item** (`StackItem.CantBeCountered`, a `game.CounterShieldMark` per source — "Target spell can't be countered", Vexing Shusher, lasting while that object is on the stack, CR 400.7, kept by a stolen spell, never inherited by a copy, CR 707.2); **the battlefield statics** (`Spec.SpellsCantBeCountered`, `game.CounterShieldStatic` in its "you control", "you cast" and "any player" forms, read live and keyed by `CatalogAbilityKey`); and **a player's "this turn" grants** (`PlayerStatic.CantBeCountered`, a `game.CounterShieldGrant` with a `PermissionFilter`, "you control" or "you cast", and an `Except` object for "other spells", ended at cleanup, CR 514.2). The same payload with `NextOnly` is **the one-use promise** ("the next creature spell you cast this turn can't be countered"): never read at the gate, it is spent at CR 601.2i by the first matching spell its player casts, and turned into a mark on that spell; two matching promises are both spent; a copy (CR 707.10) and a land spend nothing. Both new fields are plain data on disk (additive in v7; an older binary refuses a file with a mark). The player view lists live grants and unspent promises as `counter_shields`, shown on the seat as a NO COUNTER badge. **Cards:** Chimil, the Inner Sun (Full) and thirteen other printed statics in PR 2; Insist, Overmaster, Mistrise Village, Vexing Shusher, Domri, Anarch of Bolas and Bound // Determined (Full) and Veil of Summer (Caveats: no "hexproof from" keyword, ADR 0038 §6) in PR 3. **Moved to other rows:** Savage Summoning (its flash and +1/+1 counter promises), Spider-Punk (riot), Frenzied Baloth (damage that can't be prevented), Taigam, Ojutai Master (rebound), Malevolent Hermit // Benevolent Geist (disturb) and Goldbug (More Than Meets the Eye).

- **Evolve** (#1805, CR 702.100, [ADR 0106 §3](decisions/0106-five-small-seams-from-the-s50-rechecks.md#3-evolve-1805)) — the second engine-derived keyword trigger, built on prowess's model. `evolve` joins `canonicalKeywords` and is cumulative (CR 702.100d), and `keywordTriggersFor` hands `TriggersForCard` one evolve trigger per instance on the effective ability list (`game/evolve.go`), so a printed evolve (stamped by the deck importer), a token's, and a layer-6 grant (Tyranid Prime's "other creatures you control", Propagator Drone's "creature tokens you control") all work with no catalog entry. The comparison is **CR 702.100a**'s — the old registry note credited it to 702.100b — power against power and toughness against toughness, false if either side is not a creature (702.100c), and made twice under the intervening-"if" rule (CR 603.4): on `EventETB`, with the layer cache already caught up, and again when the keyed body `evolve/grow` resolves, reading the entered creature through the item's carried trigger object, live or as it last existed (CR 608.2h), and the evolving creature live only (CR 400.7). The +1/+1 counter goes through the CR 614 counter window, and the new `EventEvolved` is emitted behind it only when a counter actually landed — CR 702.100b's "evolves", which Renegade Krasis and Watchful Radstag watch. Evolve triggers keep the CR 603.3b ordering prompt (they change each other's comparisons), and each label names the creature that entered, so two triggers on one creature from creatures entering together are told apart. `evolve/grow` is a new on-disk body key; nothing else in the snapshot changed. **Cards:** Dinosaur Egg drops its caveat and goes Full, plus Tyranid Prime, Propagator Drone, Renegade Krasis, Watchful Radstag, Darwin, Adaptive Mutant, Elusive Krasis, Experiment One, Gyre Sage, Lonis, Genetics Expert, Pollywog Prodigy, Ray Fillet, Wave Warrior, Scurry Oak and Simic Fluxmage, all Full. Adaptive Snapjaw, Battering Krasis, Clinging Anemones, Cloudfin Raptor, Crocanura, Gluttonous Slug and Shambleshark need no card file. **Still open:** Fathom Mage waits on a trigger that fires once per counter (#1841) and Simic Manipulator on a target bounded by the counters removed to pay (#1842).

- **A creature that can't attack its owner** (#1794, CR 508.1c / CR 508.1d / CR 508.4c / CR 508.7b, [ADR 0106 §2](decisions/0106-five-small-seams-from-the-s50-rechecks.md)) — Xantcha, Sleeper Agent "can't attack its owner or planeswalkers its owner controls", a restriction keyed on the ATTACKER, which the old per-seat target check could not say. `Characteristic.AttackTargetRestrictions []game.AttackTargetRestriction{Source, SourceName, NotOwner, NotOwnersPlaneswalkers}` is `AttackRequirements`' twin: written by an ordinary layer-6 static (`effects.CantAttackItsOwner()`, `effects.CantAttackItsOwnerOrItsOwnersPlaneswalkers()`), only ever appended to, gone with the creature's own abilities (CR 613.1f), and additive in the snapshot through `lastKnownBattlefield` under schema v7. One predicate, `canAttackTargetWithLocked(attacker, target)`, is CR 506.2's controller check plus that list, reading the owner live (CR 108.3: a copy uses its own owner) and never refusing a battle. Both declaration verbs call it (the single verb refuses with "… can't attack its owner or planeswalkers its owner controls."; the bulk verb skips the entry), and so does the CR 508.1d search, so a creature that must attack and has only its owner to attack owes nothing. The enumerator walks a per-attacker `AttackTargetsForAttackerForEffect`, so the ADR 0105 digest's `attack_targets`, the client's per-attacker gate and the attack-all planner's per-seat count follow; `Turn.AttackTargets` stays per seat for prices and limits. CR 508.7b's reselection and CR 508.4c's entry attacking keep the controller-only check. The card view carries `attack_target_restrictions` with the owner resolved to a seat, and the board draws a "CAN'T ATTACK <name>" chip from it. **No card lands yet:** Xantcha also needs "any player may activate" (#1793), Alexios, Deimos of Kosmos "can't be sacrificed", and Elrond of the White Council secret council plus a mod kind that grants the restriction; all three are on the row's Waiting list.

- **Abilities any player may activate** (#1793, CR 602.2 / CR 602.1a / CR 602.1b / CR 109.5 / CR 113.8, [ADR 0106 §1](decisions/0106-five-small-seams-from-the-s50-rechecks.md)) — "Any player may activate this ability", which CR 602.2 lets an object say and the engine could not: every gate assumed the activator was the controller. `ActivatedAbility.AnyPlayer` (mirrored on `game.ActivatedAbilityShape`) is the row's own CR 602.1b permission, so a copy or a layer-6 grant of the row carries it and losing the ability loses it; nothing is serialised. One predicate, `game.MayActivate(player, source, zone, ab)` — the controller, or anyone for an AnyPlayer row on the battlefield, the CR 108.4a owner off it — is asked by `ActivateCatalogAbility`, by the enumerator (`activatedMoves` now visits other players' permanents for their AnyPlayer rows only, behind a fast negative) and by the view. Everything after that question already read the activator: the board-wide gate, the timing read, the `Condition`, every cost validator and payer (CR 602.1a), and the stack item's controller (CR 602.2a, 113.8), so "you" in the effect is the activator (CR 109.5) and "this permanent's controller" is `ctx.SourcePermanent().Controller`, last-known if it has left (CR 608.2h). The once-per-turn and exhaust records stay keyed by object and label (CR 602.5b). Arrest still stops every player. Register refuses AnyPlayer beside a {T}, loyalty, crew or sacrifice-this component and on a non-battlefield zone. The view marks the row `any_player` and gives every other seat its own copy stamped with that seat as the activator — price, life, condition, timing, and the option lists out of that seat's own hand — through the #1369 per-seat carrier (`stampAnyPlayerOffers`). The ADR 0105 digest needed no change: built from the seat's own moves, it lists another player's Xantcha under its ID for the activator alone. The client opens the ability popover on another player's permanent for its `any_player` rows, lights the bolt pip from the digest, and smart autopass does not count such a move as a response (owner decision 1). `ActivatedAbility.Purpose` (`game.ActivationPurpose{Draws, ControllerLosesLife}`, wire `purpose`) is the bot's reason to reach across: the heuristic never chooses a row on another player's permanent without one (owner decision 2), and the enumerator's label names the controller for the model tiers. Xantcha, Sleeper Agent lands Full, with 27 more any-player cards in the follow-up batch (26 Full; Fan Favorite carries a caveat for assist). Nine more wait on their real blockers: Lethal Vapors (skipping a turn), Deadly Designs and Task Mage Assembly (state triggers), Glittering Lion and Glittering Lynx (losing one printed ability), Mercenaries (a shield against the next damage from one source), Nullhide Ferox (a discard caused by an opponent), Land's Edge (the card discarded to pay) and Wall of Vipers (a target relative to the source). Out of scope: any-player mana abilities (Mana Cache) and abilities of a spell on the stack (Lightning Storm).

- **Each player takes from their own look, against two predicates** (#1743, CR 101.4 / CR 101.4a / CR 701.20, [ADR 0013 amendment 2026-10-01](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md#5ai-amendment-2026-10-01-a-card-set-pick-is-a-run-too--every-player-chooses-from-their-own-look)) — Explore the Vastlands' "each player looks at the top five cards of their library and may reveal a land card and/or an instant or sorcery card from among them", which slice 296-m left uncatalogued because every look-at-top-N shape was ONE player choosing at most one card against ONE predicate. Two generalisations of the existing seam, no new prompt kind and no wire change. **The fan-out is a run:** `game.ChooseCardsRunThenForEffect(legs, then)` queues one ordinary `choose_cards` prompt per seat as one §5x run (the discard leg's link, which the catalog could not set) and hands `then` `PromptedPicks` once, after the last leg settles; the legs go up together in APNAP order and are answered privately (CR 101.4a), so nothing is revealed or moved until every seat has answered, a seat with no candidate is not asked, and a departing chooser's leg settles with nothing through the existing `dropDefault`. **Two predicates are slots:** `effects.TakeFromLibraryToHand.Slots` (`TakeSlot{Label, Match, Max}`) makes the pick one prompt over every card that fits any slot, with a set rule — a maximum bipartite matching, so a card matching both slots fills one — riding the prompt's existing `Validate`; the ceiling is what the slots can hold out of the actual candidates (three lands and no spell is "up to one"), and a mandatory slotted clause takes it as its floor, so no slotted prompt can be unanswerable. `internal/legal` needed nothing: every set it offers already goes through `ChooseCardsPickLegalLocked`. `effects.EachPlayerTakesFromLibrary` is the card side: each player LOOKS (only they know their five, and no seat sees another's prompt), every player's picks are revealed before any card moves, each player's rest goes to the bottom on their own `random_order` stream with every knower cleared, and the fan-out's `Then` runs last. **1 card:** Explore the Vastlands is catalogued under `<oracle>#1` and `full`; Wandering Archaic's caveat about it is cleared and the card is `full` on both faces. **Still open:** "each player REVEALS the top N" (the public version of the same fan-out) has no card in the gap asking for it.

- **Retarget to a fixed object** (#1743, CR 115.7b / CR 400.7, [ADR 0019 amendment 2026-10-01](decisions/0019-structured-targeting.md)) — "change a target of target spell or ability TO THIS CREATURE", the shape the `Stack-item retarget` and `Targeting an activated or triggered ability on the stack` entries both left open under Spellskite's name. It is one field on the existing offer, not a second retarget: `game.RetargetOffer.To` (card side `effects.ChangeTargets.To` / `ToSource`) pins the destination, and a slot is eligible only when the item's list with that one slot replaced by the destination passes `retargetCheckLocked` — the gate a free "change the target" answer already passes — so the slot's clause and zones, hexproof / shroud / protection judged for the ITEM's controller, the same-clause duplicate rule, `Distinct` and the #1559 set rules all apply with no line of their own, which is every one of Spellskite's 2020-08-07 rulings. No eligible slot changes nothing and asks nobody. `ToSource` refuses a source that has left the battlefield, including one that came back as a new object with the same instance ID (CR 400.7, #1418's `SourcePermanent`). The only question the variant asks is WHICH slot ("you choose which one target you're changing … as Spellskite's ability resolves"): one eligible object under a mandatory change is changed with no prompt; otherwise a `retarget` prompt whose options are the CURRENT targets that could change, carrying the new `PendingChoice.RetargetTo`, which `ResolveRetarget` routes to `resolvePinnedRetargetLocked`. Same prompt kind, same departure row, same wire, same `internal/legal` case; `RetargetTo` is carried by the snapshot (additive in v7). Two instances of "target" naming one object are one option on the board and the earlier slot changes — weaker than printed, declared as a caveat. **3 cards:** Spellskite (`{U/P}`, payable with 2 life through #917) and Mizzium Meddler are new with that caveat; Hydroelectric Specimen's enters trigger is built and the card is `full`. **Still open:** Captured by the Consulate ("to enchanted creature if able") and Ricochet ("to the player with the lowest result") need no new retarget machinery and wait for a batch; Muck Drubb's "target spell that targets only a single creature" is a predicate over what a slot names, the gap #1815 records for Chef's Kiss.

- **Trigger suppression** (#1735, CR 603.2 / CR 603.10, [ADR 0018 amendment 2026-10-01](decisions/0018-triggers-on-the-stack.md)) — closes item (4) of the "Stopping abilities" row: "Creatures entering don't cause abilities to trigger" and its family. It is neither a replacement effect (the event still happens) nor an ability removal (the permanents keep their abilities). It is a static that decides whether an event triggers an ability at all. `Spec.TriggerSuppressors []game.TriggerSuppressor` is the `TriggerDoubler`'s sibling, read from the same per-event candidate list (`scanTriggerModifiersLocked`), and `harvestMatchLocked` asks it **first**: before the once-per-batch guard, so a suppressed match does not spend a "whenever one or more" slot, and before the doublers, so a suppressed ability is never doubled. With an Elesh Norn on each side of the table, nothing triggers on entry at all. A suppressed ability makes nothing: no instance, no "you may" prompt, no target prompt, no `EventTrigger`. `harvestMatchLocked` now takes a `triggerOrigin` (a permanent, a spell, or neither) in place of `fromSpell`, which is what lets Elesh Norn's "abilities of permanents your opponents control" skip emblems and cards in other zones. **Which board is asked** follows CR 603.10: an enters trigger is judged after the event, so a suppressor that enters together with the creature applies (including Elesh Norn's own entry), and one that has already left does not, even as a member of an open simultaneous exit (`modifierCandidate.live`). A dies trigger looks back in time, so a Hushbringer that dies in the same wipe still stops every death in it, its own included. Every harvest site is covered, including **evoke's sacrifice**, which is the creature's own enters trigger, so an evoked creature stays under Torpor Orb. Event-conditioned **delayed triggers** are covered too, and a suppressed one stays queued: an earthbent land that dies under Hushbringer does not come back. Helpers: `SuppressesEntering(filter)`, `SuppressesDying(filter)`, `OfOpponentsPermanents(s)` and `CreaturesEnteringDontTrigger(label)` in `effects/trigger_suppression.go`. They share the doubler's `isEntering` / `isDying`. No wire, snapshot or bot change. **Cards:** Torpor Orb, Hushwing Gryff, Tocatli Honor Guard, Doorkeeper Thrull and Hushbringer (Full); Elesh Norn, Mother of Machines loses its caveat and goes Full. That is every card the row's waiting list named for this item. "Trigger suppression" joins the coverage table's mechanic probes. **Still open on the row:** a scoped "creatures lose all abilities" (Dress Down, Tishana's Tidebinder).

- **A sacrifice as an alternative cost** (#1727, CR 118.9 / CR 601.2h / CR 701.21a / CR 702.34a, [ADR 0025 amendment 2026-10-01](decisions/0025-alternative-costs.md)) — Dread Return's "Flashback—Sacrifice three creatures" had no shape: `game.AlternativeCost` could charge mana, life, a pitched card, a bounced permanent and escape's graveyard exile, but not a sacrifice of the caster's own permanents. **`AlternativeCost.Sacrifice`** is the additional cost's clause verbatim — a `TargetSpec` over permanents with the count on `Min` == `Max`, built by `sacrificeSpec` — read through `cardComponent` like the other card components, validated by the one `validateSacrificeCostLocked` (exactly N, each once, each the caster's own, each matching, nothing moved on a refusal) and paid by the one `payCostSacrificesLocked` (an `EventSacrifice` per permanent, one simultaneous exit, with the spell already on the stack so the dies triggers resolve first and a counter gives nothing back). The picks ride `alt_cost_ids`, not `sacrifice_ids`: they pay the offer, and `PaidCost.Sacrificed`, the per-sacrifice discount and the additional-cost validator all read the other list; a permanent named to both is refused (CR 118.3). The view ships the offer's options as **`sacrifice_options`** (via `sacrificeCostOptions`, payment order, per viewer) instead of `pay_options`, so the client opens `SacrificeCostModal`; an offer the board cannot fill is not stamped, so the card is not `castable_here`.
