The event now carries `Target`, `CardID` and `Label`, and the log renders the label, because a countered ability has no card to name. **What needed nothing**: `RetargetStackItemForEffect` and `OfferRetargetForEffect` (#1196) are written over `StackMeta` already, so the "or ability" half of the redirect family is one word in a clause constructor; `CounterTargetForEffect` has discriminated on `item.Kind` since S13.1; the bot's enumerator and the protocol's `stampLegalTargets` are id-agnostic; the stack overlay already lights any item in the legal set. The client's share is three strings — the `stack_ability` and `stack_item` target modes beside `stack_spell` (the banner's sentence, `isTargetingStack`) and the cast flow's mode allowlist, now `opensTargetPicker` in `targeting.ts` rather than a chain of `===` in `Board.svelte`, since a mode missing from it fires `cast_spell` with no targets and the server refuses it with nothing on screen to say why. **7 cards:** Stifle, Tale's End and Voidslime are new and `full`; **Disallow**, **Deflecting Swat**, **Bolt Bend** and **Sublime Epiphany** lost their caveats and are `full` — the Epiphany got its printed SECOND bullet back, kept as a bullet of its own rather than merged into the first with a wider clause, because CR 700.2 lets "choose one or more" take both. **Still open, and each a different shape**: **Trickbind**'s second sentence ("activated abilities of that permanent can't be activated this turn"), a per-permanent activation restriction with a turn duration that [ADR 0073](decisions/0073-optional-additional-costs-and-the-cast-gate.md) already prices — the card is not shipped; ~~**Spellskite** (#302), which changes a target TO a named object~~ — **closed by #1743**; and **"counter target ability unless its controller pays"**, whose guard (`game/counter_unless_paid.go:105`) is keyed to a spell's stack card and which no card in the gap needs yet.

- **Amass (CR 701.47)** (#1236, CR 701.47a-d, [ADR 0087](decisions/0087-amass.md)) — the seam had no row of its own: the roadmap's detector files it under "Keyword actions with no primitive (proliferate, surveil, explore, connive, amass…)", where it was the last unclosed member. **The rules number in the issue is wrong** — amass is CR **701.47** in the pinned edition (`MagicCompRules 20260819`); 701.35 is the number it had before the Alchemy and Attraction keyword actions were inserted ahead of it, and 701.51 is *Open an Attraction*. Corrected in the ADR and in the code, the way #1260 corrected storm's. **The verb is a counted keyword action**, not a card primitive: `KeywordActionAmass`, a count on `RepEventKeywordAction`, one arm of `applyResolvedKeywordActionLocked` — the three pieces proliferate, scry, surveil and earthbend have, and the answer to #1236's third bullet (a future "double the counters from amass" doubles the INSTRUCTION, one level above the placement, which stays a plain `AddCounterByForEffect` so Hardened Scales and Doubling Season keep working). It **acts at a count of zero**, earthbend's rule for earthbend's reason: the count is a number of COUNTERS and the other three sentences do not depend on it, so Summons of Saruman off an empty graveyard still leaves an Army — which then dies to CR 704.5f, which is why the template's 0/0 is `PrintedPTKnown`. **The Army token is DERIVED from the keyword, not tabled and not an ADR 0083 template.** Not the catalog, because the Army is vanilla and `checkTokenTemplate` refuses a template with no ability — which is also why this is ADR 0087 and not an amendment to 0083. Not `tokens_table.go` either: `TokenCard` panics on a missing key, four species have a printed Army token card today (Zombie, Orc, Goblin, Sliver) with four more named by amass cards outside tournament-legal sets, the next set can name a ninth, and CR 701.47a *derives* the characteristics from the keyword's parameter rather than reading them off a card. `effects.ArmyToken(subtype)` is that derivation, pinned against the real Scryfall dump for all four printed species by the #1127 guard. **Find-or-create** is `armiesControlledLocked` then ADR 0061's one token path (`CreateTokensThenForEffect`, because the creation can pause), with the board RE-READ in the continuation — so a Doubling Season really does make two Armies and the pick below becomes a real question, which is the ruling. **The pick** is `QueueChooseCardsForEffect` over the battlefield, Min 1 / Max 1 — `effects.SacrificeChoice`'s door, the one prompt kind that carries a continuation — asked only when the controller has MORE THAN ONE Army, because Orcish Bowmasters amasses on every opponent draw and a modal you cannot answer wrongly is a worse table than a silent one. The enumerator needed nothing (`PendingChoiceChooseCards` has been enumerated since #544); `legal/amass_choice_test.go` pins that every Army is offered, nothing else is, every move dispatches and none is marked always-legal. **The fourth sentence is the one #1236 did not see**, and it is why this sits on tracker [#889](https://github.com/krakenhavoc/cmd_and_ctrl/issues/889): *"if it isn't a [subtype], it becomes a [subtype] in addition to its other types"* is a layer-4 continuous effect with no stated duration, `PinnedTo` the object for CR 611.2a + CR 400.7 — `animateEarthbentLandLocked`'s layer-4 arm one subtype over — registered only when the Army does not already have the type, which is both the rule's "if it isn't" and the reason a wheel does not leave a hundred identical layer effects in the census. **CR 701.47c** ("the Army you amassed") is a second continuation on `keywordActionTail`, `func(*Game, uuid.UUID)`, run exactly once and run with `uuid.Nil` on the abandoned path because CR 701.47b says the player amassed anyway. **4 cards:** Orcish Bowmasters (#295, the card the issue was filed for — `caveats`, Notion Thief's draw-step caveat word for word), Dreadhorde Invasion (#306) and Eternal Skylord (#451) `full`, plus Widespread Brutality, which is not on a batch list and is here because it is the only printed card that exercises CR 701.47c and the "non-Army" read side. **Closed since** (#1282, [ADR 0013](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md) §5ac): a continuation that survives a paused counter placement. `AddCounterByThenForEffect` carries CR 701.47c's clause, so it runs when the counters land. **Also closed** (#1289, [ADR 0007 §7](decisions/0007-stack-foundation.md) and [ADR 0013](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md) §5ae): a fresh 0/0 Army whose counter placement pauses survives to get its counters, because state-based actions wait for a resolution paused on its own prompt. **Still open:** the other seventy-two amass cards, each waiting on its own row — Barad-dûr (#309) on the mana pipeline and a per-turn death tally, Lazotep Plating (#388) and Gleaming Overseer (#389) on protection / prevention, Sauron, the Dark Lord (#390) on the Ring, Saruman, the White Hand (#391) on cost modification. Trackers [#889](https://github.com/krakenhavoc/cmd_and_ctrl/issues/889) (the subtype half) and [#882](https://github.com/krakenhavoc/cmd_and_ctrl/issues/882) (the three CR 614 windows it rides: the keyword action, the token creation and the counter placement — none of them new).

- **Ninjutsu, an entry that puts a CARD onto the battlefield attacking, and an exported "unblocked attacker"** (#1227, CR 702.49 / CR 506.3c / CR 509.1h, [ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md)'s, [ADR 0045](decisions/0045-combat-restrictions.md)'s and [ADR 0073](decisions/0073-optional-additional-costs-and-the-cast-gate.md)'s 2026-09-23 amendments) — the last two halves of the keyword #1221 declared out of scope, and the zone half needed nothing: `ActivatedAbilityShape.Zones = {ZoneHand}` has reached ninjutsu since #660, the `ReturnToHand` component landed with #1213, and `AbilityNeedsPermanentSource` already admitted it because the permanent a return cost returns is not the source. Not one line of `ActivateCatalogAbility` changed. **`ZoneEntryOptions.Attacking uuid.UUID`** is seeded onto **`ReplacementEvent.EntersAttacking`** exactly as `Tapped` is onto `EntersTapped`, so the entry's one settled record carries the fact and a CR 616 resume holding only the event still knows what the entry was for; the TOKEN door now carries the same field (`enterCreatedTokensLocked` copies the minted token's own `AttackingTarget` onto its entry event), which leaves **`stampEntryAttackerLocked`** as the single place CR 506.3c's "never DECLARED as an attacker" marking happens for both doors. That marking is three facts, and the third was a hole: the `AttackingTarget` stamp, `noteAttackAnnouncedLocked` (so the declaration's lock-in emits no `EventAttack` and no "whenever ~ attacks" payoff sees a ninja), and `invalidateLayersForAttackChangeLocked` — #1218's FOURTH exit, which neither door had, so an Ohran Frostfang reading "attacking creature" kept a stale answer after a Parhelion II Angel arrived. `DeclareAttackerWith` is deliberately not the route: it refuses outside `StepDeclareAttackers`, it taps, it checks summoning sickness and it emits the event, wrong on all four counts. **`Game.UnblockedAttackerForEffect`** is the first exported reader of the blocked record and answers three facts rather than one — attacking, the cursor at declare blockers or later (CR 509.1h decides "unblocked" as PART of the declaration, which is why the Gatherer ruling says ninjutsu waits for blockers), and nothing blocking it, meaning both `blockedAttackers` AND a block that is staged and not yet locked in (#830). It inherits `blockers.go`'s declared simplification whole: nothing marks the declare-blockers turn-based action COMPLETE, so an attacker at the top of the step reads as unblocked — a beat early, never after a block. **`PaidCost.ReturnedAttacking`** is `Sacrificed`'s neighbour and exists for a stronger version of its reason: the returned permanent's exit clears `Card.AttackingTarget` and LKI carries no combat state, so the payer reads it BEFORE the bounce. The timing restriction is the COST rather than an `ActivatedAbilityShape.Condition` — CR 118.3 already refuses an unpayable activation, and the three consumers all narrow off the one candidate walk (#544): the validator refuses, `returnCostOptions` ships an empty picker and `returnPayments` offers the bot no move. **No wire change at all** — `return_label` / `return_options` / `return_ids` are #1213's and Board's announce chain never inspects a card's zone. One client fix: `returnShortfall` is factored out of `contextMenu.logic.ts` and asked by `ManaAbilityMenu.svelte` too, the popover a hand card and the zone browser open, which had no return arm — a ninjutsu row is unpayable for nearly the whole game, so a row that never greyed would be wrong more often than right. **4 cards, all `full`:** Ninja of the Deep Hours, Ingenious Infiltrator, Moonblade Shinobi and Prosperous Thief. **Still open:** "put onto the battlefield BLOCKING", which CR 509.1 has no entry for and no card asks for. Commander ninjutsu closed with #1278, below.

- **Commander ninjutsu: a non-cast exit from the command zone** (#1278, CR 702.49c / CR 903.8 / CR 903.9 / CR 400.7, [ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md)'s 2026-09-24 amendment, Decisions 38–40) — the one piece #1227 left. `effects.CommanderNinjutsu(cost)` is `Ninjutsu(cost)` with `Zones = {ZoneHand, ZoneCommand}`, and again not one line of `ActivateCatalogAbility` changed (the command zone has been scanned, gated by CR 113.6 and owner-checked under CR 108.4 since #660). **`Game.PutFromCommandZoneOntoBattlefieldForEffect`** is the hand door with the command zone for the hand: `startEntryBatchLocked` admits `ZoneCommand` as a `BatchEntry.From`, so the arrival runs the one CR 614 entry window and the CR 506.3c attacking stamp unchanged, and **keeps its instance ID** because `Player.CommanderCasts` is keyed by it. The bookkeeping the issue asked about is all negative and all pinned: no CR 903.8 tax and nothing added to it (the tally is bumped by the cast path alone), no CR 903.9 prompt on the way IN (`commanderZoneReplacement` is destination-only and the battlefield is not one of its four), and `Card.IsCommander` rides the card so the shared exit primitive from #539 offers CR 903.9 on the way OUT and combat damage counts as commander damage. **`ninjutsuEnter` now checks the OBJECT** (`Card.ObjectEpoch` against `StackItem.SourceEpoch`), because a commander discarded in response takes CR 903.9's command zone — a zone the ability also names, same ID — and by CR 400.7 the ability has lost her; this tightens plain ninjutsu the same way. **No wire change**: `abilityZones()` and `stampZoneAbilities` have walked the command zone since #1221. Client: `CommandZone.svelte` hands `zone_abilities` to its card (right-click popover, as the hand) with an `ability` hint beside `cast`. **1 card, `full`:** Yuriko, the Tiger's Shadow — the only non-joke printing. **Still open:** a commander returned AS the ninjutsu cost goes to hand without the CR 903.9 offer, because cost exits cannot pause (`MustSettleNow`, the posture every cost exit takes) — #1397.

- **Storm, and the turn's cast order** (#1238, CR 702.40 — storm is **702.40** in the pinned August 2026 edition; 702.39 is provoke — plus CR 113.7a and CR 608.2h, [ADR 0086](decisions/0086-storm-and-the-turns-cast-order.md)) — the seam had no row of its own, because it was filed while re-triaging roadmap batch 02 (#295) rather than by a batch PR appending a skip; **Flusterstorm** (`edhrec_rank` 307) is the card that was waiting, and every other storm card in the format was waiting on the same thing. The gap was never the copies (`CopySpell{Count, ChooseNewTargets}` has made N independent copies with N independent CR 707.10c prompts since S30) nor the trigger (`FromStack`, the shape `Cascade()` uses) — it was the NUMBER. `Game.SpellsCastThisTurn` is `{Total, Noncreature}` **per player**, and `TurnTally`'s `EventCast` arm bumped no counter at all, so nothing could answer "how many spells were cast **before this one**, by **anyone**". `TurnTally.Casts` is that: the turn's spell casts, table-wide, **in order**, appended by the existing `turnTallyListener` arm and read through `Game.SpellsCastBeforeThisTurn(spellID)`, which returns the spell's own index. Three readings of CR 702.40a drove the shape and each is a way the obvious implementation goes wrong — **"each other spell"** is every player's (a per-player count makes Brain Freeze a blank), **"was cast"** means a spell that was cast and then COUNTERED still counts (which is why the record is an ID taken at the cast rather than a log scan that looks the card up — Thousand-Year Storm's `b12InstantsAndSorceriesCastBeforeThisTurn` keeps its scan, because that card needs the type test and can afford to be weaker), and **"before it"** is an index, which no scalar can produce. The count is read as the **trigger resolves** (CR 608.2h), not captured at announce: the list is append-only and a spell's index is frozen by its own cast, so a spell cast in response provably cannot move it — a property a test pins rather than a coincidence of when a closure ran. This is why #1238's own sketch (capture the pre-increment `SpellsCastThisTurn` at `Build`) was not taken: it is per player, it is a scalar a second instance of storm would have to re-derive, and "pre-increment" is a fact about the order of two statements in `CastSpell`. **Card-side it is a declaration**: `Triggered: []game.TriggeredAbility{Storm()}`, no new `Spec` slot, two entries for CR 702.40b. **The log gained one kind**, `storm` / `EventStorm`, rendering "Grapeshot — storm count 3": a spell copy is created and not cast so it emits no event, and an ability's `resolve` entry names the ability but not the number (it named nothing at all until #1257), so without it the table watches N copies appear unexplained. **Cards:** Grapeshot, Brain Freeze, Empty the Warrens (whose token half needed nothing new — each copy is its own CR 701.7b creation instruction, #762), Flusterstorm (over the `CounterUnlessPaid` half that has existed since #951) and Tendrils of Agony, all `full`. **Closed since (#1255, [ADR 0086](decisions/0086-storm-and-the-turns-cast-order.md) and [ADR 0043](decisions/0043-copy-effects.md) 2026-09-23 amendments):** a storm spell COUNTERED in response to its own trigger keeps the trigger (CR 113.7a) **and is copied** from last-known information (CR 608.2h). `Game.lastKnownStack` records a spell's card and stack item at the one choke point every non-resolving exit from the stack passes (`routeCardToZoneLocked` with a stack source — #1318 retired the `DropStackMeta` flag), and `CopyLastKnownSpellForEffect` reads it after the stack and the #920 resolving slot. The lookup is **opt-in** (`CopySpell.FromLastKnown`): storm, Thousand-Year Storm and the Doublecast / Galvanic Iteration delayed trigger name the spell without targeting it and take it; Reverberate, Twincast, Dualcaster Mage and Lithoform Engine target the spell, are governed by CR 608.2b, and keep the strict `CopySpellForEffect`. **Closed since (#1288 and #1340, the later 2026-09-23 amendments to [ADR 0043](decisions/0043-copy-effects.md) and [ADR 0086](decisions/0086-storm-and-the-turns-cast-order.md)):** Breeches, the Blastmaker's reflexive "copy that spell" opted in (`FromLastKnown`) — the Bolt countered after the flip is won still gets its copy. And the same choke point now ends a COPY that leaves the stack without resolving (#1340, CR 707.10a / 704.5e): a countered, bounced or exiled Twincast or storm copy ceases to exist instead of landing as a card, a countered one still announcing `EventCounterSpell`, and its last-known record is taken first.

- **"Your life total can't change", and a player-scoped replacement with a CR 611.2 duration** (#1200, CR 119.7 / CR 119.8 / CR 120.3a, [ADR 0085](decisions/0085-life-total-cant-change.md)) — the seam had no row of its own either, only a mention on the two rows below, and it is Teferi's Protection's LAST caveat. #1200 filed it as a REPLACEMENT-registry problem: put a `Duration` on `ReplacementEffect`, sweep `Game.TurnScopedReplacements` through `durationExpiredLocked` instead of clearing wholesale, and register the clause there. **The ADR declines that and puts the clause on the player instead**, and the reason is the census: `TurnScopedReplacements` holds two CLOSURES per entry, is counted in `ContinuationCensus.TurnScopedReplacements` and is classified `dropped` by the snapshot drift test — so the one card famous for buying you a whole turn cycle would also have been the one card that takes undo and persistence away from the table for that turn cycle. [ADR 0063](decisions/0063-durations-and-control.md) Decision 8's deferral therefore still stands: the registry has no `Duration` field, because the card said to need one does not. **`PlayerStatic.LifeTotalLocked` is the home** — a third payload beside `Keyword` (#1197) and `Timing` (#1195) on one slice with one sweep, one `durationExpiredLocked` read, one clone and one snapshot field, told apart by its payload rather than a discriminator, and `carried` rather than censused because it is plain data. **Two homes, ADR 0072's two:** DERIVED from the battlefield (`effects.Spec.PlayerLifeTotalLocked` → `game.CatalogPlayerLifeTotalLocked`, read through `CatalogAbilityKey` on every query so two Platinum Emperions compose and a layer-6 ability strip unlocks) and GRANTED with a CR 611.2 duration (`GrantLifeTotalLockForEffect`, because Teferi's Protection is in exile a moment after it resolves). **FOUR consumers and TWO of them were already written and unreachable:** the built-in `lifeTotalCantChangeReplacement` on the CR 614 life window (`game/life_lock.go`, `Preemptive` for the reasons ADR 0072 §4 gives one event kind over — an amount replacement's rewrite is cancelled either way, a charged shield must not spend itself, and a life PAYMENT cannot pause to be asked); `changeLifeThroughReplacementsLocked`'s CR 614.10 cancel branch, whose comment had named *"your life total can't change"* since #482; `payLifeAsCostLocked`'s CR 119.8 refusal of a cancelled payment, whose comment ended *"a card that adds one should add that check to the cost validators it reaches"*; and the DAMAGE half, four lines in `applyResolvedDamageToPlayerLocked`, because damage does not fire the life window at all (CR 120.3) — the damage is still **dealt**, `EventDealDamage` still fires, the CR 903.10a commander tally still accrues and lifelink still credits, and only the life loss does not happen. **`CanPayLifeLocked` is that check**, one predicate replacing the open-coded `p.Life < n` at eight validators (`activated.go`, `mutations.go`, `alternative_cost.go` ×2, `phyrexian_mana.go`, `entry_choice.go` ×2, `legal/abilities.go` ×2), so the bot never proposes a cost the engine refuses and the client never shows a button that can only fail — #695's argument for CR 119.4, extended to CR 119.8 and CR 614.17b. `AlternativeCost.LifePayableBy` is deleted rather than kept beside it: two spellings of one affordability rule is the drift the predicate exists to stop. Poison and every other counter on a player are untouched, because a counter is its own CR 614 window (ADR 0056). **The wire** gets `PlayerView.life_total_locked`, a bool beside `keywords` rather than a token inside it — that field is bare ability TOKENS and a lock is not an ability the player has — and the client renders a `LIFE` badge in #979's language beside the hexproof and protection ones. **3 cards `full`:** Platinum Emperion (the derived half, off batch #453), Teferi's Reproach (the granted half aimed at an OPPONENT, with the duration counted on the target's seat-turn counter), and **Teferi's Protection, which goes from one caveat to none** — three printed clauses, three sprints, three ADRs (#1197 / [ADR 0072 amendment](decisions/0072-protection.md), #1199 / [ADR 0084](decisions/0084-phasing.md), #1200 / [ADR 0085](decisions/0085-life-total-cant-change.md)). **Still open and a seam of its own:** "you can't lose the game" (CR 104.3, Platinum Angel and Phyrexian Unlife) — a locked total cannot reach 0, but CR 104.3's other three routes are untouched and nothing here gates the SBA loop; see the "Win the game by effect" row above. Trackers [#882](https://github.com/krakenhavoc/cmd_and_ctrl/issues/882) and [#883](https://github.com/krakenhavoc/cmd_and_ctrl/issues/883).

- **Phasing** (#1199, CR 702.26 / CR 502.1, [ADR 0084](decisions/0084-phasing.md)) — the seam had no row of its own, only a mention in the player-protection entry below and one comment in the tree (`game/activation_tally.go`) saying phasing was not modelled at all. It is Teferi’s Protection’s larger half and the half the card is famous for. **The rule is one sentence and the surface is 125 walks.** CR 702.26b — *"a phased-out permanent is treated as though it does not exist. It can’t affect or be affected by anything else in the game"* — is a statement about every read of the battlefield, and this engine open-codes 125 of them in `internal/game`, `internal/legal`, `internal/protocol` and `internal/aiseat`, plus 189 in the card catalog and 144 callers of eight near-identical single-card lookups. #1199 proposed one predicate at each walk; the ADR takes the same rule and makes it structural instead, because a walk that forgets to ask is a silent rules bug (a phased-out creature that still blocks, a Wrath that still kills it) that no test can notice. **A phased-out permanent leaves the battlefield SLICE**, into `Game.PhasedOut` — a `*Zone` for the plumbing (`cloneZone`, `snapshotZone` / `restoreZone`, `viewOfZone` all work on it unchanged) and explicitly NOT a zone in the CR 400 sense, which `findCardZoneLocked` enforces by not looking there. Targeting, the layer pass, the trigger harvester, the SBA sweep, combat, the autotapper, the legal-move enumerator, the view builder and every bot heuristic are then correct with **no edit and no knowledge of phasing**; `leave_game.go`’s CR 800.4a sweep makes the same argument for the same reason. **It is still not a zone change** (CR 702.26d), and the code is written so that this is checkable: `phaseOutLocked` / `phaseInLocked` call neither `MoveCard` nor `battlefieldExitLocked`, emit no `EventZoneMove` / `EventLTB` / `EventETB`, and leave `Card.ObjectEpoch` alone — the promise `activation_tally.go` has been making since S38, which is what keeps an exhaust ability spent across a phase cycle where a flicker refreshes it. `MoveCard`’s battlefield-exit block is the negative image of the rule: every field it clears is one CR 702.26d says to keep. **Three fields and a rider on `Card`**, each answering a question the rules ask: `PhasedOutBy` (CR 702.26a’s *"the active player controlled when they phased out"*, which is not `Controller` — CR 702.26f lets a control-changing effect expire while the permanent is away), `PhasedOutIndirect` (CR 702.26g/h, decided once per batch by the transitive attachment walk so a permanent named AND attached phases out indirectly), `PhaseInLockedBy` (the *"phases out until ~ leaves the battlefield"* family — Oubliette, Out of Time — released by a sweep at the top of `stateBasedActionsLocked`, not by an untap step) and `TapOnPhaseIn` (Oubliette’s rider). **CR 502.1 is one statement** at the top of `performUntapStepLocked`, ahead of the CR 302.6 sickness clear and of CR 502.3’s determination, and inside that function rather than in the step-entry arm for the reason [ADR 0070](decisions/0070-untap-step-choices.md) gives about `consumeUntapSkipsLocked`: everything ahead of the pause runs exactly once. **`phasing` joins `canonicalKeywords`**, read off `Effective().Abilities`, so a granted phasing (Shimmer, an Aura) works like a printed one and the ~70 printed-phasing permanents of the Mirage/Visions cycle are correct with **no catalog entry at all**. **The wire** gets `GameView.phased_out`, a shared `ZoneView` beside `battlefield` / `stack` / `exile`, deliberately not merged into the battlefield: the positional stamps in `view.go` index against `g.Battlefield.Cards`, and `internal/aiseat` reads the view rather than the game. The client folds it back onto the controller’s row, dimmed and badged `PHASED`, with the click withheld. **4 cards** `full`: Vodalian Illusionist, Reality Ripple, Clever Concealment, Oubliette. **Teferi’s Protection** goes from two caveats to one. **Closed since, and exactly where the ADR’s closing section said it would be**: *"your life total can’t change"* (#1200, [ADR 0085](decisions/0085-life-total-cant-change.md)) went on `Player.Statics` and was consumed by the one CR 614 life window, so the duration on `TurnScopedReplacements` was never built — see the row at the top of this section. Tracker [#889](https://github.com/krakenhavoc/cmd_and_ctrl/issues/889).

- **Triggered and static abilities on a token that is not a copy** (#1248, CR 111.1 / CR 111.7 / CR 707.2 / CR 603.10, [ADR 0083](decisions/0083-token-abilities.md)) — half the row's premise had already shipped and the row did not say so. **#521 gave a token a catalog identity** — `game.TokenKey`, `CatalogKey`'s fallback to `Card.TokenKey`, `PrintedValues.TokenKey` in the copiable values — so `TriggersForCard`, `StaticAbilitiesForCard` and `TriggersForKey` had been ABLE to answer for a token ever since; they answered with nothing because nothing could put anything in the slot. What was missing was the DECLARATION, and not by oversight: `token_catalog.go`'s builder returned a `game.Card`, and `game.Card` has no `Triggered` or `Static` field because a closure on the instance is exactly what #521 deleted (one live Treasure used to mean no restore point was written at all, ADR 0041). So the builder returns an `effects.tokenTemplate` instead: a slug, the printed characteristics, four ability slots side by side — `Mana`, `Activated`, `Triggered`, `Static`, written with the SAME `triggers_common.go` constructors and `game.StaticAbility` values printed cards use — and the printed text. Registered once at boot under `game.TokenKey(slug)` in the same `defs` map cards are filed in (never `registry`, so `All()` keeps counting cards). **No new `game.Card` field and no new dispatch path**: the instance still carries one string, so a board of ability-bearing tokens is still a restore point, and the harvest, the layer pass, the LTB harvest and the activation path all found the entry the moment it had something in it. ADR 0071's `ActiveWhen` gate and CR 613.1f ability removal ride along for free, because both live inside the shared accessor. **The registry is a LIST and the slug lives on the template**, which is not taste: a token template may name another token (Fable's Goblin Shaman makes a Treasure), and a slug-keyed map makes the package variable's initialiser reach back into itself — an initialization cycle Go refuses to compile. **Nine battlefield walks** stopped asking `c.OracleID == ""` and started asking whether the catalog KEY is empty (`block_rules.go`, `cast_timing.go`, `cast_permission.go` ×2, `land_drops.go`, `library_top.go`, `player_statics.go`, `effect_hooks.go`, `keywords.go`, plus `characteristic.go`'s printed-keyword read) — behaviour-neutral today, and the difference between a slot that answers for tokens and one that silently does not; an AST scan fails on the tenth. **The dies path** needed one field: `triggerIdentityLKI.TokenKey`, because restoring the CR 603.10 identity snapshot used to BLANK a dying token's key. CR 111.7 then CR 704.5d were already in the right order — the token reaches the graveyard, the harvest reads it there, the sweep runs last. **CR 707.2 needed nothing** and is asserted in both directions. **On the wire**: `CardDef.TokenText` → `game.TokenTextForCard` → `CardView.token_text` → `Card.svelte`'s name-fallback branch, required of every registered template at boot — a mana or activated ability reaches the player as a menu row, a TRIGGER has no control to hang text off, and a token has no printing to fetch oracle text from (ADR 0078 is still Proposed). **Cards:** Reef Worm (#459), Nesting Dragon (#397) and Mysidian Elder (#448) ship; Fable of the Mirror-Breaker (#343), Avatar Roku (#343), Beledros Witherbloom, Sedgemoor Witch and Cornered by Black Mages drop the caveat that named this row. Still open, each filed where it belongs: **Avatar Kuruk's Spirit token** ("can't block or be blocked by non-Spirit creatures" is a `game.BlockRule.Pair`, and `CardDef` has no `BlockRules` slot — ADR 0045's addendum PR 4, on the conditional-blocking row; its `OracleID` skip is fixed here, its Spec field is not); **double-faced tokens** (the transform row); **token art** (#1115 / ADR 0078); and Chocobo Racetrack (#394) and Gwaihir (#464), each now one template away and belonging to their own batch PRs.

- **Mana-production replacement event** (#1222, CR 106.12b, [ADR 0013](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md) §5ab, [ADR 0061 note 2026-09-22](decisions/0061-token-creation-and-discard-are-replaceable-events.md)) — *"If you tap a permanent for mana, it produces twice as much of that mana instead."* The mana PIPELINE was never the gap: since #763 every mana in the game arrives through one of four sites, each already knowing the colour, the restrictions and the player. What there was no event for is the AMOUNT, and ADR 0013 §13 is where it was last looked at — S17 sub-PR 6 named `RepEventManaProduced` a "seventh integration point outside the core scope" and deferred it. **`RepEventProduceMana`** is opened once per production, before any of it is in the pool, on the shape ADR 0061 decision 1 built for a token creation and #1003 reused for `RepEventMill`. It carries **COLOURS rather than a count** (`ManaColors []string`, one entry per mana) because the cards say "twice as much of THAT mana" — a doubled Forest is {G}{G}, a doubled Sol Ring {C}{C}{C}{C} — with `MultiplyMana(n)` as the whole arithmetic, a method rather than a card-side loop so a card cannot quietly rewrite the COLOURS, which CR 106.12b does not license. It also carries **`ManaFromTap`**, the printed condition (CR 106.12a) and the same bit `PendingChoice.ManaTapped` carries for ADR 0074 §3's triggers: a spell's "Add {B}{B}{B}", an untapped mana ability and a triggered mana ability's own output (Wild Growth's extra {G}, which comes from the Aura) are productions and none of them is a tap. **One body, four sites**: `produceManaLocked` (`game/produce_mana.go`) is now the only place in the engine that mints a `ManaToken` and emits `EventManaAdded` — `ActivateManaAbility`'s printed slot, `ResolveManaChoice`'s answered PICK, `materializePlanLocked`'s own taps, and `addManaSlotsLocked` for a spell and for #763's triggers. The pick is the interesting one: a pipe slot has no settled colour at the activation, so the window opens when the pick is ANSWERED — the only moment a Birds of Paradise's colour exists, which is ADR 0074 §3's reason for firing the triggers there — and the consequence is what the card wants, a doubled Birds being ONE pick minting two of the chosen colour and therefore unable to pay {W}{U}. **It can never pause**, and that is a rule rather than a cost argument: CR 605.3b makes activating a mana ability one indivisible step with no priority window inside it, and the auto-tapper's contract is "no further player decisions", so every event sets `mustSettleNow` and the CR 616.1 ordering is applied in gather order — unobservable for both printed cards, since ×2 then ×3 is ×6 either way. That buys **no tail**: the three switches naming what a kind owes on a pause, a cancellation or a dropped prompt all say "nothing, and here is why". **The auto-tap PLANNER prices through the same predicate** (`producedManaPreviewLocked` walks the same gathered `AppliesTo`/`Replace` pairs against a scratch event, read-only with its own applied set, because `gatherTapSources` runs under a read lock): a Mana-Reflected land pays {G}{G} on its own, and `priceProducedSlotsLocked` says so in the grammar the planner already has — a doubled single-option slot becomes two slots, a doubled multi-option slot becomes #779's "N mana of any one color" pick. Both halves are gated on `producesManaReplacementsExistLocked`, one battlefield walk, because on ~every board there is no such card. **Cards: Mana Reflection and Nyxbloom Ancient**, both `full`, one clause each (`cards/effects/mana_replacements.go`). **Still open**: a replacement that rewrites the COLOURS rather than the amount (Mycosynth Lattice's "no land's mana ability adds non-colorless"), which decision 1 deliberately does not offer the catalog; and Zhur-Taa Ancient, which reads like this family and is not — "whenever a player taps a land for mana, that player adds one mana of any type that land produced" is a TRIGGERED mana ability (ADR 0074), not a replacement.

- **Search to exile, a non-owner searcher, and a stack-exit sibling for "return to hand"** (#1230, CR 614 / CR 903.9 / CR 701.6) — closes the **Search with an exile destination** row and the **Counter-to-hand / counter-to-zone effect surface** row whole, and half of the **Search / library-interaction primitives** row. Three small parameters, no new pipeline. (1) `game.searchDestZoneLocked` gained a `ZoneExile` case, so a search's take routes through the EXISTING `searchRoute` / `routeCardToZoneLocked` exit exactly as a hand or graveyard destination does — that exit already resolved `ZoneExile`, only the pre-check didn't. `SearchLibrarySpec.Unbounded` is the "any number of … cards" spelling `Limit`'s zero value couldn't carry (`<= 0` already meant "default to 1"), and `SearchLibrarySpec.FaceDown` rides a `FaceDownKind` through the same route in case a future card says "exile it face down" (unused today). **Ugin, Eye of the Storms**'s −11 is the proof, `full` now. (2) `SearchLibrarySpec.LibraryOwner` names the seat whose library is actually scanned, when that differs from `Player` (the chooser) — Bribery's "search TARGET OPPONENT's library and put that card onto the battlefield under YOUR control" needed exactly one new field, because every other reader of the spec (chooser, entering controller, stamped Actor) already meant `Player` and was already right for a control-changing entry. **Bribery** ships `full`; **Aven Mindcensor**, the row's other card, stays open below — its "search the top four instead" is a static REPLACEMENT over every search a restricted player makes, board-wide, which is a new mechanism (a search-replacement hook) rather than a parameter any one search call can carry. (3) `counterSpellLocked`'s body is now `exitSpellFromStackLocked(spellID, dst, fallback, countered)`, shared by the counter (`countered: true`, `CounterTargetToZoneForEffect` is the new card-effect wrapper exposing the destination the private function always had) and by the new `ReturnSpellToHandForEffect` (`countered: false`, fallback `ZoneHand`) — CR 701.6 never applies to the second one, so a spell printed "can't be countered" is still bounced and no "whenever a spell is countered" trigger fires, while both share the S29 flashback override and the CR 614 / CR 903.9 window through `routeCardToZoneLocked`. **Reprieve** and **Narset's Reversal** (the row's two cards — read closely, neither says "counter") are the proof for the bounce half; **Devious Cover-Up**'s exile clause, caveated since it shipped with a note naming this exact gap, is the proof for the destination-parameter half, and is `full` now too.

- **The activation gate, the chosen card name, and the opponent-activation watch** (#1210, CR 602.5 / CR 101.2 / CR 614.12, [ADR 0073 amendment 2026-09-22](decisions/0073-optional-additional-costs-and-the-cast-gate.md), [ADR 0018 note 2026-09-22](decisions/0018-triggers-on-the-stack.md)) — closes the **"Can't be activated" activation gate** row (count 3) and the **"Whenever an opponent activates an ability" watch** row (count 2), and re-triages the **Ability-suppression static** row above, whose stated primitive had already shipped. ONE function, `Game.ActivationGateLocked(activator, card, zone, ability)`, a field-for-field twin of #760's `CastGateLocked` and written as a twin rather than a widening — a cast has a spell, a source zone, a face and an announcement, an activation has a source OBJECT that stays where it is, an ability INDEX inside it and a CR 605.1a mana/non-mana split no cast has, and one merged query would have made every field optional. `ActivationRestriction` is contributed by a battlefield permanent through `CatalogAbilityKey` in the `CastRestriction` / `CostModifier` shape — so CR 613.1f silences a restrictor, [ADR 0071](decisions/0071-designations-that-switch-abilities-on.md)'s designation gate switches one off, and nothing is stored, which IS the duration. A refusal is a `*CantActivateError` wrapping the EXISTING `ErrCantActivate`, so every `errors.Is` in the tree still matched and no caller changed. **`ActivationAbility.Mana` rides the QUERY and not the call site**, which is the decision the whole design turns on: Pithing Needle prints "unless they're mana abilities" and Cursed Totem does not, so the exemption belongs to the restriction — a call site that decided it would have made Cursed Totem unwritable without a second gate. **FOUR callers**: `ActivateCatalogAbility` (after the CR 113.6 zone check, before timing, X, targets and every cost — a refused activation pays nothing), `ActivateManaAbility`, `legal.abilityMovesForSource` / `legal.manaMoves` behind a board-wide fast negative (#544), and — the one the cast gate has no equivalent of — **`gatherTapSources`**, because the auto-tapper never goes through `ActivateManaAbility` at all (`materializePlanLocked` taps the permanent and mints its mana directly), so without it the plan SUCCEEDS and produces mana the rule forbids. `ProducibleManaLocked` deliberately does NOT ask: CR 106.7 is about what an ability would do IF IT RESOLVED, and #1183's exhaust narrowing stays the one declared exception. The view stamp is `cant_activate` on the ABILITY ROW (`ActivatedAbilityView` / `ManaAbilityView`), carrying the printed clause the way `cant_cast` does and sitting beside `condition_unmet` and `exhausted` rather than merging with either — the three recover differently and the client says so. `CardView.Restrictions`' existing `cant_activate` TOKEN is the per-permanent Arrest bit and is untouched. **`Card.ChosenName`** is the as-enters family's fourth member (after S26's creature type, #742's colour and #980/#1007's player) and the piece ADR 0073 §7 named as missing in its own file. The one way it differs from its three siblings: **there is no vocabulary**. CR 201.2 lets a player name ANY card name, so `ResolveCardNameChoice` checks the SHAPE (trimmed, non-empty, `MaxChosenNameLen`) and nothing else, and the wire's `name_options` is a SUGGESTION list rebuilt from PUBLIC zones only — a convenience for the picker, never a legal set, and never a hidden-information leak. `CardNameMatches` is the one comparison and asks every FACE (CR 201.2b), because `Card.Name` is the ACTIVE face's name and a direct comparison would stop matching the moment a named permanent transformed. Carried by clone and the snapshot (`chosenName`, `carried` in `snapshot_drift_test.go`), cleared at both CR 400.7 sites, and not copiable (CR 706.2), which falls out of `CopiableValuesOf` never looking there. **Meddling Mage and Nevermore, ADR 0073 §7's own out-of-scope item, are now buildable** and are not written here. **The watch needed no engine change at all**, which is what #1184 claimed when it built `EventActivateAbility` wider than its two cards: `effects.WheneverAnOpponentActivates(label, of, includeMana, build)` is one constructor over that event, with "if it isn't a mana ability" expressed as the ABSENCE of `EventManaAbilityActivated` from `Watches` rather than as a predicate a card file could forget, the source predicate run against the ability's source looked up live, and the activator handed to a `Build` closure the way Ob Nixilis, the Hate-Twisted captures a drawer — so "that player" does not target. **Cards (6, all `full`):** Cursed Totem, Collector Ouphe, Linvala, Keeper of Silence, Pithing Needle, Harsh Mentor and Runic Armasaur. **Still open and named in the ADR:** an activation ban with a DURATION ("activated abilities can't be activated this turn"), which wants #755's registries and slots into `ActivationGateLocked` without changing its signature; split second, which stays outside the gate exactly as it stays outside the cast gate; and a restriction on LOYALTY abilities as a class — **the bool arrived with #1208** (`ActivationAbility.Loyalty`, derived from the loyalty cost component so a catalog entry cannot forget CR 606.3), read by the timing twin below and by nothing in the gate, so a restriction that wants it has the field waiting.

- **Ability-copy, and a trigger that reads the event that fired it** (#1223, CR 707.10 / CR 603.2, [ADR 0043 amendment 2026-09-22](decisions/0043-copy-effects.md), [ADR 0018 amendment 2026-09-22](decisions/0018-triggers-on-the-stack.md)) — two rows, closed together because the first cannot be proved without the second: CR 707.10 copies a triggered ability together with the event that triggered it, and until now that event lived in the `Effect` closure, where a copy reached it only by accident of sharing a func pointer. **The copy is spell_copy.go's prompt with one branch at the end.** `Game.CopyAbilityForEffect(itemID, controller, mayChooseNewTargets)` reuses the CR 707.10c "you may choose new targets for the copy" offer, its frame and its submit gate wholesale — `copySpellFrame` became `copyFrame`, and `createCopyLocked` is the ONE branch, on `item.Kind`: build a card on the stack, or build an item beside the source permanent. Four things are the ability copy's own. **There is no card** — an ability is a `StackMeta` entry alone, so nothing is pushed into a zone and `ceaseToExistLocked` needs no twin (CR 608.2n already ends every ability item). **It was not activated and it did not trigger** (CR 707.10a): no `EventActivateAbility`, no `EventTrigger`, said by what `createAbilityCopyLocked` does not emit rather than by a suppression flag — which is what keeps Rings of Brighthearth from copying its own copy forever and what keeps an "opponent activates an ability" watch from double-counting. **A mana ability cannot be copied** (CR 605.3b) and that falls out of the lookup finding nothing, not out of a check. **The triggering event travels**, which is the second row. What is copied is CR 707.10's "characteristics plus the choices made": targets, modes, X, the division, the reflexive payload, `Effect` and the two specs the CR 608.2b re-check reads — plus the announcement's `PaidCost` record MINUS its mana half, because "for each counter removed this way" is a fact about the announcement while nothing was spent to make a copy (the Dawnglow Infusion ruling). **Targeting an ability on the stack** is the clause ADR 0065 listed as open — generalised to the whole catalog by #1211, whose entry above is now the canonical statement: `TargetSpec.Abilities` + `AbilityOK` enumerate `StackMeta` by `Seq`, and a chosen ability rides an ordinary `TargetRef{Kind: TargetCard}` carrying the STACK ITEM's id rather than a fifth `TargetRefKind` — the wire, the picker, the bot enumerator and the re-check all key on "a uuid that has to still be there", which is as true of an item as of a card. The CR 702 keyword gate is deliberately not applied: an ability is neither a permanent nor a player, so nothing about it can have hexproof. **5 cards:** Strionic Resonator, Lithoform Engine, Rings of Brighthearth, Scrap Trawler `full`, Cloudstone Curio `caveats` (the Whitemane Lion posture — the permanent to return is picked as the trigger goes on the stack rather than at resolution), and Weaver of Harmony's copy activation came off its caveat with it. **Still open, and each a different shape**: Peter Parker's Camera and Gogo, Master of Mimicry (#395, #396), which copy on a different axis again; a multi-slot "change a target" (no printed card); and **power and toughness on the event's object snapshot** — "a creature with power equal to the power of the creature that died" is not answerable yet, because `lastKnownBattlefield` holds a `Characteristic` and the engine applies +1/+1 counters outside it (`Card.PowerForComparison`) while `MoveCard` clears them on the way out, so a `Power` field would silently read 2 for the 3/3 a counter made.

- **Reveal-from-hand entry choice** (#1198, CR 614.1c, [ADR 0013](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md) §5z, [ADR 0018 amendment 2026-09-22](decisions/0018-triggers-on-the-stack.md)) — *"As this land enters, you may reveal an Island or Swamp card from your hand. If you don't, this land enters tapped."* The CR 614 apply-loop could stop and ask three questions before this, and all three are yes/no-shaped: a "may" (`Optional`), a shockland's "you may pay 2 life" (`EntryLifeCost`) and a copy selector's "enter as a copy of what?" (`CopySelector`, which names a permanent on the battlefield). None of them can name a card in a HAND, which is the gap the census filed in batch 02 under its own name — *a card choice inside a replacement effect*. **`ReplacementEffect.EntryHandReveal`** is the fourth branch on that loop and joins `asksItsOwnQuestion`, so the two windows that cannot put a question to anybody (`ev.mustSettleNow`, and a CR 616 ordering whose affected player has left) skip it un-applied — the weaker branch, the posture every un-prompted path in ADR 0013 already takes. Its three apply-it-inline guards are `EntryLifeCost`'s one for one: **no resume** (`ev.entryResumable` — pausing an entry with nothing to finish it strands the card in its old zone), **nothing in hand the clause admits** (the ABSENCE of the question, not a refusal of it), and **a chooser who has left** (CR 800.4a). **`PendingChoiceEntryRevealFromHand`** is one new prompt kind and the THIRD member of `isCardSetPickKind` after `choose_cards` and #826's `untap_choice`: it carries the choose-cards payload and a `chooseCardsFrame{zone: ZoneHand}`, so `checkChooseCardsPicksLocked` (#1017) stays the single copy of "would this answer be accepted" for the submit path and for `ChooseCardsPickLegalLocked` in the enumerator. It is a separate KIND because its continuation is a PAUSED EVENT rather than a card's next sentence (it settles through `finishSettledReplacementLocked`, the replacement pipeline's resume contract), because the sentence the client renders is different, and because the SIGN is inverted for a bot — the `choose_cards` branch scores an answer by what it does NOT name (#798), and a card named here is revealed and KEPT, so through that branch every reveal-land would enter tapped forever. **Declining is a real answer**: the floor is zero, which makes "reveal nothing" the enumerator's `AlwaysLegal` move and this prompt un-wedgeable even with every candidate gone from the hand — the property that also keeps it deliberately out of `pruneCardSetChoicesLocked`, whose drop would strand the entry it is pausing. **The reveal itself is `RevealForEffect`**: nothing moves (CR 701.20b — the card is still in hand when the land finishes entering), the whole table becomes entitled to read it (CR 701.20), and the other seats learn what was shown through the log, because the picker and its bounds reach the chooser alone (`choose_cards`' redaction, since how many cards in a hand match the clause is itself hidden information). **Card side is one clause and no per-card prompt code**: `EntersTappedUnlessYouRevealFromHand(name, clause, matches)`, beside `EntersTappedUnlessYouPayLife` and `SelfEntersTappedUnless`, with `Matches` a `func(game.Card) bool` over a card in hand (printed characteristics — a land-type grant on the battlefield says nothing about a card being revealed) and a `Then` continuation for a card that wants to do more. **Cards: the whole ten-card cycle**, one file and one row each — Choked Estuary, Foreboding Ruins, Port Town, Fortified Village, Frostboil Snarl, Furycalm Snarl, Game Trail, Shineshadow Snarl, Necroblossom Snarl and Vineglimmer Snarl, each shipped with a caveat for the same reason the shocklands did — a reveal-land a spell PUTS onto the battlefield out of a hand or library entered tapped, because that batch was built without `entryResumable` — and each complete since #1322 made the batch resumable. **Still open and each a different shape**: Temple of the Dragon Queen (#386) prints *"unless you revealed a Dragon card this way **or you control a Dragon**"* AND a second CR 614.12 clause (choose a colour) — two clauses on one entry; **exiling** from hand as a permanent enters (Chrome Mox's imprint is an ETB TRIGGER, not CR 614.12, and ships complete as one — #578's distinction, observable because a Mox flickered in response to its own trigger imprints nothing); and **discarding** inside an entry replacement (Mox Diamond), which is a cost and has its own row above.

- **Three cost components: return a permanent to hand, a sacrifice count the activator announces, and a discard on a mana ability** (#1213, CR 118.3 / CR 601.2h / CR 602.2b, [ADR 0073 amendment 2026-09-22](decisions/0073-optional-additional-costs-and-the-cast-gate.md)) — three rows, one vocabulary. Each component is ONE struct with ONE options walk, ONE payability predicate, ONE validator and ONE payer, shared by the engine, the protocol view's picker and the legal-move enumerator, which is the #544 invariant `counter_cost.go` and `tap_others_cost.go` already keep. **`game.ReturnToHandCost`** (`return_cost.go`, `AbilityCost.ReturnToHand`, wire `return_ids`) is `TapOthersCost` one verb over — `Count`, a `TargetSpec` filter, `ExcludeSource` for the printed word "another" — and it is deliberately neither `SacrificeOther` with a destination (a sacrifice emits `EventSacrifice` and feeds Blood Artist; a return does not) nor `BounceToHandForEffect` (that is the effect verb; paying a cost is not an effect). It pays through the one battlefield-exit door with **`zoneRoute.MustSettleNow`**, the same bit the cost discard sets and for the same reason: CR 601.2h pays an announcement's costs as one indivisible step, so a commander returned as a cost takes its owner's hand without opening the CR 903.9 prompt. Paid with the sacrifices, before the stack item is built, so a leaves-the-battlefield trigger sits ABOVE the ability (CR 603.3b); the source is a legal pick when the filter admits it (Master Transmuter returning herself), so the activation path drops its `source` handle afterwards exactly as it does after a sacrifice. **The variable sacrifice count** is an ANNOUNCEMENT rather than a clause shape: `SacrificeCostBounds(spec, x)` reads the two printed forms — an open count (`Min` ≥ 1, no ceiling — "sacrifice one or more artifacts") and `CountFromX` ("Sacrifice X Treasures") — and `SacrificeCountLegal` is the one predicate the validator, the view's picker bounds and the enumerator's payment walk all read. `CountFromX` makes `AbilityCost.DemandsX()` true even with no `{X}` in the mana component, which is the one sentence of `activated.go` this rewrote; `XSlots()` is untouched, so Grim Hireling's `{B}` is `{B}` at every X, and `Register` refuses a cost that puts `{X}` in BOTH places. **`PaidCost.Sacrificed`** records how many the announcement actually paid, for `CountersRemoved`'s reason (#789): by resolution the permanents are in graveyards and nothing on the board could count them; `ctx.Sacrificed()` is the reader. `effects.Register`'s #747 guard narrowed rather than disappeared — still refused are a floor below one, a ceiling below the floor, `AllowSame`, `Players`, and `CountFromX` where there is no X to announce (a mana ability, CR 605.3b; a cast, whose flat payment lists need a fixed width). **`effects.ManaAbilityCost.DiscardCards`** is #660's component with a second owner, like `SacrificeOther`, `RemoveCounters` and `TapOthers` before it: `discardCardsLocked` with `DiscardCauseCost`, so `EventDiscardCard` fires once per card, the CR 614 window runs over the exit and madness (CR 702.35a) sees it without learning that mana abilities exist. The **auto-tapper never plans one** (`autoTapAbilityFor`), on the ground the life cost and the tap-others cost already fail: which card to pitch is a decision, and the planner makes none. Bot enumeration is capped at `legal.maxEnumeratedVariableCounts` (3) counts per variable cost, documented in [docs/bot.md](bot.md) beside `maxEnumeratedCostPayments`, with each payment ordered by the seat's own `OrderCostFuel` price so the counts nest. **7 cards, all `full`:** Meloku the Clouded Mirror, Master Transmuter, Quirion Ranger and Wirewood Symbiote (the return cost); Radiant Lotus and Grim Hireling (the two variable-count forms); Skirge Familiar (the mana-ability discard). **Still open, and on their own rows:** Mox Diamond's entry-replacement discard — a PROMPT seam rather than a cost component, because an entry replacement has no announcement to pay at, and #1198's `EntryHandReveal` is the branch it wants with the card moving instead of being revealed. One card, so it is noted on batch issue #295 rather than given a row of its own; last-known information for an effect that reads the sacrificed permanents themselves ("the sacrificed creature's power"), which no card on the row asked for; and a VARIABLE tap-others count (Secluded Starforge, Apothecary White), which #758 stated out of scope and which should copy `SacrificeCostBounds` when it lands.

- **Per-source activations-this-turn count** (#1213, closing what #1181 and #1183 built) — the row said `TurnTally` counts what a source's abilities RESOLVED or TRIGGERED this turn and nothing counts what was ACTIVATED. #1181's `Game.Activations` built the count for exhaust and gave it a per-turn scope with no reader; #1183 made mana abilities write it too. What was left was one `Condition` per card plus the return-to-hand cost both named cards also waited on, and both landed here. `effects.OncePerTurnActivation` now reads **`Game.ActivatedThisTurn(source, label)`** instead of `ResolvedThisTurn`, which is a correctness fix as well as an unblocking: CR 602.1b counts announcements, and the two tallies disagree in both directions on a printed line — two activations held on the stack at once both resolve later, so a resolution count let the second be announced (stronger than printed, the #259 direction), and an activation countered or fizzled never resolves, so a resolution count handed the player a second use of an ability they had already spent. Beledros Witherbloom, the one existing caller, is fixed by the same line. **2 cards:** Quirion Ranger and Wirewood Symbiote, both `full`. **Still ordinary card work rather than a seam:** boast (CR 702.142a, Broadside Bombardiers) is this condition plus "only if this creature attacked this turn", which `TurnTally` already answers.

- **Player protection and player hexproof** (#1197, CR 702.16i / CR 702.11d, [ADR 0072 amendment 2026-09-22](decisions/0072-protection.md)) — the row said it in one sentence: `game.Player` carried no ability slice, so "you have protection from everything" had nowhere to live and no consumer. **The grammar did not change.** A player's protection is the same token a permanent's is, parsed by the same closed-grammar reader in `protection.go` and matched against the same `Characteristic` snapshot; what is new is a second kind of thing that can HAVE a quality. The slot is **two halves, and only one is stored**: a permanent's printed static ("You have hexproof" — Leyline of Sanctity, Aegis of the Gods) is `effects.Spec.PlayerKeywords`, read off the battlefield through `game.CatalogPlayerKeywords` on every query and written nowhere, so two Leylines compose and one of them leaving cannot revoke the other's grant — the argument `CatalogNoMaxHandSize` (#338) and `land_drops.go` both make at length; a resolved spell's grant ("you gain protection from everything until your next turn" — Teferi's Protection, The One Ring) is `Player.Statics`, modelled on `Player.CastPermissions` to the letter (plain data with no closure, a CR 611.2 `Duration`, swept through the same `durationExpiredLocked`, cloned by value, mirrored into the snapshot, classified `carried`), because its source is in a graveyard a moment after it resolves. **Not a `ScopedStatic`**, and the reason is the layer engine: that registry is adapted into a `ContinuousEffect` whose `Apply` takes a `*Characteristic` and a `*Card`, and a player is neither — the same argument `Spec.NoMaxHandSize` and `Spec.CostModifiers` already make for themselves. **Three consumers, one predicate each, and every one of them was a branch that already existed and returned early**: targeting (`canPlayerBeTargetedByLocked` at both `TargetPlayer` sites in `targets.go`, on the existing `targeting` switch, so a cost payment that names a player is still not targeting); damage (the CR 702.16e built-in's non-permanent branch in `builtin_replacements.go`, which used to say "player protection has no home yet" — one branch covers combat AND noncombat, because every damage path routes through `damageThroughReplacementsLocked`); attachment (`attachmentLegalLocked`'s `TargetPlayer` host, for an "enchant player" Aura — Curse of Opulence is the card that proves that branch exists). The bot's enumerator and the client's `legal_targets` follow **by construction**: both read `legalTargetsLocked`, so neither learns the rule and neither can disagree with it. Hexproof keeps CR 702.11b's asymmetry verbatim — an OPPONENT's spell is refused, your own still reaches you, which Leyline of Sanctity needs to be playable at all; shroud on a player is absent because no card prints it. `PlayerView.keywords` puts a seat's tokens on the wire, public like emblems and effective like `max_hand_size`. **4 cards:** Leyline of Sanctity (`caveats` — the opening-hand clause is the Gemstone Caverns gap), Aegis of the Gods (`full`), Teferi's Protection (`caveats` — see below) and The One Ring, which had carried a caveat naming this exact gap since S40 and is now `full`, its "if you cast it" read off the event log the way Zacama and Tiamat read theirs. **Still open, and each a seam of its own rather than this one**: **phasing** (CR 702.26 — was "not modelled anywhere in this engine", Teferi's Protection's larger half and its first caveat; **closed by #1199 / [ADR 0084](decisions/0084-phasing.md)**, row above); **"your life total can't change"** (**closed by #1200 / [ADR 0085](decisions/0085-life-total-cant-change.md)**, row at the top of this section — it became a third payload on this row's own `Player.Statics`, not the duration on `TurnScopedReplacements` the issue proposed, and Teferi's Protection is `full`); **protection on a player FROM a player** (reachable, since the quality kind and the reader are shared, but no catalogued card prints it); and the **client badge** for a protected or hexproof seat, which is a client-only change now that the wire carries it (#1201).

- **Three resolution-time prompt kinds** (#1214, CR 608.2 / CR 701.20, [ADR 0018 amendment 2026-09-22](decisions/0018-triggers-on-the-stack.md), [ADR 0060 amendment 2026-09-22](decisions/0060-leaving-the-game.md)) — three rows closed together because they were one shape: a decision a card asks for *while it is resolving*, answered by a named seat, with the rest of the card hanging off the answer. **`reveal_pick`** — an opponent picks from a set you revealed (Gifts Ungiven, Intuition; a pile split's first leg is now this kind, which is what it always was). **`their_permanents`** — a seat choosing among the permanents somebody ELSE controls (Tragic Arrogance), the direction the sacrifice run cannot face, since `PendingChoiceSacrifice` is queued `{Chooser: p, FromPlayer: p}` at its one queue site. **`own_permanents`** — "choose N of your own permanents" at resolution, untargeted (Scapeshift's "sacrifice any number of lands"), which the fixed-count sacrifice prompt could not say and could not be read for a total. **ONE PAYLOAD:** all three carry the choose-cards fields and a `chooseCardsFrame`, so `isCardSetPickKind` admits them and `checkChooseCardsPicksLocked` (bounds, candidates, duplicates, the live-zone re-check, #1017's set-level `Validate`), `ChooseCardsPickLegalLocked`, `setCardSetCandidates` and both prunes are written once; `reassignChoiceLocked`'s candidate prune became one `isCardSetPickKind` arm in the same pass. **THREE KINDS** because each difference is observable: what a non-chooser may see (a reveal is PUBLIC, where `filterPendingChoices` withholds even a `choose_cards` prompt's bounds), which way a bot should read the answer (#1014's `OrderTargets` for the two that rank somebody else's material, `OrderCostFuel` for the one that spends the seat's own), and who inherits it under CR 800.4g. **ONE CONTINUATION:** every one is a RUN (`runPromptsLocked`, #1019 / #1027), so Tragic Arrogance's one printed instruction asked as one prompt per player settles once — and `dropDefault` reaches `settleRunLegLocked` through `PendingChoice.promptRun`, the branch `defaultDroppedChoiceLocked` already took on the run link rather than on the kind, so the three kinds needed no new code in the departure sweep, the performer or either gate. It also fixed a hole one leg upstream of #1006: a Fact or Fiction splitter who left AFTER the question went up hit a `dropDefault` that found no run and no option frame, ran nothing, and put neither pile anywhere. **Cards:** Intuition, Gifts Ungiven, Scapeshift and Tragic Arrogance all ship `full`; Fact or Fiction moved onto the shared first leg unchanged. **Still open and a second axis rather than a deeper version of this one:** The Legend of Yangchen's chapter I (#343) needs several players choosing from ONE SHARED POOL with each answer narrowing the next, and Gluntch, the Bestower (#391) needs two target players before the pick and a different effect per seat. Neither is blocked on a prompt kind any more; both are card work plus a sequencing shape, and they keep their own notes on their batch issues.

- **Stack-item retarget** (#1196, CR 115.7, [ADR 0019 amendment 2026-09-22](decisions/0019-structured-targeting.md)) — rewriting the targets of a spell or ability that is already on the stack. The row the 2026-09-16 audit singled out as undercounted, and the whole of it is one observation: **retargeting is the announce gate run a second time, for a different chooser.** `Game.RetargetStackItemForEffect(itemID, chooser, policy, next)` takes the WHOLE new target list, one-for-one with the item's current one, and re-runs `validateAnnouncedTargetsLocked` over it with the item's own announced clause list and its own `TargetSource` — so per-clause predicates, zones, `Min`/`Max`, `AllowSame`, `Distinct` and `CanBeTargetedBy` (protection, hexproof, shroud) are all enforced without being restated — including, since #1197 landed a day earlier, the PLAYER half of that gate, so a seat with hexproof is not among the alternatives offered for an opponent's spell and a seat with protection from red is not among a red spell's, with no line of this seam's own. Three rules are the retarget's own: **one-for-one** (CR 115.7 changes which objects are targeted, never how many, and never which clause a slot answers), a **change count** (`RetargetChangeOne` for "change the target", `RetargetChooseNew` for "choose new targets"), and an **unchanged slot's predicate is waived** — CR 115.7c lets a player leave a target alone even if it is now illegal, which is the one new parameter on the announce walk (`validateAnnouncedTargetsWithLocked`'s `waive`). **The chooser is not the controller**: legality is judged from the ITEM's point of view (its controller, its own card as the protection source) while the prompt goes to the retargeting effect's controller, so a Swat on an opponent's "target creature you control" can only move it to a creature THAT OPPONENT controls. `PaidCost`, modes, X, `AltCost` and `CastFromZone` are untouched; `Distribution` is REMAPPED positionally, because it is keyed by target id and CR 115.7c says the division can't be changed. **`PendingChoiceRetarget`** is a prompt kind of its own rather than another `pick_target`, and the departure table is why: `pick_target` is `{reassign: true}` and nobody else aims another player's Deflecting Swat, so `retarget` is `{}` — dropped, targets unchanged. It carries DATA, not a continuation (the object it rewrites is already on the stack), so a game paused on one is a restorable snapshot, which is true of no other prompt in this family. The wire and the client picker reuse `pick_target`'s projection unchanged; `internal/legal` gets its own case, ordered through #1014's `OrderTargets` and offering the decline when `min` is 0. **CR 707.10c moves onto the same check**: `spell_copy.go`'s re-target validated with the ANNOUNCE gate, which refused a target the player left alone that had since become illegal and allowed the count to change — both wrong for a copy, and both now the shared `retargetCheckLocked`. **4 cards:** Deflecting Swat (#294) and Bolt Bend ship `caveats` for the "or ability" half; Misdirection and Imp's Mischief (#298) ship `full`, since both print "target spell". **Still open, and each a different shape**: ~~targeting an ABILITY on the stack~~ — **closed by #1211**, which made Deflecting Swat and Bolt Bend `full` by widening their clause and needed no line of retarget code, because this entry point was written over `StackMeta` from the start; a multi-slot "change *a* target", which would have to ask which slot first and has no printed card; and ~~**Spellskite** (#302), which changes a target TO a named object rather than offering a choice, and does it from an activated ability with a Phyrexian mana cost~~ — **closed by #1743**, which also built the multi-slot "change *a* target" for a fixed destination.

- **Per-player "cast as though it had flash"** (#1195, CR 307.1, CR 702.8, [ADR 0066 amendment 2026-09-22](decisions/0066-granted-cast-and-play-permissions.md)) — the primitive, and its inverse. ADR 0066 had put a timing rule on a PERMISSION (`CastPermission.Timing`), which is right for a grant that opens one cast of one object — madness, suspend's free cast — and wrong for the sentence Vedalken Orrery prints: "you may cast spells as though they had flash" names no card, names no zone and outlives no particular object. **`game.CastTiming`** is that sentence, and it invents no vocabulary: `GrantTiming` is ADR 0066's own enum gaining `TimingYourTurnOnly` (Dosan the Falling Leaf, which is **not** sorcery speed — it leaves every instant-speed window on your own turn open), `PermissionFilter` is the zone filter gaining `NoncreatureOnly` and `SorceryOnly`, and the window is [ADR 0063](decisions/0063-durations-and-control.md)'s `game.Duration` swept by the one `durationExpiredLocked`. **Two homes, ADR 0066's two:** DERIVED from the battlefield through `CatalogAbilityKey` (`Spec.CastTimings` — so an Orrery that has lost its abilities stops granting, two of them compose, and nothing is stored to expire), and STORED on the player (`Player.CastTimings`, `GrantCastTimingForEffect`) for a statement that outlives its source — Emergence Zone sacrifices itself to pay for its own ability, Teferi's +1 resolves and the planeswalker may die before your next turn. A catalog entry cannot name a seat, so `CastTimingAffects` (`you` / `each opponent` / `each player`) is expanded by the derivation. **ONE read** — `game.CastTimingOpenLocked` — folding the card's own timing, the permission's ADR 0066 override, the per-player grants and, LAST, the per-player restrictions, because CR 101.2 says "can't" beats "can": an opponent's Orrery does not get them past your Teferi. Its three callers are ADR 0073 §7's three, and the read sits BESIDE `CastGateLocked` rather than inside it, because "banned" and "not yet" are different answers and `cant_cast` means the first: `CastSpell`, `legal.castMovesFromZone` (which drops the `speed` parameter it threaded through two functions to keep a copy of the rule), and `protocol.castStampsFor`, where `castable_here` gains its third input — a fix in both directions, since a flashback SORCERY in a graveyard used to carry the bit in an opponent's end step where the announce path refuses the cast. A land PLAY keeps its own branch: CR 116.2a makes it a special action, so no timing statement reaches it either way. **Cards:** Vedalken Orrery and Emergence Zone (both `full`, both off this row), Leyline of Anticipation (`caveats`, the opening-hand clause every Leyline in this catalog defers), and **Teferi, Time Raveler goes from `caveats` to `full`** — its static and its +1 were the two homes, and both of its caveats were this seam. **Still open and ordinary card work now**: the other seven on this row each need only a `Spec` — Borne Upon a Wind, High Fae Trickster, Alchemist's Refuge, Liberator (colourless and artifact spells), Shimmer Myr (artifact spells), Gandalf the White (legendary and artifact spells) and Winding Canyons; the colour- and supertype-shaped filters (Yeva's "green creature spells", Gandalf's "legendary spells") are `PermissionFilter` fields nothing has needed yet. **The activated-ability twin was built by #1208** (below), and this row's prediction about it was wrong in both halves: Grand Abolisher's "or activate abilities of artifacts, creatures, or enchantments" is a BAN and landed with #1210's activation gate (the card ships `full`), and the twin wanted ONE home rather than two. What it did share is the shape — one read, folded in CR 101.2 order, with three callers.

- **Per-player ACTIVATION timing** (#1208, CR 602.5d / CR 606.3 / CR 101.1, [ADR 0066 amendment 2026-09-23](decisions/0066-granted-cast-and-play-permissions.md), [ADR 0073 note 2026-09-23](decisions/0073-optional-additional-costs-and-the-cast-gate.md)) — "you may activate [these abilities] any time you could cast an instant". Before this, `ActivatedAbilityShape.SorcerySpeed` and `AbilityCost.Loyalty != nil` were the engine's whole opinion about when an ability may be activated, written out in three places that each kept their own copy of it, and NOTHING could open that window or narrow it. **The cards are the grant, not the restriction**, and that is the one thing the row above got wrong when it predicted this one: every printed restriction on somebody's activations says "can't be activated" (Cursed Totem, Linvala, Collector Ouphe, Pithing Needle, Karn, Grand Abolisher) and goes through #1210's `ActivationGateLocked`, so the eight printed cards on THIS row all print a grant. **`game.ActivationTiming`** is a struct of hooks mirroring `ActivationRestriction` field for field — a printed `Label`, a `Covers func(ActivationQuery) bool`, an `ActiveWhen` designation gate — and it is a SECOND type rather than `CastTimingRule` with a scope field, because a cast statement has a stored home and must be pure data while this one has only a derived home and can carry a predicate. It needs one: what the cards narrow on is the ability's SOURCE ("loyalty abilities of Teferi") and the ABILITY ("equip abilities"), two things a filter over *the object being cast* says neither of. So `ActivationAbility` grew `SorcerySpeed`, `Loyalty` (the bool ADR 0073's #1210 scope note predicted, derived from the loyalty cost component so a catalog entry cannot forget CR 606.3) and `Equip` (the CR 702.6 keyword, NAMED rather than made a kind — `EquipOnlyAbility` and `TestEveryEquipAbilityIsMarked` exist because three cards wrote a narrowed equip out by hand). **ONE HOME, not two:** every card that prints one of these is a permanent whose static says it for as long as it is there, so `Spec.ActivationTimings` is derived through `CatalogAbilityKey` and nothing is stored — no `PlayerStatic` payload, no sweep, no clone, no snapshot field. The Wandering Emperor's "as long as she entered this turn" rides that derivation as `game.EnteredThisTurn` (#1009's entry tally), deliberately NOT `Card.SummonedThisTurn`, which survives until your untap step and would have given a flashed-in Emperor instant-speed loyalty abilities for a whole turn cycle. **ONE read** — `game.ActivationTimingOpenLocked` — folding, in this order: a mana ability (never asked, CR 605.3a gives it its own window, so a card that stops mana abilities is a BAN and belongs to the gate, where #1210 already put the decision on the card), the ability's own clause, the per-player grants, and LAST the restrictions, because CR 101.2 says "can't" beats "can". **A grant DOES reach loyalty abilities**, against what the issue proposed: CR 101.1 lets a card beat a rule, and both planeswalkers here print exactly that sentence — refusing would have made the only two printed users unwritable. **Four callers:** `ActivateCatalogAbility`, `legal.abilityMovesForSource` (which dropped the `speed` parameter it threaded through two functions to keep a copy of the rule), `protocol.viewOfActivatedAbilities`, and `ActivateLoyalty` — the sandbox manual loyalty verb, which `internal/legal` skips by design but which a statement about "planeswalkers you control" reaches on a walker the catalog has never heard of. **The wire grows one field**, where the cast side needed none: `activated_abilities[i].timing_closed`, negative and `omitempty`, because the rows carried only `sorcery_speed` (the PRINTED clause) and `client/src/lib/timing.ts` re-derived CR 307.1 to grey them — its own comment called that "the LAST rules derivation left in this file". The client now takes the BIT from the server and still writes the SENTENCE from the snapshot. **Cards:** Leonin Shikari (`full`) and Teferi, Master of Time (`caveats`, for the −10's extra turns) are new; **The Wandering Emperor goes from `caveats` to `full`** — and her three loyalty abilities were WRONG and are corrected in the same PR (S27 shipped a permuted, half-invented set; the printed +1 puts a +1/+1 counter and grants first strike, the −1 makes the Samurai, the −2 exiles a tapped creature and gains 2 life). ~~**Still open:** the EMBLEM home — Teferi, Temporal Archmage's −10 and Teferi's Talent both make an emblem that says this, which is the derived home one zone over (`Player.Emblems` beside the battlefield, plus a slot on `EmblemSpec`); not built because the one card behind it also needs a library-look prompt that does not exist.~~ — **closed by #1275**, the entry at the top of this list. **Also still open and a different seam:** Forge Anew's `{0}` equip cost (cost modification for activated abilities) and Thousand-Year Elixir's "as though those creatures had haste" (CR 302.6's tap-symbol restriction, not CR 602.5's window).

- **Earthbend, and a delayed trigger keyed to an OBJECT** (#1178, CR 701 keyword actions, [ADR 0081](decisions/0081-earthbend-and-object-keyed-delayed-triggers.md)) — twelve Avatar cards print *"Earthbend N. (Target land you control becomes a 0/0 creature with haste that's still a land. Put N +1/+1 counters on it. When it dies or is exiled, return it to the battlefield tapped.)"*, and #1178 filed it as four gaps. It is **one verb**: `KeywordActionEarthbend` joins proliferate, scry and surveil on `RepEventKeywordAction` ([ADR 0013](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md) §5s), so the printed count rides a CR 614 window and a future "earthbend twice that much instead" is a card-side replacement rather than an engine change. Its count means a **third** thing — proliferate counts TIMES, scry and surveil count CARDS, earthbend counts **+1/+1 counters** — and it is the first action in the family that acts at a count of ZERO (`KeywordAction.actsAtZeroCount`): Rockalanche with no Forests still animates the land, which then dies to CR 704.5f and comes back tapped. The animation is **three** `ScopedStatic`s with **one** CR 613.7 timestamp and **one** duration — layer 4 adds Creature and leaves Land alone, layer **7b** (not 7a; #1178's shorthand was wrong) sets base P/T to 0/0, layer 6 grants haste — the duration being `IndefiniteDuration()` **pinned to the object**, which is CR 611.2a plus CR 400.7 plus garbage collection in one value and what makes "the haste and the animation end together" a fact about the data. The 7b bucket is load-bearing twice over: it REPLACES a printed body (Dryad Arbor earthbent 2 is a 2/2, not a 3/3) and it sets `Characteristic.PTDefined`, without which CR 704.5f skips the 0/0 whole. Counters go through `AddCounterByForEffect` (the PLACEMENT window — Hardened Scales applies; the entry pipeline is deliberately not involved) and go **last**, because the placement is the only part that can pause.
