The return is the new mechanism: a **CR 603.7 delayed trigger whose condition is an EVENT about one object** (`DelayedTrigger.On` + `AppliesTo`, #663, previously only ever watching a cast) — `EventLTB` whose `NewZone` is a graveyard or exile, so a Rest in Peace-style replacement that exiles a dying land still satisfies "dies **or is exiled**"; its ID is DERIVED from `{instance, EnteredBattlefieldAt}` so a second earthbend on the same object queues no second return; and it carries the first non-`UntilEndOfTurn` duration a delayed trigger has had, `Indefinite` pinned to the object so an unsatisfiable entry is swept rather than carried for the game. `Game.ReturnToBattlefieldForEffect(cardID, controller, tapped)` is the router the trigger needs, dispatching on the card's live zone over the two existing doors (each keeps its own CR 400.7 bookkeeping). **Cards:** Earthbending Lesson and Rockalanche ship `full`, and The Legend of Kyoshi's front face goes from `caveats` to `full` (#1179's earthbend caveat deleted). **Still open, and ordinary card work now**: the other nine earthbend cards each wait on something else — **exhaust** (Bitter Work, Ba Sing Se; nothing in the engine has it — filed as #1181, 41 printed cards), a reflexive "when you do" around the verb (Earth Rumble), two target clauses in one sentence (Cracked Earth Technique), per-mode targets (Sandbenders' Storm, Dai Li Indoctrination), and reading the animated land's power afterwards (Earthshape, **shipped with #1282** on `effects.Earthbend.Then` / `game.EarthbendThenForEffect`, which run once the counters have landed, including after a CR 616 counter-order prompt; since #1289 the bare land that is a 0/0 during that prompt survives it, because state-based actions wait for the resolution). **Also still open**: a per-instance return queue, which only matters the day a card can counter a triggered ability.

- **Attack taxes** (#1063, CR 508.1a, [ADR 0080](decisions/0080-attack-taxes.md)) — "creatures can't attack you unless their controller pays {2} for each creature they control that's attacking you". [ADR 0045](decisions/0045-combat-restrictions.md) named this as deliberately outside the restriction vocabulary and predicted the shape it wanted; this is that shape. **`game.AttackTax`** is a per-permanent static on the DEFENDER's side, catalog-contributed like `CostModifier` and gated in one accessor (`AttackTaxesForCard`, through `CatalogAbilityKey`, so a permanent under CR 613.1f stops taxing and the engine and the enumerator cannot price a declaration differently). Its "you" is **structural** — a tax protects its own controller and nobody else, so no card file can typo its way into taxing the table — and the one line cards differ on, "you" versus "you or planeswalkers you control", is `AttackTaxScope` with the NARROWER reading as the zero value, so a card that forgets under-taxes. `ManaCost` returns a cost STRING per attacking creature, because a declaration sums several by concatenation (`{2}` three times is `{2}{2}{2}`, six generic, no arithmetic to get wrong) and because a count-scaled tax renders its count once (Sphere of Safety) and a hybrid or Phyrexian tax is already what the parser eats (Norn's Annex, when it is written). **`PriceAttackDeclaration`** is `PriceCast`'s sibling, not `PriceCast`: what the two share is the mana machinery one level down, which `payAbilityManaCostLocked` already shared between casts, activations and special actions — so the declaration became that payer's fourth caller and `ManaTrigger` fires for its taps with no second copy of anything. The cost is charged **at CR 508.1a, on the declaration, before anything is staged**: `DeclareAttackerWith` / `DeclareAttackersWith` price the ELIGIBLE set, pay it ALL OR NOTHING, and leave the board untouched on a refusal (`ErrAttackTaxUnpaid`). No `pay_unless` prompt — CR 508.1a is part of the turn-based action, so the action that declares the attack pays for it, exactly as ADR 0073 has the action that casts a spell pay its additional costs. `legal/combat.go` prices every candidate through the same function, drops what the seat cannot afford (#544), stamps `MoveCost.Mana` so a policy that may not import `internal/game` can weigh it, and sets `auto_tap` when the move is offered on the strength of the tapper. The heuristic subtracts it in `attackValue`. Propaganda, Ghostly Prison and Sphere of Safety ship `full`. **Still open and a different seam**: the count-limit shape (Silent Arbiter, Crawlspace), which is a property of the whole declaration rather than a price on it and belongs beside `blockerBoundsLocked`.

- **Transform a permanent** (#343 / #1112, CR 701.27, CR 712.18, [ADR 0079](decisions/0079-transforming-a-permanent.md)) — the verb, and it is deliberately **two** verbs. `Game.TransformPermanentForEffect` turns a battlefield permanent over IN PLACE and never goes near `MoveCard`: CR 712.18 says the permanent doesn't become a new object, so `InstanceID`, `ObjectEpoch`, counters, marked damage, tap state, attachments, the CR 613.7 timestamp, summoning sickness, the controller and every `Game`-side per-object registry all survive, and no `EventZoneMove` / `EventETB` / `EventLTB` is emitted — a back face's "when this enters" trigger does **not** fire off a transform, because nothing entered. `Game.ExileAndReturnTransformedForEffect` is the OTHER shape ("exile this Saga, then return it to the battlefield transformed", CR 712.14a): two zone changes, CR 400.7 in full, a genuinely new object. Conflating them gives Fable of the Mirror-Breaker a Saga that kept its lore counters and Storm the Vault a permanent that re-triggers its own ETBs — wrong in opposite directions, which is why they are two functions and not a flag. Card-side: `effects.Transform{Target}`, `effects.TransformThis{}`, `effects.ExileAndReturnTransformed{Target, Controller}`. **What made it small** is that ADR 0034 had already built the representation — `Card.Faces` / `ActiveFace` / `SetFace`, the `"<oracle_id>#1"` catalog key that hands the back face its whole `Spec` for free, `MoveCard`'s CR 712.8a reset, and `layout` / `faces[]` / `active_face` already on the wire, in `cardSnapshot` and in `cloneCard`. **No new `game.Card` field**: "this permanent is transformed" is `ActiveFace != 0`, which is how CR 701.27g reads and which was already on disk and already on every snapshot. **`EventTransform`** carries the permanent, its controller, the face it turned to and the name of the face it turned from; `layerVersionBump` gains an arm for it, which is not optional — a face change is a *printed-value* change and `Effective()` serves a warm cache, so without it a transformed Storm the Vault keeps reporting "Legendary Enchantment". `LogTransform` narrates it, per #984's gate. **CR 701.27c/d is `nil` and no event**, not an error and not `EventEffectError`: an instruction that legally does nothing is a resolution, not a card that threw, and the catalog soak fails a game on the latter. The layout allowlist in `CanTransform` is the load-bearing line — an `adventure` card also has two `Faces`. One rider came with it: **CR 712.8e**, a nonmodal back face's mana value is computed from its FRONT face's cost, which `ParsedManaValue` was getting wrong as 0 for every transformed permanent. **Transforming Sagas needed no change at all**: `IsSaga` reads the effective subtypes and `SagaFinalChapter` derives from the back face's (absent) chapter triggers, so CR 714.4 finds nothing to sacrifice whichever shape the card uses. **Cards:** Storm the Vault // Vault of Catlacan (`full`) and Fable of the Mirror-Breaker // Reflection of Kiki-Jiki (`caveats`); the four Avatar: The Last Airbender transforming Sagas (`#343` — The Legend of Kuruk // Avatar Kuruk, The Legend of Kyoshi // Avatar Kyoshi and The Legend of Roku // Avatar Roku, `caveats`, and The Legend of Yangchen // Avatar Yangchen, `caveats`), all using `ExileAndReturnTransformed` exactly as Fable does; and Aang, Swift Savior // Aang and La, Ocean's Fury (`caveats`), the first card to reach a back face through `TransformThis` on an ACTIVATED ability rather than a Saga's chapter III — its Waterbend {8} pays a flat mana cost instead of the printed discount, which is the tap-another-permanent seam below, not this one. Still open on this row, each now filed where it belongs rather than here: **Incubator and other double-faced TOKENS** (the same object model as "Triggered and static abilities on a token that is not a copy", #521 and #1248 — a token can carry a trigger since ADR 0083, so Fable's Goblin Shaman has its attack trigger; what a token still cannot have is a second FACE); **meld** (CR 701.42, CR 712.4c explicitly forbids melded permanents from transforming, so the verb is already right about them); **day and night** (CR 730 — a turn-machinery designation, which is what the werewolves actually wait on); **CR 701.27f**'s "hasn't transformed since the ability was put onto the stack" anti-loop clause; **"as this permanent transforms"** (CR 712.20); and **entering the battlefield transformed from a zone other than the stack** beyond the one exile-return use (Ojer Axonil, Deepest Might is the card waiting on that last one, and ships without its dies trigger rather than returning as a 4/4 — #1107). **Brass's Tunnel-Grinder** is off this row entirely: it also needs discover (CR 701.57) and descend, neither of which exists.

- **Protection from a player, and a choice option that names a seat** (#980 / #994, [ADR 0072 §7 amendment 2026-09-19](decisions/0072-protection.md), [ADR 0018 amendment 2026-09-19](decisions/0018-triggers-on-the-stack.md)) — closes the last piece of the protection row (CR 702.16k) and the last piece of the choose-a-player row, which were the same piece. **`Card.ChosenPlayer`** is the CR 614.12 "as this enters, choose a player" answer, modelled on `Card.ChosenColor` to the letter: per instance, `carried` by clone and the snapshot, cleared at BOTH CR 400.7 sites (`zone.go`'s battlefield exit and `resetAsNewObjectLocked`'s new-object reset, because a blinked permanent reaches the second without passing the first), and not a copiable value (CR 707.2) — which needs no rule, since `CopiableValuesOf` projects printed characteristics and never looks at it. **`Game.QueueChoosePlayerAsEntersForEffect(chooser, source, question, among)`** writes it, queued from the `AsEnters` hook rather than by pausing the CR 614 pipeline (S26's declared simplification, third use), with `effects.ChoosePlayerAsEnters(label, pool)` and `ChosenPlayerOf` as the card-facing pair. **No new prompt kind, again**: it is #929's `option_pick` with #929's option list, so the gate, the enumerator, the wire and the client modal are untouched. **The quality** is `ProtectionQualityPlayer`, token `game.ProtectionFromChosenPlayer`, and it is the one entry in the closed grammar tested against the source's CONTROLLER rather than any characteristic of it — which is what `TargetSource.Controller` and `Characteristic.Controller` on the damage LKI were already carrying. The seat is NOT in the token: the parser records only the kind and the READER binds `ProtectionQuality.Player` off the protected card (`bindProtectionQuality`, called by `ProtectionQualities` and `MatchedProtection`), so all four DEBT checks keep their existing `ProtectedFrom(card, chars)` call unchanged and `Printed` stays "the chosen player" — no raw UUID reaches a display string. An unanswered prompt leaves the permanent protected from NOBODY, never from everybody. **#994, the seam under it:** `ChoiceOption.Player` lets an option name its subject, `uuid.Nil` on every option that is not about a seat. A departed player is now pruned off every open option list — `reassignChoiceLocked` when the chooser is the one leaving, `pruneDepartedSeatOptionsLocked` (from `leaveGameObjectsLocked`) when anybody else is — where before, eligibility was filtered once at queue time and a prompt went on offering somebody who was not a player (CR 800.4a). A list pruned to nothing is dropped rather than left unanswerable. Because the list can shrink, a seat prompt is answered with its SEAT and not its index: `OptionPickPrompt.ThenSeat` receives `PickOptions[i].Player` off the list the chooser was shown, and `PickOptionView.player` puts the same id on the wire (public by CR 400.2, so no redaction). **2 cards:** True-Name Nemesis (new — the quality end to end) and Sawhorn Nemesis (new — the same stored field read by a damage replacement, touching no protection code, which is the evidence the field is general as-enters machinery and not a protection back door). A PLAYER having protection was the last unrelated piece and closed with #1197 (its own entry above).

- **Set-level rule on a library-top pick** (#998, the PROMPT half of the row above) — `game.ChooseCardsPrompt.Validate` has judged the picked SET since #624, is enforced in exactly one place (`checkChooseCardsPicksLocked`, which `ResolveChooseCards` and `internal/legal`'s `ChooseCardsPickLegalLocked` both go through) and had its own wire contract in [protocol.md](protocol.md) — but the two library-top pick primitives built their `choose_cards` prompt without forwarding it, so "any number of nonland permanent cards with **total mana value 4 or less** from among them" could not be stated over a look at the top seven. `effects.PutFromLibraryOntoBattlefield.Validate` and `effects.TakeFromLibraryToHand.Validate` are that field, forwarded verbatim; no engine change. One behaviour rides with it: a set rule switches OFF the "the only legal answer is every candidate" shortcut, because with a rule it is the rule and not the count that decides which subsets are answers, and the shortcut would otherwise perform a set the prompt would have refused. **Ao, the Dawn Sky** ships `full` on it — `WhenThisDies` + `TriggeredAbility.Modes` (#764) + `LookAtTopOfLibraryForEffect` + `b23TotalManaValueAtMost(4)`, all of which already existed. The other pick prompts were checked and need nothing: `QueueDiscardChoiceForEffect` already forwards `Validate` (#624), and every other catalog `choose_cards` over a hand, a graveyard or the battlefield is a one-card pick, where a rule about the set is a rule about the card and `Match` already says it. The COST half of the row (a sacrifice clause's set-level predicate) is untouched and still open.

- **Cast provenance, "unless it escaped"** (#653, CR 400.7d, CR 702.138b) — `Card.Provenance` is a `CastProvenance{AltCost, FromZone}` record of the SPELL that became the permanent, stamped at the one place a spell becomes one and cleared by `MoveCard` on the way off the battlefield (CR 400.7). `Card.NamedTribe`'s lifecycle exactly: per-entry, carried by clone and snapshot, classified in `snapshot_drift_test.go`. Readers are `ctx.Escaped()` and `ctx.CastProvenance()`, and which OBJECT they ask about is the point — the SOURCE PERMANENT, not the item being resolved, because by the time "sacrifice it unless it escaped" resolves the item on the stack is that trigger and the spell finished two steps ago (`ctx.PaidAltCost`, what an overloaded Cyclonic Rift reads, is the wrong question here). **One place, and it had to be made one**: stack resolution kept its own inline copy of the battlefield push while the RESUME of the same event went through #919's `executeEntryToBattlefieldLocked`; the inline copy is gone and the stamp is one line in the finisher, before `EventETB` so the entry trigger already sees it. **Cards:** Phlage, Titan of Fire's Fury, Uro, Titan of Nature's Wrath (its other blocker, "put a land card from your hand onto the battlefield", shipped with #654) and Pharika's Spawn, all Full; the shared clause is `effects.SacrificeThisUnlessItEscaped`. Still waiting, each on something that is not provenance: **Kroxa** on "each opponent who didn't discard a nonland card this way" (a discarded-card report, #650 — only an event-log scan exists); **Tizerus Charger** on "escapes with your choice of a +1/+1 counter or a flying counter", which is a CHOICE the fixed `EntersWithCounterName` pair cannot express AND a keyword counter the engine does not read; **Skyway Robber** on "escapes with [an ability]" (CR 702.138d) plus "cast from among cards exiled with this creature". The record is the growth point for all three and for #664's "if it was kicked": a sibling field on `CastProvenance`, not a second record.

- **Adventure casting** (#719, CR 715, [ADR 0034](decisions/0034-multi-face-cards.md) step 6) — the whole lifecycle on ADR 0066's one permission model. `CastableFaces` offers both halves of an `adventure` card, because CR 715.3 lets the caster choose; an Adventure spell that RESOLVES is exiled instead of going to a graveyard (CR 715.3d, `routeResolvedAdventureToExileLocked` in `game/adventure.go`, on the resolution path and NOT in `routeStackCardToGraveyardLocked`, which also carries a fizzle); and the permission that lands on it there is a card-scoped `CastPermission{Zone: exile, WhileInZone, CastOnly, Faces: [0]}` naming its OWNER (CR 715.4). It needs no sweep: the grant names the card's CR 400.7 object epoch, so casting the creature — or anything else moving the card — ends it. Countered, fizzled, discarded or milled, an adventure card is an ordinary card in an ordinary graveyard (CR 715.4, 701.6a) and there is no grant. **The permission grew one field to say this**: `Face int` became `Faces []int`, because zero had to mean both "this grant does not speak about faces" and "the creature half", and an adventure's creature half IS face 0 — the wire's `exile_play.face` moved with it to `exile_play.faces`. The restriction belongs on the grant rather than on the card, and that is the point: a Ragavan that impulse-exiles an adventure card opens BOTH halves, and CR 715.4's grant opens one. `deck/validate.go` stopped declaring the `adventure` layout a simplification. **Cards:** Foulmire Knight // Profane Insight ships Full — the cheapest printed Adventure half that needs no announce-time choice — and Bonecrusher Giant // Stomp ships the TARGETED case (#992), which was a client gap rather than an engine one: the view published `target_mode` and `legal_targets` for the face that was up, so a human client asked to cast face 1 had no picker to open (the bot enumerator was already face-correct). The whole announce surface is published per castable face now and the face picker swaps the chosen half's in, so Petty Theft and Swift End need nothing further. An uncatalogued adventure card is unaffected — it has no announce data on either face and resolves by hand. Omens (shuffle instead of exile) are the same shape and are one branch away.

- **Triggered mana abilities, and the colour they read** (#763, [ADR 0074](decisions/0074-triggered-mana-abilities.md)) — closes BOTH the "Triggered mana abilities (CR 605.1b)" row (audit 15 / 19) and the "Mana-ability trigger carrying produced colour" row (count 2, audit 1 / 4), which were always one pipeline. `Spec.ManaTriggers []game.ManaTrigger` is a slot of its own, read through `CatalogManaTriggers` / `game.ManaTriggersForCard` (`CatalogAbilityKey` + the ADR 0071 `ActiveWhen` gate, like `TriggersForCard`) — NOT a flag on `TriggeredAbility`, because none of that struct's nine stack-shaped fields survive an ability with no item, no targets and no batch. Each entry is `{Label, AppliesTo(prod, source, g) bool, Produced(prod, source, g) string, ActiveWhen}`, and `Produced` returns the ordinary `ParseProducedMana` grammar so "{G}", "{G}{G}" and a pipe all work. ONE firing function, `fireManaTriggersLocked(ManaProduced{Source, Controller, Colors}, pending)`, called from the three production sites and never from `AddMana` (CR 106.12a): `ActivateManaAbility` after the rider when it minted directly, `ResolveManaChoice` when the answered pick came from a tap (`PendingChoice.ManaTapped` — the only place a Birds-style colour is known), and `materializePlanLocked` per planned source. Each real card takes exactly one of the first two branches, so it fires once. A colour choice INSIDE a trigger reuses the pipeline's two existing modes through the shared `addManaSlotsLocked`: a `mana_pick` by hand, a greedy `pickColorForSlot` against the cast under the auto-tapper. `EventManaAdded` now carries the colour it added in `Event.Colors`. Triggered mana does not re-trigger (the adder never calls the firing function, which is also the rule). Shipped on Wild Growth, Overgrowth, Utopia Sprawl (with #742's chosen colour), Fertile Ground, Mana Flare and Caged Sun (#1600 — `WheneverYouTapALandForManaOfTheChosenColor`, which also checks the produced colour rather than just the source, since Caged Sun's additional mana is conditional on the land having produced the chosen colour and Wild Growth's family is not). Out of scope and still open: mana-production REPLACEMENT (CR 106.12b, Nyxbloom Ancient — its own row below), turn-scoped mana triggers (High Tide, Bubbling Muck — #663's registry question), counting triggered mana in the auto-tap PLAN (declared: the planner may tap one land too many and the surplus floats), and production without a tap.

- **Library to hand for a named subset of a look or a reveal** (#952) — "look at / reveal the top N, take a subset, do something with the rest" is one of the most common templates in the format and the engine had no door for its middle step. `Game.TakeFromLibraryToHandThenForEffect(player, ids, then)` and its fire-and-forget twin (`server/internal/game/library_to_hand.go`) are that door, on the ordinary route machinery: `takeToHandRoute` is a sibling of `bounceRoute` carrying the taking player as `Actor`, the move goes through `routeAllThenLocked` so it is one simultaneous exit with the CR 614 window open and CR 903.9 offered, and `then` is told what LANDED (CR 400.7) rather than what was asked to move. Cards that are no longer in a library are skipped, which is the whole difference between this and the bounce the catalog had been borrowing: `zoneRoute.CardID` finds a card's zone by scan, so "return it to its owner's hand" happens to move a card that was never on the battlefield, and Goblin Ringleader shipped on that accident. The card side is `effects.TakeFromLibraryToHand` (`take_from_library.go`), `put_from_library.go`'s twin clause for clause — `Match` / `Max` / `Optional` / `All` over a `choose_cards` prompt with `Zone: ZoneLibrary`, a `Reveal` flag for "you may REVEAL a creature card from among them" (the take becomes public, the rest of a LOOK stays private), and a `Then` that is handed `Taken` and `Rest` — with `TakeRestOnBottomInRandomOrder`, `TakeRestIntoGraveyard`, and the whole-sentence helpers `LookAtTopThenMayTakeToHand` and `RevealTopThenTakeToHand`. Shipped on **Horn of the Mark** (full: the card prints "in a random order", so no caveat), and `b41RevealTopThenTakeMatching` — Goblin Ringleader — moved onto it off the bounce. **#952's triage correction:** of the four cards filed against the closed #744, only Horn of the Mark was waiting on this, and none of the four was waiting on a random-order bottom, which is what #744 was. **Ao, the Dawn Sky** puts cards onto the BATTLEFIELD, not into a hand; it waits on a set-level "total mana value 4 or less" rule over the picked set — the row above. **Chaos Wand** is not blocked at all and can be written today: `effects.MillToZone{To: ZoneExile, Until: …}` is the exile-until and takes any player's library, `CastPermission.Player` is keyed to a PLAYER rather than the card's owner (`{0}` + `CastOnly`, cascade's grant), and `PutOnBottomInRandomOrderForEffect(actor, ZoneExile, ids)` routes the leftovers to that library's bottom. It carries cascade's declared simplification as a caveat: the engine has no inline cast during a resolution, so "you may cast it" is a permission taken as a separate action. **Sandstone Oracle** reads a hand size and draws — Slithermuse's sentence, already written — and waited only on the choose-an-opponent prompt, #929, which landed in the row below while this was in flight; it is unblocked and ready to write. The wider roadmap row this leg belongs to — "Library-top placement and ordered look", 45 / 31 / 37 sole unlocks across the three passes — finally has a tracker, **#996**, and the ordered bottom ("in any order") that Goblin Ringleader's caveat names is the largest thing still open on it. Ao's one-field gap is **#998**.

- **Ordered library placement — "in any order" on top, on the bottom, or top-or-bottom** (#996, [ADR 0088](decisions/0088-ordered-library-placement.md)) — the placement half of the roadmap's "Library-top placement and ordered look" row (45 / 31 / 37 sole unlocks). One prompt kind, `put_in_library` (`game/library_order.go`), is the fourth member of the scry family: same `ScryCards`, same chooser-only projection, same client dialog, answered with scry's `{top_order, bottom}` keys, both TOP-FIRST, and a `LibraryPlacement` (`top` / `bottom` / `top_or_bottom`) that says which lanes are open. `Game.PutInLibraryInChosenOrderThenForEffect` takes the cards from anywhere — a card already in its owner's library is REORDERED (no window, no event), anything else goes through the tuck route one leg at a time in pile order, so the CR 614 window opens, a commander is offered CR 903.9 and a paused leg pauses the pile. `From` is CR 400.7: a card that left the zone it was in is skipped. Knowledge afterwards follows CR 401.4 — a card alone in its lane keeps its knowers, two or more are known by the chooser alone, because the order is not revealed. No prompt for a pile of one on a one-lane placement; `top_or_bottom` always asks. Gate: blocks; leave-game: dropped with the chooser; enumerator: leave-alone + each-to-front (+ bury-all and bury-each for top-or-bottom); bot: best own card on top, bury opponents' cards. Card side (`effects/library_order.go`): `PutInLibraryInAnyOrder`, `TakeRestOnBottomInAnyOrder` (the `Then` for the forty-odd "the rest on the bottom of your library in any order" cards), `LookAtTopThenTakeOneRestOnBottom` (Impulse's whole sentence), `PutFromHandOnTopInAnyOrder` (Brainstorm's put-back — now pick, THEN order, instead of the pick order standing in for the order), and `PutIntoLibrary{Depth}` for "second / third from the top", plus `SearchLibrary.Depth` for "then shuffle and put that card third from the top". **Cards:** Goblin Ringleader goes `full` (its random-order caveat is gone), Brainstorm moves onto the two-prompt shape, Aetherspouts stops borrowing Scry (it fired "whenever you scry" payoffs and opened the scry window), Vampiric Tutor's stale "goes straight to your hand" caveat is cleared, and Impulse, Oust and Long-Term Plans are new. The scry dialog's bottom lane is reorderable, which scry wanted too (CR 701.22a). **Closed by #1298** ([ADR 0088's 2026-09-23 amendment](decisions/0088-ordered-library-placement.md#amendment-2026-09-23-1298-the-library-positions-put_in_library-did-not-cover)), on the same prompt: a look at ANOTHER player's library (`Game.LookAtTopOfPlayersLibraryForEffect(looker, owner, n)` marks the looker alone; `effects.LookAtLibraryThenPlace` — Jace, the Mind Sculptor's +2, Portent); a top lane at a DEPTH chosen by the card's owner (`PutInLibrarySpec.TopDepth`, `effects.PutIntoLibraryAtDepthOrBottom` — Temporal Cleansing; a real `top_or_bottom` prompt rather than the `ConfirmPrompt` first guessed at, so the wire, enumerator and dialog carry the position); an EXACT top-lane count (`TopCount`, refused otherwise — Cream of the Crop); and a COUNTER to a position chosen by the counterer (`Counter`, each leg `Game.CounterSpellToLibraryThenForEffect` on the shared stack exit — Hinder, Spell Crumple — asked BEFORE the counter, so an uncounterable spell is never asked about and a commander's owner answers CR 903.9 knowing the end). A card placed alone in its lane from a public zone is known to the whole table afterwards (`publicKnowersOfLocked`, CR 400.2). Wire: `top_count` / `top_depth`, public. Still open: Library of Leng's batch discard order; Lost Days and Wan Shi Tong, All-Knowing are card work on `PutIntoLibraryAtDepthOrBottom` (Wan Shi Tong also needs a batched "one or more cards are put into a library" event).

- **Protection (CR 702.16)** (#662, [ADR 0072](decisions/0072-protection.md), superseding [ADR 0038 §7](decisions/0038-protection-style-keywords.md)) — closes the protection row (count **38**). All four DEBT checks are against the **source object**, never its controller: targeting (CR 702.16b), attachment (CR 702.16c-d), damage (CR 702.16e) and blocking (CR 702.16f). The quality is a parameterised token in `Characteristic.Abilities` ("protection from red", "protection from Demons") with ONE closed-grammar reader, `game/protection.go` — colours, card types, creature subtypes and "everything"; a quality it cannot parse mints no token and leaves the card flagged by the ADR 0037 signal, which is how "protection from monocolored" still reads as unimplemented. The targeting refactor is the body of the work: `game.TargetSource{Controller, Object, Snapshot}` replaced the bare caster ID at `targets.go`'s choke point and every targeting call site now names its source — cast, activation, trigger pick, pending-choice resume, the spell-copy re-target (a value copy, because the original can be countered), the CR 608.2b re-check, the view's legal-target stamps and `internal/legal`'s `legalTargetSets`. Damage reads `ReplacementEvent.SourceLKI`, a `Characteristic` snapshot taken where the event is created, so a SPELL (never on the battlefield) and an attacker already swept into a graveyard both answer correctly. Prevention is one engine built-in beside #960's regeneration shield, declared `Preemptive` so it applies before every other replacement and spends no prevention-shield charge (#420) — a declared simplification of CR 616.1. Shipped on **5 cards**: **Baneslayer Angel** (a subtype quality; a changeling counts as a Demon through #939's `AllCreatureTypes`), **Sword of Fire and Ice**, **Sword of Feast and Famine**, **Animar, Soul of Elements** — all four clearing their protection caveats — and **Mother of Runes**, new, on #742's existing `choose_color` kind. **Protection FROM a player (CR 702.16k) closed with #980** — see the row below. Player protection — a PLAYER having it — closed with #1197 (its own entry above). Still open: CR 615.12 "can't be prevented" (modelled nowhere), and CR 616.1 ordering against protection.

- **Keyword actions with a count are replaceable events** (#976, [ADR 0013 §5s](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md)): "If you would proliferate, proliferate twice instead" (Tekuthal, Inquiry Dominus) and "if you would scry, scry that many plus one instead" (the Crystal Ball shape) replace the ACTION, and there was no event for one — `ProliferateForEffect` applied its choice directly and `lookAtTopForEffect` queued its prompt directly, so neither entered the CR 614 pipeline. One `game.RepEventKeywordAction{KeywordAction, KeywordActionCount, Actor, Source}` now opens once per INSTRUCTION at the one entry point of each action (proliferate CR 701.34, scry CR 701.22, surveil CR 701.25), on ADR 0061's token-creation shape. What the count means is per action and is the rules' own distinction: for proliferate it is the number of TIMES the whole action is taken (base 1, choice and all), for scry and surveil the number of CARDS. Card-side it is `KeywordActionBecomes(action, count, label)` with `ProliferateTwice` and `ScryPlusOne` as the named wrappers — the count function applies to the count the EVENT carries, so two of them compose the way CR 616.1 composes them. Identity and ordering are inherited, not declared: two Tekuthals are one declared effect (#792's skip) and proliferate four times with no prompt, while a doubler beside a "plus one" prompts because ×2 then +1 and +1 then ×2 differ. Both entry points can PAUSE on that prompt and carry what they owe on `keywordActionTail`, so a paused scry has queued nothing and `ScryThenForEffect` returns 0; a cancelled action still runs the continuation after "then". "Look at the top N cards of your library" is not a keyword action and opens no window. **Cards:** Tekuthal, Inquiry Dominus goes to `full` — the last of the two things #943 left it blocked on. No catalog card prints the scry or surveil side yet.

- **The mill amount is a replaceable quantity** (#569, [ADR 0013 §5u](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md)): "If an opponent would mill one or more cards, they mill twice that many cards instead" (Bruvac the Grandiloquent) replaces the NUMBER, once, before anything leaves the library, and `ReplacementEventKind` had no kind for it. What was NOT missing is the per-card window, and the claim that it was circulated on three status comments and a PR body: every milled card has gone through `routeCardToZoneLocked` since #529, so Leyline of the Void sees each one and a milled commander is offered the command zone (#539). One `game.RepEventMill{MillPlayer, MillCount}` now opens once per mill INSTRUCTION, on the shape `RepEventCreateTokens` (ADR 0061) and `RepEventKeywordAction` (#976) already use — so a mill opens two kinds of window in sequence, the amount then the per-card exit. It opens only for a mill the rules can replace the amount of: a GRAVEYARD destination (CR 701.13a defines the keyword action by where the cards go, so "exile the top N cards of your library" is not a mill) and a POSITIVE count (an unbounded `until` run names no number). The count is the INSTRUCTION's, not the library's — CR 701.17b's "as many as possible" clamp stays in `millPlanLocked`, after the window. Card-side it is `MillBecomes{Count, Scope, Label}` with `OpponentsMillTwice` and `OpponentsMillPlus(n)` as the named wrappers. Both mill forms can now PAUSE before any card moves, carrying the destination, the `until` predicate and the caller's continuation on `millTail`; a cancelled, zeroed or abandoned mill runs that continuation with an empty list. **Cards:** Bruvac the Grandiloquent and The Water Crystal, both `full` — ×2 and +4, the pair CR 616.1's ordering prompt is about, and the first printed board on which #982's affected-player rule is observable (the prompt goes to the opponent being milled, not to the player who controls both). Combustible Gearhulk moves to the continuation form because it read the fire-and-forget slice. A surveil's graveyard leg is deliberately NOT a mill (CR 701.14a) and opens no amount window.

- **Every replaceable event kind is switched on** (#982): a `ReplacementEventKind` has obligations at five switches and no compiler to enforce any of them — `eventKindMatches` (the watch key), `affectedPlayerForEvent` (the CR 616.1 chooser), `applyResolvedReplacementEventLocked` (the resume), `finishSettledReplacementLocked` (the cancelled-event outcome) and `abandonZoneRouteLocked` (the abandoned-prompt outcome). A missing arm is silently WRONG rather than a build error: the chooser switch had no case for `RepEventDiscard` or `RepEventCreateTokens`, so both fell through to the first gathered effect's controller. `TestEveryReplacementEventKindIsSwitchedOn` reads the kinds and the switches out of the source, the way `TestEveryChoiceKindIsClassifiedAndEnumerated` does for `PendingChoiceKind`, and found a second gap on its first run — an abandoned `RepEventKeywordAction` dropped its continuation. Not a seam a card was waiting on; listed here because the next kind is.

- **Optional additional costs** (#664, [ADR 0073](decisions/0073-optional-additional-costs-and-the-cast-gate.md)) — closes the "Optional additional costs (kicker, buyback)" row (count 15, audit 32 / 47). `AdditionalCost` gains `Optional`, `Key`, `ManaCost` and `Repeat`, so kicker (CR 702.33), multikicker (CR 702.33d) and buyback (CR 702.27) are the components ADR 0021 already shipped with one bool set — Constant Mists' "Buyback—Sacrifice a land" IS the S21 sacrifice clause. `ManaCost` is the one new component and joins the total at CR 601.2f through `AddOptionalCostMana`, which the cast path, the auto-tapper and the bot enumerator all call, so a kicked cast is never offered at the unkicked price. The choice is announced as `CastSpellParams.OptionalCosts []int` (indices, repeated once per payment) at CR 601.2b with the modes; the card-shaped halves ride the flat `discard_ids` / `sacrifice_ids` lists against ONE ordered payment plan, so there is still one validator and one payer. It is recorded on `PaidCost.OptionalCosts`, read at resolution through `ctx.WasKicked()` / `ctx.KickedTimes()`, and carried onto an entering permanent as `Card.PaidOptionalCosts` (CR 400.7d) — the half the stack item cannot answer, because the item is out of `StackMeta` before the ETB event is emitted. Cleared on the way out with the other per-instance state; carried by clone and snapshot; a copy of a kicked spell is kicked (CR 707.10). Buyback replaces the RESOLUTION destination only (CR 702.27a) through #529's shared stack-exit route, so a bought-back commander still gets its CR 903.9 choice and one countered by game rules still goes to the graveyard. **Cards:** Constant Mists, Capsize, Burst Lightning, Rite of Replication, Gatekeeper of Malakir and Wolfbriar Elemental, all Full. Still to be written on the row's other waiters: Everflowing Chalice, Comet Storm and Marshal's Anthem each read the kick count at ENTRY rather than at resolution, which wants the counters-per-payment seam this ADR deliberately left out; Tear Asunder, Maddening Cacophony, Galadriel's Dismissal and Orim's Chant are ordinary catalog work now. Escalate and entwine are explicitly NOT covered — their cost is per extra mode, which the index-list announcement cannot express.

- **"Can't cast" restriction gate and the legendary-sorcery cast condition** (#760, [ADR 0073](decisions/0073-optional-additional-costs-and-the-cast-gate.md)) — closes both the "\"Can't cast\" restriction gate" row (count 4, audit 23 / 31) and the "Legendary-sorcery cast-condition hook" row (count 3), which is why they were one ADR. ONE function, `Game.CastGateLocked(caster, card, zone, params)`, with two callers and one view stamp: `CastSpell` at the point [ADR 0066](decisions/0066-granted-cast-and-play-permissions.md) named (after every CR 601.2b choice, before any cost is paid, so a refused cast costs nothing and every free cast passes through it), `legal.castMovesForCard`, and `protocol.stampLegalTargets`, which turns the refusal into `cant_cast` on the card and clears `castable_here`. Two sources: a `CastRestriction` contributed by a battlefield permanent, collected through `CatalogAbilityKey` in the `CostModifier` / `CostQuery` shape — so ability removal turns it off, a designation gate switches it off, and nothing is stored, which IS the duration — and the spell's own `Spec.CastCondition`, read off the CardDef under `CatalogKey`. A refusal is a `*CantCastError` wrapping `ErrCantCast` and carrying the printed clause. Split second deliberately stays OUT: it restricts taking an action, blocks activations too, and correctly does not block a land play (CR 702.61b). **Cards:** Rule of Law, Grafdigger's Cage (with a caveat — only its second sentence is a cast restriction), Urza's Ruinous Blast and Rakdos, Lord of Riots, whose "unless an opponent lost life this turn" the audit had misattributed and which is the legendary-sorcery shape exactly. Still open and named in the ADR: bans with a DURATION (Silence's "this turn", Reflector Mage's "until your next turn") want #755's registries, and a ban on a chosen card NAME (Meddling Mage, Nevermore) wants a choose-a-card-name prompt the engine does not have. A nonartifact cast count (Ethersworn Canonist) is one line when its card arrives.

- **Designations that switch printed abilities on** (#757 / #759, [ADR 0071](decisions/0071-designations-that-switch-abilities-on.md)): Class levels (CR 716.2), solved Cases (CR 719.3) and station charge-counter thresholds (CR 721.2) are ONE gate — an `ActiveWhen Designation` field on `StaticAbility`, `TriggeredAbility`, `ActivatedAbilityShape` and `CostModifier`, with one predicate (`Designation.Active`) and one filter, evaluated in the four object-level accessors that sit on ADR 0046's `CatalogAbilityKey` (`StaticAbilitiesForCard`, `TriggersForCard`, `ActivatedAbilitiesForCard`, `CostModifiersForCard`). An inactive ability is ABSENT from what the object hands back, so the layer pass, the trigger harvester, the activation path, the cost pricer, the legal-move enumerator and the wire cannot disagree about whether it exists. `Card.ClassLevel` (a designation, not a counter, and not copiable — CR 716.2c) and `Card.Solved` are both carried by clone and snapshot; `EventClassLevel` and `EventCaseSolved` bump the layer version, and charge counters already did. Level-up is an ordinary activated ability from one constructor (`LevelUp`, carrying CR 716.2d sorcery timing and 716.2e "only from level N-1"); "To solve" is one constructor (`ToSolve`) with the CR 603.4 intervening if checked twice. **Cards:** Wizard Class and Case of the Shattered Pact go Full; Fortune Teller's Talent (#333) registers with two caveats on library-top cast (#765) and The Seriema (#337) with one on the station cost (#758). Still open above: Rooms (CR 709.5). The station ability's cost closed with #759 (see Station, above). **#1321** ([ADR 0071 addendum 2026-09-23](decisions/0071-designations-that-switch-abilities-on.md)) added a fifth designation, `DesignationHarnessed` (CR 701.64 / CR 702.186b's "∞ — [Ability]"), the same three pieces `DesignationCaseSolved` is — `Card.Harnessed`, `HarnessForEffect` / `IsHarnessed` (idempotent, since CR 701.64a is worded "if … isn't … it becomes" rather than a legality restriction), `EventHarnessed` bumping the layer version and silent on the public log (board state is visible via `CardView.harnessed`). The Mind Stone is the proof card and ships Full: indestructible, `{T}: Add {W}`, `Harness` (a plain `Plus(ManaCost, TapCost)` activated ability with no `ActiveWhen`/`Condition`) and the ∞ end-step flicker (Thassa, Deep-Dwelling's blink one clause wider, `ActiveWhen: Harnessed()`).

- **Event-conditioned delayed triggers, "when you next cast"** (#663, CR 603.7b, [ADR 0026 amendment 2026-09-18](decisions/0026-delayed-triggers.md)) — closes the "When you next cast" delayed-trigger row (count 7). `DelayedTrigger` gains an event condition beside its step: `On []EventKind` plus an `AppliesTo` predicate, checked by ONE hook at the end of `triggerHarvester.OnEvent` after the zone walks, where the first matching event fires the trigger and removes it. It fires once (CR 603.7b) and ends with the turn whether or not it fired — carrying [ADR 0063](decisions/0063-durations-and-control.md)'s `Duration` rather than a private int, so `durationExpiredLocked` stays the one place the engine decides a duration is over, and swept by `sweepTurnEndLocked` beside the scoped statics and the turn-scoped replacements (CR 514.2). It never sees the cast that created it, because that spell's `EventCast` was emitted before the resolution that scheduled it — the ordering does the work and nothing has to remember the exclusion. A fired trigger goes through `dispatchTriggerLocked`, the harvester's own dispatch, so the CR 603.5 "you may", the CR 603.3d drop, the CR 603.3b ordering prompt and the APNAP drain are one implementation rather than two; the triggering event's object rides on the item as `Payload`, which is how "copy THAT spell" names the spell without the `Effect` closing over it. This REVERSES ADR 0026 §1-2, which is why the amendment is dated and says what was rejected (a second `TurnScopedTriggers` registry) and why. The catalog side is `effects.WhenYouNextCast(label, pred, effect)` and the general `DelayedOnEvent{…}`, over the existing `CopySpell`. Shipped on Doublecast and Galvanic Iteration, both full. Unblocked and still to be written: Summon: Fenrir (#312), Thunderclap Drake (#313), Sword of Wealth and Power (#313), Yuna, Grand Summoner (#397), Ral, Storm Conduit and Chandra, the Firebrand (#397, both also want emblem-free minus abilities on a planeswalker), Teach by Example, Complete the Circuit and Rimefire Torque — each still needs its own check for other gaps.

- **Triggered abilities that watch from another zone** (#925, CR 113.6) — `TriggeredAbility.Zones []ZoneKind` is the trigger-side sibling of `ActivatedAbilityShape.Zones` and of `CastableZones`: nil means the battlefield, `{ZoneGraveyard}` is "when you cycle this card" (CR 702.29c), Bloodghast's landfall and Narcomoeba's arrival, `{ZoneExile}` is suspend's countdown (CR 702.62b). ONE narrow harvest (`harvestFromDeclaredZones`, `server/internal/game/trigger_zones.go`) walks only the zones some registered card declared for THIS event kind, off an index `effects.Register` builds once at boot — so an event kind nothing declares costs one map lookup and the battlefield walk's per-event cost is unchanged. A declared zone list REPLACES the default rather than adding to it, checked in the same three places the battlefield is walked (`harvestFromZone`, `harvestLTB`, the simultaneous-exit pass), so a graveyard ability can never fire off a permanent. "You" is the card's OWNER while it sits outside the battlefield (CR 108.4), which is what decides both the predicate and who gets the stack item. Everything downstream is unchanged: `dispatchTriggerLocked`, the CR 603.3d target drop, the CR 603.5 prompt, `OncePerBatch` / `BatchKey`, the APNAP drain and undo across the prompt. Constructors: `effects.InGraveyard`, `effects.InExile`, `effects.WhenThisIsPutIntoYourGraveyardFromYourLibrary`. `Register` panics at boot on any zone but the graveyard and exile (`game.TriggerZoneUnsupported`), including `ZoneStack`, which is `FromStack`. Shipped on Narcomoeba and Bloodghast (the latter with its conditional haste declared as a caveat — a static keyed on a life total goes stale, the "Layer invalidation on hand / life / attack / graveyard state" row). Suspend's exile countdown (#659) has since landed on it, and Magmakin Artillerist's own "when you cycle this card" trigger (#1392) is the first `EventCycle` reader — `InGraveyard(On(game.EventCycle, Self, …))` — dropping its cycle-trigger caveat (it keeps one unrelated caveat, the printed CR 603.1 discard-batching behaviour). Decree of Pain is still open on this row.

- **A copy of a permanent spell becomes a token** (#666, [ADR 0043 amendment](decisions/0043-copy-effects.md)): `resolveTopOfStackLocked`'s refusal (an `EventEffectError` and a vanished copy) is replaced by the rule. `resolvePermanentSpellCopyLocked` (`game/spell_copy.go`) takes the copy off the stack first — the creation can pause on a CR 616 prompt — then creates one token from the spell's copiable values through `CreateTokensThenForEffect`, [ADR 0061](decisions/0061-token-creation-and-discard-are-replaceable-events.md) / #923's ONE token-creation path. Everything else follows: the CR 701.7b window opens so **Doubling Season doubles it**, the token takes the ordinary battlefield entry so enters-tapped, enters-with-counters, `EventETB` and `fireETBHookLocked` all reach it, and `PrintedValues.MakeToken` stamps the Token supertype so CR 704.5d removes it from any zone but the battlefield and a bounce cannot make it a card. The face is settled as the ordinary permanent branch settles it, so CR 707.10g (a copy of a double-faced permanent spell is double-faced) falls out. `CopySpellForEffect` and `effects.CopySpell` gain an `Except` taking the same `*game.PrintedValues` an entering permanent's except clause edits, applied where the copy is created so no closure has to cross the CR 707.10c re-target prompt. **What travels and what does not (CR 707.2):** the token is the copiable values plus the Token supertype, so `StackItem.Foretold` and `Card.FaceDownKind` (#987) are absent — a copy is created, not cast, and cannot be born face down — while `PaidCost.OptionalCosts` (#988) is NOT a characteristic but still reaches the token, because CR 707.10b copies the choices made when casting and CR 400.7d carries them onto the permanent: `resolvePermanentSpellCopyLocked` stamps `Card.PaidOptionalCosts` onto the template exactly as the ordinary permanent branch stamps it, so a Double Major on a twice-kicked Wolfbriar Elemental makes a token that creates its own two Wolves. **Cards:** Double Major, Full — the first card in the catalog that copies a permanent spell at all. Still open and NOT closed by this: copying an ABILITY (Lithoform Engine's other halves, Strionic Resonator — the "Ability-copy" row above); a token copy of an AURA spell enters attached to nothing and dies to CR 704.5m; X is not re-derived for the token.

- **A copy "except" clause that grants an ability or adds a subtype** (#665, [ADR 0043 amendment](decisions/0043-copy-effects.md)): `PrintedValues` gains `AddSubtype` / `RemoveSubtype` / `HasSubtype` (CR 707.9b) and `GrantAbility(name)` (CR 707.9a). A grant is stored as a NAME in the copiable values — `PrintedValues.GrantedAbilities`, materialised onto `Card.GrantedAbilities` — so a Clone copying a Phantasmal Image inherits the Illusion type and the sacrifice trigger, and a snapshot carries both. The abilities themselves are static catalog data on the card that grants them (`effects.Spec.Grants`, `AbilityGrant{Key, Triggered, Static, Activated}`), filed under `game.GrantKey(Key)` in the same `defs` map cards use, the way an emblem is. **One lookup seam:** `CatalogKey` returns a composite `"<oracle_id>|grant:<name>"` and `catalogDef` merges the card's definition with each grant's (`game/copy_grants.go`), so the trigger harvester, the layer pass, `ActivateCatalogAbility`, the replacement gather and the view all see a granted ability with no reader of their own; ability removal (`CatalogAbilityKey`) and face-down silence (CR 708.2a) both still win, because both answer before it. `game.BaseCatalogKey` is for the two sites that read a key as an identity. **Cards:** Phantasmal Image (new, with the declined-0/0 caveat Clone also carries) and Sakashima the Impostor, which drops its caveat and is now Full. Still open: a copy effect with a DURATION (Mirage Mirror, Cytoshape); granted MANA abilities have no slot; a granted ability's text is not on the wire. Estrid's Invocation (#387) and Machine God's Effigy (#389) are unblocked by this and still need their own files.

- **Layer dependency ordering (CR 613.8) and effects that outlive their source (CR 613.6)** (#668 / #669 / #670, [ADR 0067](decisions/0067-layer-dependency-ordering.md)): layer 4 is ordered by CR 613.8 dependency, detected by TRIAL APPLICATION against a fixed base rather than by a declared read/write set on every static, with the CR 613.8c re-check after each application and a timestamp fallback on a loop (`game/layer_dependency.go`). All five catalogued pairs come out the same whichever card entered first (Urborg + Song of the Dryads, Urborg + a slumbering Arixmethes, Maskwood Nexus + a crewed Vehicle, + The Warring Triad, + Arixmethes), and Urborg + a Song on a permanent that was ALREADY a land stays independent. An ability removal is applied in the layer it happens in and reaches forwards only, so a silenced source still applied in the earlier layers (Magus of the Moon's 2021-03-19 ruling) and a multi-layer effect carries on through `ContinuesAfterRemoval` (CR 613.6, Humility + Opalescence); the S24 ability-removal fixed point is gone and the recompute is one pass. CR 305.7's loss moved into layer 4 (`effects.SetsBasicLandType`), so a grant that lands in layer 6 survives it. "Is every creature type" is a layer-4 fact on the Characteristic, not the changeling keyword (#670). CR 704.5p joined `attachmentLegalLocked` as the enabling rule (#675's SBA half). **Cards:** Magus of the Moon ships; Urborg, Song of the Dryads and Arixmethes are `CompletenessFull`; Maskwood Nexus and The Warring Triad lost their dependency caveats. Arcane Adaptation, Leyline of Transformation, Encroaching Mycosynth, Yavimaya, Cradle of Growth and Prismatic Omen are unblocked and still need their own files. Adding a dependency-ordered bucket other than layer 4 needs a catalogued pair and a benchmark — every layer costs 14x on a saturated board.

- **The rest of counter costs** (#789, [ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md) addendum 2026-09-18) - one `game.CounterRemovalCost` with TWO owners: `AbilityCost.RemoveCounters` and, new, `ManaAbilityShape.RemoveCounters`. It covers five printed shapes — from the source, from one other permanent you control, of any kind, a count the activator announces (`RemoveCountersXFromThis`, reaching the effect through `StackItem.Paid.CountersRemoved` and `ManaAbilityShape.ProducedForPaid`), and a removal split across permanents (`RemoveCountersAmong`, validated as a set like a crew payment). `AbilityCost.AddCounter` is the other direction, Devoted Druid's "put a -1/-1 counter on this creature": paid at announce, refused by one `canPlaceCounterLocked` predicate (CR 118.3), and — since #1710 — through the CR 614 counter window like any other cost-paid counter, so Doubling Season still does nothing to it (CR 614.16) but Vizier of Remedies and Winding Constrictor do (see #1710 below). The auto-tapper plans a counter-cost mana source only when it can both decide and afford the cost, so a Vivid land out of charge counters is not a five-colour source. Shipped on Vivid Creek, Vivid Grove, Ramos Dragon Engine, Mage-Ring Network, Devoted Druid, Hopeful Initiate and Iron Spider (whose caveat is cleared). The one shape it left open — an any-kind removal split across permanents — is closed by #943 below, and the counter-cost row has come out of the open table.

- **The last counter-cost shape: an any-kind removal split across permanents** (#943, [ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md) addendum 2026-09-18 §22) - `CounterRemovalCost.Counter` may be empty WITH `Among`: "Remove three counters from among other artifacts, creatures, and planeswalkers you control" takes three counters of whatever kinds are there, so the payment names a kind per permanent as well as a count. No sixth component and no second wire shape — `counter_kinds` is a parallel array beside `counter_source_ids` / `counter_counts`, sent only when the parts actually differ in kind, so a payment of one kind is byte-for-byte what a #625 client sends. The payment's set is keyed on (permanent, KIND), which is what lets one creature pay with its +1/+1 counter and its shield counter and still refuses naming one permanent twice in the same kind. One validator, one candidate walk, one enumerator arm (drain the fullest first, kinds included), one projection — the option list was already a (permanent, kind) list, so the client's many-pick modal needed no kind dropdown, only a label. `effects.Register`'s refusal of the combination is gone. **Cards:** Tekuthal, Inquiry Dominus ships, with one declared caveat that was NOT this seam — "if you would proliferate, proliferate twice instead" is a replacement of a keyword action (CR 701.34), which had no seam either until #976 built one (see the keyword-action row above); the caveat is gone and the card is `full`. Its `{U/P}` symbols are payable both ways since #971 landed the activation-time Phyrexian announcement, and its indestructible counter is CR 122.1b carried by its own static (the Vraska Joins Up posture). The old open row's count of 14 predated #625 and was never re-triaged; the component now covers all six printed shapes, so any of those cards still missing is missing for some other reason.

- **Mana-spent-to-cast record on the stack item** (#761, [ADR 0068](decisions/0068-the-mana-spent-on-a-spell.md)) - `StackItem.Paid` is a `game.PaidCost{Mana, OnPaper, CountersRemoved, CountersAdded, LifePaid}`: `attemptSpend` and `SpendManaFor` return the tokens they spent, and every payment path records them. A permissive or `ForceCast` cast records `OnPaper` — "the engine waived this charge" — rather than an empty record, so "if no mana was spent" is never ambiguous, and every reader takes unknown as the weaker-than-printed answer. A copy of a spell records a real zero (CR 707.10). The solver gained one strategy switch, `SpendDistinctColors`, thrown by `Spec.WantsDistinctColors` and applied to the GENERIC half only, so it can never change whether a cost is payable. `EventManaSpent` and `StackItemView` carry the amount and the distinct colours. Readers: `ctx.ColorsSpent`, `ColorsSpentCount`, `ManaSpentOfColor`, `NoManaSpent`, `ManaSpentKnown`, `Paid`, and `Game.StackItemPaidForEffect` for a cast trigger. Shipped on Painful Truths (converge), Etched Oracle (sunburst), Slaying Fire (adamant) and Vexing Bauble ("if no mana was spent"). The row above is still open for SPEND RIDERS.

- **Regeneration (CR 701.19)** (#667, [ADR 0013 §5p](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md)): a regeneration shield is a count on the permanent (`Card.RegenerationShields`), created by `Game.RegenerateForEffect` / `effects.Regenerate{Target}`, cleared at the cleanup step beside the marked damage and on the way off the battlefield (CR 400.7). One engine built-in owns the rule — `regenerationShieldReplacement` in `game/builtin_replacements.go`, beside CR 903.9's — watching the DESTROY `RepEventMove`: it spends one shield, cancels the move, taps the permanent, removes all damage from it (through the shared `clearBattlefieldDamage`, which is also what stops the CR 704.5g check re-firing) and removes it from combat (#921's `removeFromCombatLocked`), then emits `EventRegenerated`. Nothing leaves the battlefield, so no dies-trigger fires and `destroyedThisWayLocked` counts no destruction. Two shields never prompt (a built-in is one registration, so two shields are one applicable effect); a shielded COMMANDER does, because CR 903.9 applies to the same event and CR 616.1 gives its controller the order — both orders leave it alive on the battlefield. **"Destruction" is declared on the event, never derived**: every exit off the battlefield shares one primitive, so `destroyRoute` sets `ReplacementEvent.Destruction` and `battlefieldExitRoute` does not, and the state-based-action sweep tags each doomed permanent with its own rule (`doomedPermanent`) so CR 704.5g/h are destructions and CR 704.5f/i/v are not while all of them still leave as one event. **"It can't be regenerated"** (CR 701.19c) is a variadic `DestroyOptions{CantBeRegenerated}` rider on the destroy verbs that gates the built-in's `AppliesTo`, so an ignored shield is not spent (also CR 701.19c); it is wired onto the **11 catalog cards that print it** (Terminate, Mortify, Putrefy, Pongify, Rapid Hybridization, Snuff Out, Damn — both halves, Damnation, Wrath of God, Winds of Rath, Shatterstorm) and deliberately left off the printings that don't (Day of Judgment, Supreme Verdict, Vanquish the Horde), which is why Wrath of God and Day of Judgment no longer share one helper. The board shows a REGEN badge with the shield count. **4 cards converted**: Asceticism (full — the hexproof half has worked since #353), Wrap in Vigor, Welding Jar and Goblin Chirurgeon. Still open and NOT closed by this: Golgari Charm and Dawn Charm need the modal-clause seam (#764) before their regenerate modes can be written; totem armor (CR 702.111) and CR 701.19b's static "if this would be destroyed, regenerate it" are unmodelled, and no catalog card needs either yet. The rest of the row's cards are unblocked by this and still need their own card files and their own gap check.

- **Emblems (CR 114)** (#623, [ADR 0064](decisions/0064-emblems.md)): an emblem is a `game.Card` with a synthetic `"emblem:<source key>"` catalog key, living in `Player.Emblems` — a second command-zone slice beside the commander pile. Its statics join the layer pass's existing source gather (`activeStaticAbilitiesLocked` → `emblemContinuousEffectsLocked`) and its triggers join the harvester's existing zone walk (`triggerHarvester.OnEvent` → `harvestFromEmblemsLocked`), so a card author writes an emblem's abilities in exactly the vocabulary a permanent's are written in — one new `Spec.Emblem` slot, `CreateEmblem{}` as the ability's whole effect. The split slice is the enforcement of CR 114: `ZoneRef` is `{Kind, Owner}`, so every cast, move, route and lookup resolves `{command, owner}` to the commander pile and can never name an emblem. Snapshot carries it (schema 1 → 2, so a pre-emblem binary refuses a post-emblem restore point rather than silently dropping them), clone carries it, and CR 800.4a is the only exit. `GameView.seats[i].emblems[]` is public and unredacted; the board shows chips beside the player identity with the printed text on hover. **2 of the row's 4 cards converted**: Elspeth, Sun's Champion (−7, static) and Teferi, Hero of Dominaria (−8, targeted trigger). Wrenn and Six's −7 stays omitted — its emblem's only text is a retrace grant, which needs retrace and #652; its caveat now names #652 rather than emblems. Teferi Akosa of Zhalfir's −2 joined them in #626 — the third card, and the first emblem carrying a static AND a trigger in one printed text. "Knights you control get +1/+0 and have ward {1}" is an anthem plus a WARD, and ward is a triggered ability carrying a cost (CR 702.21a), so the emblem carries the trigger rather than writing a keyword onto the Knight: `effects.WardGranted`, of which `Ward` and `WardAttached` are now the two narrow cases. One predicate value serves both halves, so the layer half and the trigger half cannot drift. **#1315** ([ADR 0064 amendment 2026-09-23](decisions/0064-emblems.md)) gave `EmblemSpec` two more slots, `UntapStep` and `DrawStep`, for the two clauses that widen a turn-based action (CR 502.3, CR 504.1) instead of triggering — the emblem-side mirror of `Spec.UntapStep` (#74) and the new `game.DrawStepPermission`. The untap-step and draw-step gathers now walk every seat's `p.Emblems`, the third and fourth walks alongside the statics and triggers gathers above. Teferi, Who Slows the Sunset's −7 is the fourth card converted, closing the row. **#1275** added a fifth slot and a fifth walk: `EmblemSpec.ActivationTimings`, read by the per-player activation timing walk over `p.Emblems` (Teferi, Temporal Archmage's −10 — see the entry at the top of this list).

- **A discard is a replaceable event with a cause** (#650, [ADR 0061](decisions/0061-token-creation-and-discard-are-replaceable-events.md)): the shared exit primitive opens `RepEventDiscard` rather than an anonymous hand → graveyard move, carrying `DiscardPlayer`, `DiscardCause` (`"effect"` / `"cost"` / `"cleanup"` — the distinction the rules draw, per ADR 0013 §10a, not "voluntary") and the causing `Source`, alongside the move payload a `Replace` rewrites. `EventDiscardCard` still fires wherever the card lands (CR 701.8a defines a discard by the move OUT of the hand), now carrying the cause, and the CR 903.9 built-in watches both kinds so a discarded commander is still offered the command zone. A COST discard still settles without asking (CR 601.2h), so an `Optional` replacement on one is skipped un-applied. Cards declare it through `DiscardBecomes{Dst, Causes, Optional, …}.Build()`. Shipped on **Library of Leng** (1 card, caveat: no ordering choice when one effect saves several cards), which batch 28 (#390) had skipped. Madness (#657) has since landed on it and took the last two cards off that row (Fiery Temper, Big Game Hunter) — the row is gone from the open table and its own bullet is below. The two graveyard arrivals this left outside the window — search-to-graveyard (`effect_api.go`) and surveil (`ResolveSurveil`) — were closed by #931 (row below), which took **Rest in Peace** off #383's skip list. A public "discard these card IDs" primitive (Kroxa, the Obstinate Baloth redirect) is still unwritten.

- **A sacrifice batch with a continuation** (#910, [ADR 0013 §5r](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md)): destroy, exile, bounce, tuck and mill have all reported what they did since #815 / #866 / #893; sacrifice had only the per-card call, so Living Death's second pass fired and forgot and every "sacrifice all X, then for each …" read nothing back. `sacrificeRoute(source)` is the seventh route template, built on #667's `battlefieldExitRoute` — the same battlefield-exit mover a destruction takes, deliberately WITHOUT its `Destruction` flag, so the CR 701.19 regeneration built-in can never see a sacrifice (CR 701.17a) — plus the `EventSacrifice` announcement the leg now owns (before the window, while the permanent is still there) and its own "this way" rule. `Game.SacrificeAllThenForEffect(source, ids, then)`, `SacrificeThenForEffect` (single card, "sacrifice a creature. If you do, …") and the fire-and-forget `SacrificeAllForEffect`; `sacrificePermanentLocked` is the same leg with no batch, so there is one sacrifice path and not two. A sacrifice is not replaceable (CR 701.17a) but the MOVE it makes is, so a sacrificed commander opens CR 903.9 and a leg can pause. **"Sacrificed this way" is the permanent that LEFT the battlefield**, wherever it landed — the one row where it differs from "destroyed this way" (`sacrificedThisWayLocked` vs `destroyedThisWayLocked`): CR 701.7a defines a destruction by the graveyard, CR 701.17a's sacrifice is the controller's move off the battlefield. **3 cards converted:** Living Death (pass 3 now waits for pass 2, and the creatures leave as one simultaneous exit), God-Eternal Bontu ("then draw that many cards" is the continuation's count) and All Is Dust, which lost its caveat — a Blood Artist swept by the spell sees every death including its own (CR 603.10). Slaughter the Strong rides the same helper and keeps its own, unrelated caveat.

- **A bot prices the cards a cost eats** (#1013, [ADR 0033 §1 amendment](decisions/0033-ai-bot-seat.md)): the enumerator offered exactly ONE payment for the card-shaped half of an alternative cost (`legal.maxEnumeratedCostPayments` was 1) and built it out of the first N candidates in ZONE order, so an escaping Uro over a graveyard holding a second Uro, a Snapcaster target and three lands ate whichever three were oldest. The cap was never the constraint — the missing EVALUATION was, because a card in a graveyard was worth nothing to `aiseat/heuristic` and two payments were indistinguishable to it. **`aiseat.CostFuelPricer`** is the price: `TargetOrderer`’s twin, asked once per decision and threaded in as `legal.Options.OrderCostFuel`; a second hook rather than a second meaning for the first, because the two ask OPPOSITE questions (a target order ranks the board by importance and the enumerator keeps the TOP; a fuel price ranks your own cards by what you would lose and it spends the BOTTOM). The heuristic’s answer is one function, `heuristic.fuelValue` (`aiseat/heuristic/fuel.go`), read BOTH by the enumerator (which payment to offer) and by `valueOfCast` (what the payment it was offered costs) — a second scorer would drift and the bot would take a payment it then priced as a mistake. It closes #673’s declared gap on the way: a non-hand cast still costs no card in HAND, and what it really spends is now priced. The cap rises to THREE and ADR 0033 §1’s corollary still holds, because the extra payments come out of whatever `MaxExpansionPerSource` the TARGET walk did not use — an Uro with no targets gets its alternatives, a removal spell over a wide board gets none. Still open and named in the amendment: the price does not know what the spell being cast IS, and cannot see a graveyard synergy the wire does not carry (a Crucible makes a land in the graveyard worth keeping, and `FuelFloor` does not know).

- **A prompted sacrifice with a continuation** (#1019, [ADR 0013 §5x](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md)): `PlayerSacrificesForEffect` and `EachPlayerSacrificesForEffect` do not sacrifice anything — they queue a QUESTION per seat and return how many seats were ASKED — so every clause a card wrote on the next line paid out before anybody had chosen. It is the #911 / #993 payout shape arriving through a prompt rather than through a fire-and-forget exit, and it is why ADR 0013 §5v left both entry points out of the lint’s tables: there was no continuation form to name. A **RUN** is that form (`game/sacrifice_run.go`): one run is one printed instruction, however many prompts it takes to ask it, and its continuation runs ONCE — after the last prompt has settled AND the permanents those seats named have finished moving — handed `game.PromptedSacrifices`, one entry per ASKED seat in ask order with what really left the battlefield. Four entry points over one body: `EachPlayerSacrificesThenForEffect` (the APNAP fan-out), `PlayersSacrificeThenForEffect` (a named set of seats), `PlayerSacrificesThenForEffect` (one seat asked N times) and `PlayerSacrificesNForEffect` (the same ask with nothing waiting), plus `effects.EachPlayerSacrifices.Then` card-side. `ResolveSacrificeChoice` routes the picked permanent through `SacrificeThenForEffect`, so the leg rides `sacrificeRoute` and a sacrificed commander’s CR 903.9 prompt holds the payout — and a commander that takes the command zone still counts (CR 701.17a). A WITHDRAWN prompt settles its leg with "sacrificed nothing" rather than stranding the run: `PendingChoiceSacrifice` is the second kind to declare `dropDefault` (#1016), so both `pruneSacrificeChoicesLocked` and the departure sweep reach it through the one action. Run state lives on `Game.sacrificeRuns` keyed by a plain uuid on the choice, not in a frame shared by pointer, because the prompts of one run share MUTABLE state and `cloneLocked` copies each `PendingChoice` by value — `Game.replacementsAppliedThisEvent`’s shape, for #808’s reason. **6 cards converted:** Rise of the Witch-king (the only changed outcome — "if you sacrificed a creature this way" was "was the controller handed a prompt"), Lich-Knights’ Conquest (the COUNT), and Planar Engineering, Cornered by Black Mages, Will of the Abzan and Priest of Forgotten Gods for the ORDER. Archfiend of Depravity, Phyrexian Obliterator and Lotus Field lost their hand-written "ask N times" loops to `PlayerSacrificesNForEffect`. Both of the things it left open closed with #1027 / #1026 (next bullet): Archon of Cruelty, whose printed order needed a DISCARD continuation as well, and Springbloom Druid, whose caveat is gone. Its machinery now lives in `game/prompt_run.go`, shared with the discard run — `sacrifice_run.go` keeps the four entry points and the verb.

- **A prompted discard with a continuation, on the same run** (#1027 / #1026, [ADR 0013 §5y](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md)): `QueueDiscardChoiceForEffect` discards nothing either — it queues a question over the player's own hand and returns the PROMPT'S ID — and since #853 a discard is a real exit, so a discarded commander's CR 903.9 prompt holds the batch for an action after the answer. Archon of Cruelty is the card the hole was filed on: *"target opponent sacrifices …, discards a card, and loses 3 life. You draw a card and gain 3 life"* ran its last three clauses beside two open prompts. **The run is #1019's, and the body is now shared** (`game/prompt_run.go`): `promptRun`, `runPromptsLocked` with the per-verb prompt builder injected, one `settleRunLegLocked`, one `clonePromptRuns`, one `SeatCards`, and `PromptedSacrifices` / `PromptedDiscards` as two defined types over it spelling their own verb. `Game.sacrificeRuns` became `Game.promptRuns` (ONE registry) and `PendingChoice.sacrificeRun` became `PendingChoice.promptRun`; `defaultDroppedChoiceLocked` branches on that LINK rather than on the prompt kind, because a discard prompt is a `PendingChoiceChooseCards` and Thoughtseize's revealed-hand pick is the same kind and no run's leg. Three entry points plus a fire-and-forget fan-out: `PlayerDiscardsThenForEffect`, `PlayersDiscardThenForEffect`, `EachPlayerDiscardsThenForEffect`, `EachPlayerDiscardsForEffect`. **"Discarded this way" is CR 701.8a's move OUT of the hand** (`discardedThisWayLocked`, `sacrificedThisWayLocked`'s shape with the hand in place of the battlefield), so a madness card exiled instead of binned counts, a Library of Leng card on top of the library counts, a CR 903.9 commander counts, and a leg the CR 614 window cancelled does not; the landed list is accumulated by `discardBatchLocked` and carried forward by value across a paused leg. `DiscardPrompt.Then` stays as the LEG's own sentence and takes `(g, seat, discarded)` now, because a fan-out copies one template per seat. `DiscardRandomThenForEffect` is the random discard's answer to the same lint row. **4 cards converted:** Archon of Cruelty and Vicious Rumors for the ORDER, Syphon Mind and the `b39MayDiscardThenDraw` pair (Thrilling Discovery, Cathartic Pyre) for the COUNT — Syphon Mind's declared "one card per answer" simplification and its hand-rolled empty-hand skip are both gone. **Springbloom Druid** (#1026) rides #1019's sacrifice run at resolution instead of picking its land as a trigger target, and lost its caveat. Still open: `graveyardVerb` is the last lint row with no continuation form (`PutIntoGraveyardForEffect`), and a discard prompt whose candidates all leave the hand before it is answered has no prune beside `pruneSacrificeChoicesLocked`, so it wedges rather than settling its leg — unreachable from the catalog today, filed as #1045.

- **Every graveyard arrival opens the CR 614 window** (#931, [ADR 0013 §5q](decisions/0013-replacement-effects.md)): the last two movers that put a card into a graveyard without asking now go through the shared exit primitive — a library SEARCH with `Dest: game.ZoneGraveyard` (`executeSearchTakeLocked`, sixth route template `searchRoute(player, dest)`; the hand destination rides the same take, so a tutored commander gets CR 903.9 there too) and SURVEIL's graveyard leg (`ResolveSurveil`, on `millRoute`, keeping the `EventMill` it has emitted per binned card since S22). Both join the one batch body (`routeAllThenLocked`), so both can PAUSE: a search reports the cards that ARRIVED (CR 400.7) and finishes its `EventSearchLibrary`, its shuffle and its `Then` from the continuation; surveil's `EventSurveil` (`Amount` = how many really reached a graveyard) and the rest of its effect do the same. Cards declare the sentence through `effects.GraveyardBecomesExile{OpponentsOnly}.Build()` (`cards/effects/graveyard_replacements.go`), which watches both exit kinds. **2 cards shipped off #383's skip list: Rest in Peace** (caveat: a dying commander sent to the command zone is not exiled) **and Leyline of the Void** (same caveat, plus no "begin the game with it on the battlefield"). Still waiting: **Dauthi Voidwalker**, which needs this plus a void counter on the exiled card and permission to cast it.

- **Token creation is a replaceable event, and a token takes the entry pipeline** (#762, [ADR 0061](decisions/0061-token-creation-and-discard-are-replaceable-events.md)): `RepEventCreateTokens` is opened once per creation INSTRUCTION (CR 701.7b) and carries token GROUPS — template, count and entry clause per kind — so a doubler multiplies the counts (`ev.MultiplyTokens`) and Academy Manufactor rewrites the kind set (`ev.ReplaceTokenKindsWhere`). Two copies of one doubler are ×4 with no prompt (#792's identical window); two DIFFERENT effects are a CR 616 ordering prompt the creation's controller answers, and either half can pause — the rest of the batch rides the entry's continuation, and `CreateTokensThenForEffect` is how a caller keeps its "then". Every token the window settles on then enters through `enterBattlefieldThroughPipelineLocked`, #478's one effect-side entry primitive, finished by the same `executeEntryToBattlefieldLocked` a fetched, reanimated or blinked permanent takes — so enters-tapped, enters-with-counters and `fireETBHookLocked` all reach a token, a paused token entry resumes through that frame, and the rest of the batch rides its `entryTail`. Shipped on 4 new cards — **Parallel Lives**, **Anointed Procession**, **Academy Manufactor** and **Mondrak, Glory Dominus** (caveat: its Phyrexian-mana ability) — and cleared **9 catalog caveats**: Doubling Season and Primal Vigor's token halves, the enters-tapped gap on Urabrask the Hidden, Manglehorn and Kinjalli's Sunwing, the enters-with-counters gap on Renata, Called to the Hunt, Dragonstorm Globe and Arwen, Weaver of Hope, and All Will Be One's token clause. Still open and named in #762: a choice INSIDE a creation replacement (Jinnie Fay, Esix, Mirrormind Crown, Moonlit Meditation) and "the first time you would create tokens each turn". CR 111.13's copy-becomes-a-token shipped in #666 (see above) and is a fifth caller of this creation path.

- **A free yes/no for a resolving effect's own controller** (#796, [ADR 0018 amendment 2026-09-18](decisions/0018-triggers-on-the-stack.md)) - `effects.MayChoice{Player, Question, YesLabel, NoLabel, LifeCost, OnYes, OnNo}` (`effects/may_choice.go`): "you may [do X]. If you do, [Y]" asked while an effect RESOLVES, where X is neither a search nor a cost the engine already prompts for. No new prompt kind — it is built on `PendingChoiceConfirm` (#552), which is already a two-way question with card-supplied continuations, addressed by `Chooser`, classified in the gate, enumerated for bots and rendered by the client modal. `Player` defaults to the controller ("you may") and names any seat otherwise, which is the whole of what #568's opponent-facing cards needed. Branches are ctx-taking effects bound to a FRESH `Context` on the game the resolver hands back, so they rewind with an undo like every other continuation. **3 cards converted:** Eden, Seat of the Sanctum (now `CompletenessFull` — one activation, the question after the mill, and the #636 reflexive trigger picking its target after the sacrifice, so a card the mill just binned is a legal pick); Mask of Memory (the optional draw is a real question, and the linked discard happens only on the branch that drew); Crossway Troublemakers (the pay-2-life decision moved from the trigger's CR 603.5 prompt to the ability's resolution, where the card prints it). `OtherThan(id)` joined `effects/targets.go` for Eden's "another".

- **An opponent's non-mana choice at resolution** (#568, same ADR amendment) - `PendingChoiceOptionPick` (`option_pick`, `game/option_pick.go`): "choose one of the following", addressed to ANY seat, answered with `{option_index: N}` (routed by kind — zero is the commonest answer). An option is a label, a declared life cost, and optionally the cards it is about; what it MEANS lives in the frame the queuing effect supplies. **A pile split needs no kind at all** — it is two chained prompts to two different seats (#552): `choose_cards` to the splitter with a floor of zero, then an `option_pick` back to the controller whose two options each carry a pile (`Game.QueuePileSplitForEffect` / `effects.PileSplit`). Legality is enforced at QUEUE time (CR 608.2 "as much as possible"): an option the chooser cannot take is never offered, and the first option must be one that always works, because that is the answer `legal.choiceMoves` marks always-legal. **Redaction is one function**, `protocol.redactChoiceCards`, applied to `Options` and to every option's cards: a non-knower loses the card entirely (dropped, not a back — an instance ID out of a hidden zone is the correlation handle PR #513 closed), and the CHOOSER keeps unknown cards as answerable backs only over their own material or another player's HAND, whose size is public (CR 400.2).
