So a cross-seat prompt over a library must reveal first (#549), which every printed card of the family does. **A dropped option pick still finishes the card** (#1006): the departure table's `dropDefault` action runs the frame with `game.NoChoiceIndex`, from one performer (`runChoiceDropActionLocked`) reached both by the departure sweep and by `dropChoiceLocked`, the door every prune uses — before it, a Torment of Hailfire stopped at the victim who conceded and a pile split put neither pile anywhere. **And a dropped mid-card `choose_cards` does too** ([#1225](https://github.com/krakenhavoc/cmd_and_ctrl/issues/1225), [ADR 0018 amendment 2026-09-23](decisions/0018-triggers-on-the-stack.md)): the third shape a dropped prompt can have is a `chooseCardsFrame` with no run link and no option frame — `effects.SacrificeChoice`, the sacrifice in the MIDDLE of an effect — and `dropDefault` ran nothing for it, so Torment of Hailfire stopped at the victim whose BOARD emptied, one prompt further in than the drop #1006 fixed. `defaultDroppedChoiceLocked` now runs that frame with nothing picked, which is the same closure and the same empty answer every one of those callers already runs when the candidate list was empty at queue time. **5 cards converted:** Fact or Fiction (its "which opponent separates" caveat was cleared by #929's choose-a-player prompt and it is now `CompletenessFull`); Torment of Hailfire (X repetitions x each opponent, asked strictly one at a time so a later repetition sees an earlier one's answer); Combustible Gearhulk; Charismatic Conqueror; Painful Quandary. **Chain of Vapor shipped in #920**, whose resolving-item slot is what its copy leg was waiting on (see the row below). Braids, Arisen Nightmare, Tempt with Bunnies and Druid of Purification are unblocked by this seam and still need their own files and their own gap check.

- **A spell copying itself at resolution** (#920, [ADR 0043 amendment 2026-09-18](decisions/0043-copy-effects.md)) — `resolveTopOfStackLocked` removes an item's `StackMeta` entry before it calls `OnResolve`, which is right as far as the STACK is concerned, and left `CopySpellForEffect` — which finds its source through that map — with nothing to copy when the resolving spell was the source. CR 707.10 allows exactly that ("that player may copy this spell"), and the whole Chain cycle prints it. **One slot, `Game.resolving`** (`game/resolving_item.go`), holding the `*StackItem` and a VALUE copy of the card the stack held: by the time the copy is actually made neither is otherwise reachable, because the copy decision is a PROMPT and the spell has already been routed to its owner's graveyard while it is open. The copy is therefore built from last-known information — in the rules the spell is still on the stack, since CR 608.2n puts it in the graveyard as the final step of its own resolution. **Lifetime is one event batch:** the slot is set when an item begins to resolve and cleared by `beginEventBatchLocked`, which runs at exactly the two points where play moves on (#829) — the next resolution and the cursor entering a step. That is what makes it survive any number of paused continuations belonging to the resolution that opened it and not one event past it, and it is safe because a resolution-time prompt blocks the table, so neither boundary can be crossed while a copy decision is open. `stackSpellLocked` consults the slot only for the resolving item's OWN id, so Reverberate pointed at a spell countered in response still gets `ErrCardNotFound`. Clone carries it (undo across the prompt); the persisted snapshot drops it and the drift plan says why, the `enteringTokens` precedent. **2 cards converted:** Chain of Vapor (`CompletenessFull` — the bounced permanent's controller is asked, the sacrifice gates the copy, and the copy is theirs per CR 707.10b) and Chain of Smog (caveat: the two cards discarded are random, which is `DiscardCards` everywhere and not this card's doing). Still open and NOT closed by this: copying an ABILITY item (Strionic Resonator, the ability-copy row above) is a different shape — an ability item carries a resolution closure rather than a card — and the slot parks ability items only so "the item currently resolving" has one answer.

- **Choose-a-player / choose-an-opponent prompt** (#929, [ADR 0018 amendment 2026-09-18](decisions/0018-triggers-on-the-stack.md)) — `Game.QueueChoosePlayerForEffect(ChoosePlayerPrompt{Chooser, Among, Source, Question, Item, Then})` (`game/choose_player.go`) and the card-facing `effects.ChoosePlayer{Chooser, Among, Except, Question, Then}` (`cards/effects/choose_player.go`), with `Among` one of `Players`, `Opponents` or `OpponentsOf(id)`. **No new prompt kind**: it is a `PendingChoiceOptionPick` with one option per eligible seat, labelled with that seat's name, so the choice gate, `legal.choiceMoves`, the wire projection and the client modal all answer it unchanged. It is the one Options-bearing prompt with nothing to hide — who is seated is public (CR 400.2) — so `redactChoiceCards` drops nothing and every viewer sees the same list. **The answer rides `StackItem.Payload`** as a `TargetPlayer` ref (#636's field, not a second one; `Targets` was not an option because a `pick_target` answer replaces it wholesale). A clause that asks twice appends twice in ask order: `ctx.ChosenPlayers()` is the exclusion list behind "choose a SECOND player" and `ctx.ChosenPlayer()` is the most recent answer, with a `TargetNone` marker recorded when a question could not be asked so a later clause never reads the earlier clause's player. **Eligibility is CR 800.4a** — a seat that has left is never offered — and the remaining seats are offered most-life-first, which IS the bot policy (the first option is the enumerator's always-legal one; see `docs/bot.md`). A prompt whose chooser is already gone when the question would be asked is simply not queued and the absence is recorded; reassigning one whose chooser leaves AFTER it is queued is `reassignChoiceLocked` (`game/pending_choice.go`), the CR 800.4g/h/i function that landed with the CR 800.4 remainder in #959. **4 cards converted:** Gluntch, the Bestower (new — three exclusive choices in one trigger, with the chosen player's own pick of a creature chained off the first); Skullwinder (new — the controller names an opponent and that opponent then picks from their own graveyard, which also takes it off the pick-from-graveyard row); Slithermuse (from an auto-pick to a real prompt, caveat cleared, now `CompletenessFull`); Fact or Fiction (the controller now names the opponent who separates the piles, clearing the caveat the row above carried, also `CompletenessFull`). **The as-enters half landed in #980** — see the row below; True-Name Nemesis is no longer waiting on anything. Sandstone Oracle (#448) is unblocked by it too — #952's re-triage moved it off the closed #744 and onto this row, and the row closed underneath it. Victory Chimes now needs exactly one thing, and it is not this: a mana ability whose mana goes to a player other than its controller (a mana ability resolves with no stack and no prompt, CR 605.3b, and `ManaAbilityShape` has no recipient) — its "untap during each other player's untap step" half has been expressible since #74 shipped `Spec.UntapStep`.

- **Durations of continuous effects** (#755, [ADR 0063](decisions/0063-durations-and-control.md)) — `ScopedStatic.Duration` replaces `ExpiresAfterTurn`, and one expiry function (`durationExpiredLocked` in `game/duration.go`) decides when every continuous effect in the game is over. Four kinds: `UntilEndOfTurn` (CR 514.2, unchanged), `UntilYourNextTurn` (ends as that player's next turn begins, counted on the new `Player.TurnsBegun` from [ADR 0059](decisions/0059-turn-machinery.md) Decision 1 — which also ends it at the moment a departed player's turn would have begun, CR 800.4m), `ForAsLongAs` (CR 611.2b, re-evaluated at the top of every layer pass, with `WhileSourceOnBattlefield` and `WhileYouControlSource` conditions keyed on instance ID plus battlefield-entry stamp so a flicker ends it, CR 400.7) and `Indefinite` (CR 611.2a). The sweep runs at the cleanup step, at the turn-began hook and at the top of the layer recompute, and nowhere else. `Duration` is plain data — no closures — so it clones and is ready to persist when #515 makes the ability re-derivable; an optional pin drops an entry when the one object it exists to affect is gone, which is what keeps an indefinite effect from sitting in the snapshot census for the rest of the game. The registry is renamed `Game.ScopedStatics` / `RegisterScopedStaticForEffect` (the census keeps its `turnScopedStatics` wire key). Card-side: `StaticForDuration`, `SnapshotAffected` and the four `Duration…` builders in `cards/effects/durations.go`; `StaticUntilEOT` forwards to them. Shipped on Mass Diminish (until your next turn, with flashback), Sower of Temptation (for as long as), Agent of Treachery (no stated duration). Not folded in: `TurnScopedReplacements`, ADR 0057's `TurnScopedGameEndGates` and ADR 0045's `TurnScopedBlockRules` still clear wholesale at cleanup, because no card needs a longer duration from them yet. Decision 8 was tested by #1200 and held: Teferi's Protection's *"your life total can't change"* looked like the first replacement that wanted a duration and turned out to be a **player-scoped** statement, so it went on `Player.Statics` with this same `Duration` and this same `durationExpiredLocked` ([ADR 0085](decisions/0085-life-total-cant-change.md) Decision 1) — which also names the reason a closure registry is the wrong place to put a turn-cycle-long effect: it is censused `dropped` and costs the table its restore point for as long as the effect lasts. Teferi, Time Raveler's +1 and Alela's goad still carry their caveats — both are "until your next turn" in prose and neither is a layer effect (a cast-timing permission, #760; a goad marker the engine does not enforce).

- **Gain / exchange control from spells and abilities** (#756, [ADR 0063](decisions/0063-durations-and-control.md)) — `Game.GainControlForEffect(source, target, controller, duration, label)` registers a layer-2 `ScopedStatic` in the bucket Mind Control already used, so `Card.BaseController` is the reversion, CR 613.7 timestamp ordering against another control effect is free, and `materialiseControlLocked` carries the consequences: summoning sickness under the new controller (CR 302.6) and removal from combat (CR 506.4). The latter now clears the attack and block ANNOUNCEMENTS as well as the declarations, through one `removeFromCombatLocked` helper — without it a creature stolen mid-combat stayed marked as already-declared and could never fire a "whenever ~ attacks" trigger again that combat (#871). `ExchangeControlForEffect` is CR 701.12: both objects checked before either effect is registered (701.12b, all or nothing), two entries sharing one timestamp so they are one effect for CR 613.7, both `Indefinite` and each pinned to its own object. Card-side `GainControl{Target, Controller, Duration}` and `ExchangeControl{A, B}` in `cards/effects/control.go`. Shipped on Act of Treason (until end of turn, plus the untap and the haste grant the control change makes necessary), Agent of Treachery, Sower of Temptation and Switcheroo. **A layer-2 delta is now an event** (#930): `materialiseControlLocked` emits one `EventControlChanged{CardID, Actor = gained, Target = lost, Source}` per delta — after the pass, collected by value, so a harvested trigger cannot re-enter the recompute — which covers a theft, an exchange, an Aura destroyed and a duration expiring with one emission point. Card side: `WhenYouLoseControlOfThis` (the item is built for the player who LOST control), `WhenYouGainControlOfThis`, `WheneverAnOpponentGainsControlOfAPermanentYouOwn`. "An opponent of your choice gains control" is unblocked on both halves since #929 — the primitive already took an arbitrary controller, and the choose-a-player prompt has shipped — but no catalog card prints the clause yet, so nothing has been converted for it. Khârn the Betrayer is still uncatalogued for the OTHER half of his third ability: a damage-prevention replacement handing the permanent to "an opponent of your choice". Still open: Zidane, Tantalus Thief wants the sibling "a permanent **from you**" predicate; control of a player's turn (Mindslaver) is not layer 2 at all. Control of a SPELL (Commandeer) is: it landed with [ADR 0104](decisions/0104-gaining-control-of-a-spell.md) (#1745). Unblocked and still to be written: Zealous Conscripts (#384), Captivating Crew (#396), Mob Rule (#395), Coercive Recruiter (#397), Reins of Power (#400), Homeward Path, Humble Defector (#309), Gilded Drake, Perplexing Chimera.

- **Modal and multi-target clauses** (#764, [ADR 0065](decisions/0065-modal-and-multi-target-clauses.md)): a `game.TargetSpec` **is** a target clause and hangs the rest of a multi-clause statement off it (`Rest`), built with `Clauses(first, then…)` and `Distinct(…)`; each clause carries its own predicate and count, is enforced on its own at announce (CR 601.2c) and re-checked on its own at resolution (CR 608.2b). `TargetRef` gains `Slot` / `Mode`, so `item.Targets` stays one flat list in announce order and a positional card file is unchanged; `ctx.ClauseTarget(slot)` and `ctx.ModeTargets(occurrence)` are the new reads. One `game.ModeSpec` now serves three owners — `Spec.Modes`, `TriggeredAbility.Modes` and `ActivatedAbility.Modes` — with `Repeatable` for CR 700.2d and `ModeOption.Effect` (`ModeDoing`) for a bullet's body; the `Register` panic on "targeted modes with Max > 1" is gone. A trigger's modes are chosen as the ability is put on the stack (CR 603.3c) through the new `mode_pick` pending choice, before the CR 603.3d target pick, with unfillable bullets not offered; a spell's at CR 601.2b and an activated ability's at CR 602.2b, in the announcement itself. The client walks the clauses one prompt at a time, the bot enumerates modes-outermost under `MaxExpansionPerSource`, and the stack overlay prints the chosen bullets instead of raw indexes. **9 cards:** Bite Down, Soul's Fire and Aetheric Amplifier are now Full (the first two lost their per-slot caveats, the third gained its second bullet); Resourceful Defense's second slot is a Distinct clause; Gala Greeters trades "the engine picks" for a real mode prompt and keeps a narrower caveat; Kolaghan's Command, Mystic Confluence, Sublime Epiphany and Glissa Sunslayer are new. Still open: "choose one that hasn't been chosen this turn" (a per-source tally — Gala Greeters, Monument to Endurance, Silent Hallcreeper keep a caveat), modes chosen by another player (CR 700.2e), divided damage (CR 700.2i), set-level `Validate` on a clause list (#626), ~~targeting an ABILITY on the stack (Sublime Epiphany's second bullet)~~ — **closed by #1211**, and the Epiphany ships all five bullets — and per-clause retargeting of a spell copy.

- **Statics that function from a graveyard** (#1221, [ADR 0071](decisions/0071-designations-that-switch-abilities-on.md)'s second 2026-09-22 addendum) — closes "Statics read from a non-battlefield zone" (count 3, four cards). `StaticAbility.Zones []ZoneKind` is `ActivatedAbilityShape.Zones`' and `TriggeredAbility.Zones`' sibling, with the same nil default and the same per-DECLARATION scope, read by ONE narrow gather beside the battlefield walk (`Game.declaredZoneStaticsLocked`, `game/static_zones.go`). The gather is `trigger_zones.go` (#925) one consumer over, down to the boot-time index: `staticZones.zones()` answers "does any registered card declare a static from a non-battlefield zone" — nil for almost every game, so the whole walk is one slice read — and `declares(oracleKey)` lets a walked graveyard skip the catalog lookup for every card that does not. The battlefield walk gains exactly one comparison per ability, which is what keeps a declared zone from ALSO applying there: a battlefield Anger is a 2/2 with its own printed haste and no anthem, and the card prints the keyword as a separate line for exactly that reason. `supportedStaticZones` is the GRAVEYARD alone and `effects.Register` refuses the rest at boot, because a zone the gather does not walk would be a declaration the engine silently ignored. Three details of the bound source are rules rather than choices: `Controller = Owner` (CR 108.4 — an Anger milled out of an opponent's library helps THEM), `live: false` (the flag exists only for CR 613.1f silencing, and nothing on the board can silence a card in a graveyard), and a timestamp taken from the source's last battlefield entry, which is unobservable for this whole family because every incarnation is a layer-6 GRANT and grants commute — a graveyard static that SET a characteristic would need a real zone-entry timestamp, and none is printed. The gather goes through `StaticAbilitiesForCard` rather than the raw hook, so the ADR 0071 designation gate is evaluated in the second place an object becomes statics exactly as in the first. **No new invalidation was needed**: #1117 widened the zone-keyed bump so any event crossing a graveyard boundary bumps `layerVersion`, arrivals and departures alike, and this is the first static that makes it observable — "the cached resolution survived the graveyard arrival" is now a one-line assertion instead of an argument. Shipped on Anger, Wonder, Brawn and Valor, all `full`, off one `incarnationAnthem(keyword, land)` constructor. Still open on this family: Yixlid Jailer is NOT this row (a battlefield static ABOUT graveyards — it wants the ability-suppression seam), "as long as this card is in your hand" statics have no card asking yet, and REPLACEMENT effects still have no zone field at all — mana abilities got theirs with #1228, below.

- **A MANA ability that functions from the HAND, and an exile-this cost on it** (#1228, [ADR 0011](decisions/0011-mana-pool-and-auto-tapper.md)'s and [ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md)'s 2026-09-23 addenda) — finishes "Ability activatable from a non-battlefield zone" down to its one remaining non-zone item, and closes the two cards #1221 could not take off the row. CR 605.1a makes "Exile this card from your hand: Add {R}" a mana ability, so it takes the OTHER entry point: `ActivateManaAbility` found its source on the battlefield and nowhere else, and `ManaAbilityShape` had neither a zone field nor a cost component, which is exactly what [ADR 0071](decisions/0071-designations-that-switch-abilities-on.md) Decision 1 note 3 predicted ("the field plus its accessor is the same two lines on the day one does"). `ManaAbilityShape.Zones` is `ActivatedAbilityShape.Zones`' sibling with the same nil default and the same posture — a declared zone is not an ADDITION to the battlefield, so a Spirit Guide that got cast is a 2/2 Ape with no abilities — and `game.supportedManaAbilityZones` is the HAND alone, refused at boot for the rest, the shape `supportedStaticZones` took for the graveyard. `ManaAbilityCost.ExileSelf` is #1221's clause with a SECOND OWNER rather than a second component (`exile_cost.go`'s validator and payer now take the zone and the bit), paid last through the one exit primitive with `MustSettleNow`, so a commander spent this way gets its CR 903.9 window and settles without pausing. The zone and the cost imply each other at boot in both directions: a hand zone with no exile cost is a FREE REPEATABLE mana source, and the auto-tapper's picker states the same demand a second time rather than trusting a check two packages away. **The reader with no CR 602 counterpart is the auto-tapper**, because nothing plans a cycling activation for the player and a mana source is what the planner exists to find: `gatherManaZoneSources` walks the supported piles beside the battlefield loop, `autoManaExileAbilityFor` is `autoTapAbilityFor`'s sibling (two pickers rather than a zone parameter — the battlefield one excludes payments the planner may not decide, this one demands a payment that exists at all), and `tapSource.LeavesHand` is a third tier BELOW #1215's sacrifice tier: a Treasure is a resource already on the table and a card in hand is a spell not yet played, so the order reads `Wanted → LeavesHand → Sacrifices → Frozen`. The executor re-finds each planned card in whatever zone holds it rather than carrying a zone on the plan, mints with `fromTap: false` (CR 106.12a — nothing was tapped, so Mana Reflection does not double it and no triggered mana ability fires), and the undo stack's pre-mutation clone now covers a HAND exit (`internal/ws/undo_exiled_mana_source_test.go`). On the wire, `zone_mana_abilities` is `zone_abilities`' twin on the same per-seat carrier, and **`mana_abilities` is now filtered to the battlefield** — it always meant "what does this permanent do" and only now has a card that would make the difference visible. Shipped on Simian Spirit Guide and Elvish Spirit Guide, both `full`, and they are the entire printed family: a Scryfall scan for "exile this card from your hand" finds nine other cards and all nine are CR 602 abilities that GRANT a mana ability to a land. Still open on this family: a mana ability from a graveyard, exile or the command zone (one line in `supportedManaAbilityZones`, no card asking); ~~`ManaAbilityCost.ExileCards`, "Exile A CARD from your hand" as a battlefield mana ability's cost (Cadaverous Bloom)~~ (closed by #1283, above); and REPLACEMENT effects, which still have no zone field.

- **Abilities from the GRAVEYARD and exile, and an exile-this cost component** (#1221, [ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md)'s 2026-09-22 addendum and [ADR 0071](decisions/0071-designations-that-switch-abilities-on.md)'s) — finishes the ACTIVATED half of "Ability activatable from a non-battlefield zone" that #660 started. The SHAPE was already there and had been since #660; what was missing was every consumer past the activation path. `internal/legal`'s enumerator grew `abilityZones()`, the activation sibling of #1014's five-zone cast walk and deliberately one pile shorter — hand, graveyard, command, exile, and NOT the library, because CR 401.2 makes it hidden and reading it would touch cards the seat is not entitled to see to answer a question whose answer is always "nothing". `protocol/view.go`'s `stampHandAbilities` became `stampZoneAbilities` over the same four, with the CR 108.4 "you" read off `Card.Owner` for exile (one shared pile, everybody's cards) and off the seat for the per-seat piles. **The wire field `hand_abilities` became `zone_abilities`**, one field rather than a second list beside it, and — the half that matters — it stopped riding the hand's own secrecy: a graveyard is PUBLIC, so writing one seat's rows to the exported field would have shipped their `legal_targets` to the whole table, which is #1055 and #1167's surface arriving one field over. The rows ride `castOffers` now and `publicIn` drops them, so the seat whose card it is gets the list and nobody else does (a merge rather than a `stampsFor`, because `stampLegalTargets` files the same map first and replacing it would blank a flashback card's announce surface). `AbilityCost.ExileSelf` is scavenge's and embalm's "Exile this card from your graveyard" — `DiscardSelf`'s sibling one zone over, a second bool rather than a zone on one component because discarding is a CR 701.8 keyword action with its own event and cause while exiling as a cost is a plain CR 406 move with neither; validated before anything is paid, paid last through the one exit primitive with `MustSettleNow`, and refused at BOOT on an ability that does not declare the graveyard. The effects read their own source back out of exile by instance ID rather than off a snapshot on the stack item: a card outside the battlefield has no layers, so the printed power scavenge wants is the same number the graveyard held. `effects.Unearth(cost)`, `Scavenge(cost)`, `Embalm(cost)` and `Eternalize(cost)` build the abilities; `ExileInsteadOfLeavingBattlefield` is factored out of Whip of Erebos and shared with unearth, and its two caveats (turn-scoped, and a bounce bypasses it) are inherited whole. The client's zone browser wires the pop-over it was already capable of rendering and had never been given. Shipped on Dregscape Zombie (unearth), Deadbridge Goliath (scavenge) and Sacred Cat (embalm). Still open on this family: nothing — the mana half closed with #1228, below. Ninjutsu (CR 702.49) shipped with #1227 — the return-an-unblocked-attacker cost and the attacking entry are both built, and the ZONE half of it needed nothing from this row; statics that function from a graveyard are the other row of the same issue.

- **Abilities from the hand, and a discard cost component** (#655 / #660, [ADR 0062](decisions/0062-abilities-and-special-actions-from-the-hand.md)) — closes "Discard-a-card cost on an activated ability" (count 10) and the HAND half of "Ability activatable from a non-battlefield zone" (count 10, 6 of them the cycling cards on both rows). `ActivatedAbilityShape.Zones []ZoneKind` is the CR 113.6 dimension on the ONE activation path: nil means the battlefield, `{ZoneHand}` is cycling, and `ActivateCatalogAbility` finds its source in whatever zone holds it, takes the OWNER as "you" off the battlefield (CR 108.4), and refuses a mismatch with `ErrActivationZoneNotAllowed` before anything is validated or paid. `effects.Register` panics at boot on a non-battlefield ability that declares a tap, sacrifice-this, crew or loyalty component — none of them has a permanent to pay with. `AbilityCost.DiscardSelf` is cycling's "Discard this card"; `AbilityCost.DiscardCards` (`DiscardACard()`, `DiscardCardsMatching(n, label, match)`, `DiscardN`) is the general "Discard N cards" clause, its picks named at announce as `discard_ids`. Both pay through the one discard helper with `DiscardCauseCost`, so `EventDiscardCard` fires per card for every discard payoff, the CR 614 window runs over the exit, and a commander pitched to a cost goes to the graveyard (CR 601.2h / 602.2b — a cost cannot pause on CR 903.9's "may"). `effects.Cycling(cost)`, `Typecycling(keyword, cost, what, pred)` and `BasicLandcycling(cost)` build the ability; activating one emits `EventCycle` (CR 702.29b) and `effects.WheneverYouCycle` is the trigger constructor over it. "cycling" joined `canonicalKeywords`. The wire carries `hand_abilities` on a hand card (owner only) plus `discard_self` / `discard_cost_n` / `discard_cost_label` / `discard_cost_options` on `ActivatedAbilityView`; the enumerator loops the seat's own hand through the same cost solve the battlefield loop uses. Shipped on Raffine's Tower, Ketria Triome, Marauding Mako, Scrounging Skyray, Magmakin Artillerist and Sylvan Reclamation (basic landcycling), all six of which dropped their cycling caveats, and on Fauna Shaman, added as the general component's first card. "When you cycle THIS card" fires from the graveyard (CR 702.29c) via the `TriggeredAbility.Zones` dimension #925 built (the row above) — Magmakin Artillerist dropped its cycle-trigger caveat with #1392; Decree of Pain is not added yet. Unblocked by the cost and still to be written: Tortured Existence (#307), Cryptbreaker (#311), Survival of the Fittest (#384) and Glint-Horn Buccaneer (whose other gap, the activation condition, closed with #743). Foretell (#658) and suspend (#659) are the same ADR's Decision 4, and are now built (see below).

- **Special actions from the hand, foretell and plot** (#658, #1342, [ADR 0062](decisions/0062-abilities-and-special-actions-from-the-hand.md) Decision 4) — the CR 116.2 verb, built: one `special_action {card_id, kind}` wire verb, one engine entry (`game.PerformSpecialAction`), one per-kind timing table (`SpecialActionTimingOKLocked`) that the engine, the bot enumerator and the wire's `available` flag all read. No stack, no announce, nothing to respond to. Foretell is its first kind: the special action pays {2} and exiles the card through the shared exit primitive with `FaceDownKind = foretold`, so ADR 0069's viewers table makes the owner the only knower; the cast out of exile is a per-instance `game.CastPermission` (ADR 0066) scoped to that one object, keyed `"foretell"`, priced at the card's foretell cost and floored at `NotBeforeTurn = turn + 1` (CR 702.143a). "If this spell was foretold" (CR 702.143c) is `StackItem.Foretold`, stamped at announce from the card's face-down state because CR 406.3a turns it face up as it is cast — so it is true of a foretold card cast any way, not only of one cast for the foretell cost. CR 702.143f's game-end reveal joins the owner-leaves half ADR 0069 shipped. `effects.Foretell(cost)` is the card-file line; `Spec.SpecialActions` is the slot; `Register` refuses at boot a kind the engine cannot perform. "foretell" joined `canonicalKeywords`. Shipped on **Saw It Coming**, **Behold the Multiverse** and **Cosmic Intervention** (whose caveat claiming `CastableZones` + `AlternativeCost.FromZone` already covered it was wrong twice and is gone). **A special action's cost is priced through the CR 601.2f pass too, since #1319** — the special action verb used to parse its printed string and pay it directly, so an effect that discounts a special action had nowhere to reach. `CostQuery.SpecialAction` and `CostModifier.SpecialActions` are `.Ability` / `.Activations`'s third door (a cast, an activation and a special action price through the same pass and never share a modifier), `Game.SpecialActionManaCostForEffect` is `AbilityManaCostForEffect`'s special-action twin, and `Game.ForetoldThisTurn` / `ForetoldCountThisTurn` is the per-turn tally "the first card you foretell each turn" reads — bumped in `foretellLocked` only once a foretold card actually lands in exile. `effects.SpecialActionCostsLess` is the card-file constructor. Shipped on **Ranar the Ever-Watchful** (its "create a Spirit" trigger — #1320's own row above — still carries the one-event-batch caveat, #1341). **Plot is the fourth kind, since #1342** ([ADR 0062 amendment 2026-09-23](decisions/0062-abilities-and-special-actions-from-the-hand.md)): `SpecialActionPlot`, a hand kind whose window is `sorcerySpeedOpenLocked` (the keyword's window, not the card's — an instant is plotted at sorcery speed too), and `plotLocked` exiles the card face up with `MoveCauseSpecialAction` and calls #1318's `PlotExiledCardForEffect`, so a card plotted from hand and one Aven Interrupter plotted share one permission. `effects.Plot(cost)` is the card-file line and "plot" joined `canonicalKeywords`; the enumerator, wire row and client menu row needed no code. Shipped on **Djinn of Fool's Fall**, **Spinewoods Paladin**, **Beastbond Outcaster** and **Plan the Heist**. **"When this card becomes plotted" is an event, since #1382** ([ADR 0062 amendment 2026-09-24](decisions/0062-abilities-and-special-actions-from-the-hand.md)): `game.EventBecomesPlotted`, emitted only by `PlotExiledCardForEffect` once the card is in exile and plotted, so the special action and "it becomes plotted" effects both fire it and a plain exile never does; `Actor` is the plotter, `Source` what did it. `effects.WhenThisBecomesPlotted` watches it from exile (#925's `TriggeredAbility.Zones`), and the trigger is the owner's. Shipped on **Longhorn Sharpshooter** and **Aloe Alchemist**. **A special action granted to another card, since #1391** ([ADR 0062 amendment 2026-09-24](decisions/0062-abilities-and-special-actions-from-the-hand.md)): `game.SpecialActionGrant` on `CardDef.SpecialActionGrants` lets a permanent give a kind to a card in another zone — only plot from the top of the controller's own library is built — optionally at the card's own mana cost. `Game.SpecialActionsOfferedLocked(actor, card, zone)` is the one accessor the engine, the enumerator and the view ask; `special_action` gained an optional `cost` to pick between two offers of one kind; the view stamps the rows on the library top (owner's frame only) and the client shows them as pills beside the library pile. Shipped on **Fblthp, Lost on the Range**. Still open on this family: Starnheim Unleashed's {X}{X}{W} foretell cost, and the "was foretold" payoffs (Poison the Cup, Haunting Voyage) — the bit they read exists, the cards are not written.

- **Suspend** (#659, [ADR 0062](decisions/0062-abilities-and-special-actions-from-the-hand.md) Decision 4) — the special-action verb's second kind, and the one that closes the "Suspend (CR 702.62)" row outright. The special action pays the suspend cost and exiles the card FACE UP with N time counters, at the card's own casting speed and never under split second (CR 702.62c). Having time counters in exile IS being suspended — no flag, which is what makes a Path to Exile'd copy of the same card inert. The countdown is an exile-zone triggered ability (#925's `TriggeredAbility.Zones = {ZoneExile}`, the dimension that issue built for this), grown by `buildDef` from the suspend declaration so no card file writes it; it fires only on its OWNER's upkeep and only while counters remain. The last tick offers the cast through the same `PendingChoiceMayCast` prompt cascade uses, and a "yes" stamps a per-instance `CastPermission` with `Cost: "{0}"`, `TimingFlash` (the trigger resolves in an upkeep and Rift Bolt is a sorcery, CR 608.2g) and `GrantsHaste` (CR 702.62a). `{0}` rather than an empty override is also what makes Lotus Bloom and Ancestral Vision castable at all: their printed cost is CR 118.6's unpayable one, and CR 118.6a's exit is exactly an alternative price. X is locked at 0 by #873's shared rule, with nothing suspend-specific. `effects.Suspend(n, cost)` is the card-file line; "suspend" joined `canonicalKeywords`. **The declared-`ZoneExile` shape is retired**: S29 allowed a card-level `CastableZones: exile` "for the shape suspend and foretell will use", `TestExileCastAllowedByCardLevelDeclaration` pinned it, and it was the wrong shape — it opens exile for every copy of the card at any time, so a Path to Exile'd Rift Bolt would have been castable for free. `CastSpell` and `validateCastPathLocked` now take a per-instance permission as the only way in, `effects.Register` refuses the declaration at boot, and the test is replaced by one asserting the declaration buys nothing. Shipped on **Rift Bolt**, **Lotus Bloom** and **Ancestral Vision**. **#990 retired both of #659's declared simplifications.** CR 702.62b's two triggered abilities are two `TriggeredAbility` entries — `game.SuspendUpkeepTrigger()` removes a counter and stops, and `game.SuspendLastCounterTrigger()` watches `EventCounterPlaced` with a post-change count of zero and makes the offer — so a removal from Jhoira's Timebug, Clockspinning or Vampire Hexmage ends the countdown exactly as the upkeep does, each ability is separately counterable, and they cannot double-fire because the offer lives in one of them. CR 702.62a's haste is now a `ScopedStatic` with a `ForAsLongAs` duration — `game.UntilYouLoseControlOfDuration`, whose new `WhileYouControlSourceOnceItLands` condition holds while the object is still a spell on the stack and is false the moment the named player stops controlling it — so the grant outlives the turn, ends for good on a control change (CR 400.7: getting the creature back does not get the haste back), and is swept the moment a non-permanent spell leaves the stack. The layer listener bumps on `EventControlChanged` for the same fix: the pass that MOVES control writes `Card.Controller` after the layer walk has read it, so without the bump a stale "you control" resolution survived the theft. Still open on this family: Greater Gargadon (activating from exile while suspended needs the per-instance ACTIVATION grant, ADR 0062 open question 2) and Hypergenesis (#654).

- **Madness** (#657, CR 702.35, [ADR 0066](decisions/0066-granted-cast-and-play-permissions.md) amendment 2026-09-19) — the last keyword on the discard row, and the one that needed no new model at all. A card file declares one string, `Spec.Madness: "{R}"`, and `buildDef` grows both printed abilities from it (`game.MadnessReplacement`, `game.MadnessTrigger`) so no card writes either and none can forget half the keyword. The CR 702.35a replacement watches `RepEventDiscard` (#650) and reads NO cause — a discard as a cost, an effect's instruction and the cleanup step's hand-size trim all work, which is what the Fiery Temper ruling (2022-12-08) says — and it is found by the SELF-replacement pass in `gatherActiveReplacementsLocked`, the one that consults a card that is not on the battlefield, which is what lets a card in a hand replace its own exit. The exile is FACE UP with no marker (the #94 body said otherwise and was wrong). The "when you do" half is an exile-zone trigger (#925) watching the same `EventDiscardCard`, which fires wherever the card LANDED (CR 701.8a), so `ev.NewZone == ZoneExile` is what distinguishes a madness discard from one that reached a graveyard, a library (Library of Leng) or the command zone (CR 903.9). Its "may" is the `PendingChoiceMayCast` prompt cascade and suspend use; a "yes" stamps a per-instance `CastPermission` priced at the madness cost, keyed `"madness"` (so #653's "if its madness cost was paid" will read it off `StackItem.AltCost`) and `TimingFlash` — CR 608.2g, and the first use of the value ADR 0066 reserved for this keyword by name. A "no" puts the card into its owner's graveyard inside the trigger's resolution (CR 702.35b), through the exit primitive so a discarded commander is still offered the command zone. "madness" joined `canonicalKeywords`. Shipped on **Fiery Temper** and **Big Game Hunter**, with **Olivia's Dragoon** as the enabler that proves the COST-discard road (#660's `DiscardACard()`, which settles without pausing per CR 601.2h). Declared simplifications, both cascade's: the accepted cast is a GRANT rather than an inline cast, so there is a CR 117.3b response window paper does not have, and a card accepted but never cast goes to its owner's graveyard at the next end step rather than at the trigger's resolution. One declared looseness: if another replacement (Rest in Peace, Leyline of the Void) was the one that exiled the discarded card, the trigger cannot tell and offers the cast anyway — unreachable by a player acting in their own interest, since CR 616.1 asks the discarding player and madness is strictly better for them. Still open behind it: Avacyn's Judgment (divided damage plus #653's cast provenance) and Asylum Visitor.

- **Trigger doubling** (#752, [ADR 0018 addendum](decisions/0018-triggers-on-the-stack.md#addendum-2026-09-17-trigger-doubling-cr-6032d--accepted)): `Spec.TriggerDoublers` counts extra instances at the event harvest, using current or last-known characteristics and simultaneous-exit snapshots. Each instance has its own optional/target prompt and is attributed on the stack and prompt. Counts add (two doublers give three triggers); delayed/reflexive/manual triggers are excluded, while evoke and Saga abilities participate where their causes match. Panharmonicon, Teysa Karlov and Isshin are the reference cards; Cloud, Midgar Mercenary gains its missing ability. The accepted `OncePerBatch` limitation remains: a filter that matches only a later member of an already-open batch can undercount. Other first-wave cards remain follow-ups under #752; trigger suppression and copying an ability remain separate seams.

- **Fear, intimidate, shadow, horsemanship and skulk** (#825) — canonical keywords enforced by the shared block-pair check, from effective colors/types/power. Importing a card with one of these printed abilities now retains it; keyword-only creatures need no effect file. Each refusal carries a server-built explanation; the enumerator and block-decision signal read the same check, and the bot's attack estimate reads the public characteristics. First catalog consumers: Shizo, Death's Storehouse; Bladetusk Boar; Looter il-Kor; Lu Xun, Scholar General; Furtive Homunculus. Cover of Darkness and Bellowing Tanglewurm are enabled by the mechanic but still need card entries. Parameterized blocking rules and block-count bounds remain #750.

- **Cumulative upkeep** (#567, CR 702.24): `CumulativeUpkeep(label, cost)` in `cards/effects/cumulative_upkeep.go` is the whole keyword in one constructor over primitives that already existed — an `AtYourUpkeep` trigger, `AddCounter` for the age counter (`game.CounterAge`, CR 122.1d) and `PayUnless` for "sacrifice it unless you pay". The cost is not fixed when the card is written, and the fix is `strings.Repeat(cost, age)` at resolution: three counters on a "cumulative upkeep {1}{U}" permanent is `{1}{U}{1}{U}{1}{U}`, which `ParseCost` accumulates, so hybrid and Phyrexian symbols stay separate symbols to pay rather than a multiplied number. The prompt is `pay_unless` through `effects.UpkeepPayUnless` — the shape of that kind that stops the table, because unlike Rhystic Study it asks the ACTIVE player during their own upkeep and the answer decides whether a permanent is still on the battlefield. Both narrowings on `(*game.Game).ChoicePromptBlocksTable` are now DERIVED from the board and read by the engine gate and the enumerator alike (ADR 0018 §6 is amended for the prompt, not the kind): `PendingChoice.GuardsStackItem` (#951, `game/counter_unless_paid.go`) blocks while the object a decline would counter is still on the stack, and `PendingChoice.OwedInStep` (#997, `game/upkeep_pay_unless.go`) blocks while the cursor is still in the step the prompt was raised in — which is Stasis's and Pact of Negation's rule as well as cumulative upkeep's. `PayUnless.Blocking` is gone: the per-card switch #567 shipped was missed by both of the cards written after it. "Cumulative upkeep" does NOT join `canonicalKeywords`, for ward's reason: it carries a cost and a bare token has nowhere to put one. Shipped on Mystic Remora, both halves. Still open: non-mana cumulative upkeep — "Pay 2 life" (Glacial Chasm) and "Sacrifice a creature" (Phyrexian Soulgorger) are the same trigger with a payment the pay-or-else prompt cannot parse.

- **Untap-step choices** (#826, [ADR 0070](decisions/0070-untap-step-choices.md)): CR 502.3's "the active player determines which permanents they control will untap" is a real decision, and the untap step's turn-based action can pause for it. `Spec.UntapCaps` is "players can't untap more than N ⟨kind⟩ during their untap steps"; `Spec.UntapOptOuts` is "you may choose not to untap this during your untap step". Both families raise ONE prompt, the `untap_choice` kind, over the permanents actually in question — a cap that does not bind and an untapped opt-out permanent ask nothing. Several caps compose as one solver (`untapChoicePlan.legal`: at most N per cap, and no wasted untap, since untapping is otherwise mandatory), run through `ChooseCardsPrompt.Validate` so the submit path and the enumerator share one copy. Nothing untaps until the determination is complete, and `exitUntapStepLocked` is the step's one exit, called from the step-entry hook or from the prompt's continuation. Shipped on Winter Orb, Static Orb, Winter Moon (caps) and Rust Tick, Amber Prison (opt-outs, with their "for as long as this remains tapped" tap abilities declared as caveats). Still open: the per-source linked-object field those tap abilities need (ADR 0058 Decision 8's second bullet, designed in ADR 0070 Decision 6 and not built), Smoke and Stoic Angel (a restriction with a cost, not a cap), Dovin Baan's emblem, and exert.

- **Untap holds: "doesn't untap … for as long as you control ~ / ~ remains tapped"** (#1313, CR 611.2b / CR 502.3, [ADR 0058 amendment 2026-09-23](decisions/0058-doesnt-untap.md), building [ADR 0070](decisions/0070-untap-step-choices.md) Decision 6): ADR 0058 Decision 8 said these need "a per-source linked-object field". They do not. The effect is about the TARGET, and the target already carries a per-object list of reasons it will not untap. `UntapSkip` gains `While *Duration` (ADR 0063's type, so one `durationExpiredLocked` decides when it ends). With `While` set, the entry is a HOLD: `untapSkippedForLocked` reads it at every untap step of the permanent's controller, `consumeUntapSkipsLocked` never uses it up, and `sweepUntapHoldsLocked` drops it at the end of the first layer pass after its duration ends. That pass runs after layer 2 is materialised, so losing control of the source and regaining it does not revive the hold (the Dungeon Geists ruling). `WhileSourceRemainsTapped` is a new `DurationCondition`. The hold is cloned, snapshotted (`nextUntapSkips[].while`) and cleared on a zone change by the code that already does this for the one-shot marker. It projects as `CardView.no_untap.static`, including face-down, so the badge, the footer, the heuristic and the auto-tapper's `Frozen` ordering read it with no new wire field. Card helpers: `TapAndHoldWhileYouControlThis`, `TapAndHoldWhileThisRemainsTapped`, and `DoesntUntapWhile` for any other duration. Proof cards: Ty Lee, Chi Blocker (prowess is still #706), Dungeon Geists, Tidebinder Mage, and Rust Tick and Amber Prison, which lose their tap-ability caveats. Writable next: Merchant Raiders, Time of Ice. Icefall Regent still waits on a cost-modifier shape for "spells that target this cost {2} more". Out of scope: Spider-Woman, Secret Agent's "can't become untapped" (an untap-primitive rule, like stun), and the "for as long as you control ~ and ~ remains tapped" control effects (Willow Satyr, Rubinia Soulsinger, Hivis of the Scale).

- **Untap restrictions, next-untap markers and stun counters** (#751, [ADR 0058](decisions/0058-doesnt-untap.md)): `Spec.UntapStepRestrictions` checks self, attached and filtered restrictions at the controller's untap step after layers. `DoesntUntapNextUntapStep` and `TapAndFreeze` record controller- or player-keyed markers that survive skipped steps and snapshots. Stun counters replace any attempted untap, including effect and sandbox untaps. The first wave adds the Monoliths, Mana Vault, Meekstone, Back to Basics, Intruder Alarm, freeze spells and stun cards, and completes Tangle. The board shows a tapped-only badge and hover explanation; bots and auto-tap account for the next missed untap. Exert remains a separate seam; choose-N untaps closed with #826 (below), and duration-bound locks with #1313 (below).

- **Granted cast and play permissions** (#652, [ADR 0066](decisions/0066-granted-cast-and-play-permissions.md)) - `game.CastPermission` (`game/cast_permission.go`) is the ONE model of "an effect lets you cast or play a card from a zone its own text does not open", and it replaced `ExilePlayPermission` rather than sitting beside it: impulse exile, airbend, warp, cascade and a defeated Siege's face grant are all `CastPermission` values now. Two scopes — `ScopeCards` names card OBJECTS (one card, or a set locked at resolution under CR 611.2c), `ScopeStanding` is a rule over a zone narrowed by a pure-data `PermissionFilter`. Three homes, each chosen so a duration falls out: `Player.CastPermissions` for stored grants (a `game.Duration` since #945 — ADR 0063's one duration vocabulary, `UntilEndOfTurn` for impulse exile and Snapcaster, `UntilEndOfYourNextTurnDuration` for Reckless Impulse, the `WhileInZone` kind for airbend, warp and foretell, plus a `NotBeforeTurn` FLOOR that is not a duration at all: CR 702.185a's and CR 702.143a's "on a later turn"), the battlefield for standing ones (`Spec.CastPermissions` → `CardDef` → `CatalogCastPermissions`, derived per query so two Underworld Breaches compose), and nothing at all for the rest. CR 400.7 is an identity check rather than a sweep: `Card.ObjectEpoch` counts how many times a card has become a new object and a permission names `{instance, epoch}`, which replaced six hand-written "zero the grant on the way out" sites (one of which was missing). One query — `Game.CastPermissionForLocked` — is read by the cast path, the view (`castable_here`, and since #978 every announce-time stamp on an exiled or library-top card) and the bot enumerator, so none of them can disagree; the price rides the same object, synthesising the `AlternativeCost` the cast is judged under, so CR 702.34a's "exile it if the flashback cost was paid" (now on `StackItem.AltCostExiles`) and CR 702.138b's "escaped" work however the permission arrived. A card that both prints and is granted the same key keeps its PRINTED cost. The PRICE question itself has one answer since #673 — `Game.CastOffersForLocked`, the list of CR 118.9 costs a cast may claim out of a zone, with a nil entry for the printed one — and since #1012 the view reads it too, so `alternative_costs` on the wire is that list verbatim and the client's picker and the bot's move list are the same set by construction. Two wire facts are derived from it and from nowhere else: `castable_here` is "no `cant_cast` and at least one claimable price" (#1015 — an escape card in a graveyard too small to pay for it is no longer a cast surface), and the new `alternative_cost_required` is "the printed cost is not among them", which the cost picker reads instead of inferring. Still open here: a permission over a card in somebody ELSE's graveyard reaches the view as nothing at all (#1022). `CastPermission.Timing` is where a grant says it opens the sorcery-speed gate, and no card in this batch does. Card side: `effects.GrantFlashbackToCard`, `effects.GrantCastFromYourGraveyard`, `Spec.CastPermissions`. Shipped on Snapcaster Mage, Past in Flames, The Grim Captain's Locker and Underworld Breach. Queued behind the same model, each card work plus a key: madness (#657), foretell (#658) and suspend's free cast (#659) have all shipped on it, and madness is `CastPermission.Timing`'s first user — the `TimingFlash` value ADR 0066 reserved for it by name. Out of scope and stated in the ADR: Muldrotha's per-type per-turn allowance, the Descendants' Path "reveal and cast it free" family, and #760's announce-time cast gate (hooked, not built).

- **Play and cast from the top of your library** (#765, [ADR 0066](decisions/0066-granted-cast-and-play-permissions.md)) - `castZoneFromWire` and `castSourceZoneLocked` gained `library`, resolved to the caller's own; the top-card restriction rides `CastPermission.TopOfLibraryOnly` rather than the zone, and lands go through `cast_spell` so a land on top reaches the same land branch, spends the same land drop (CR 305.2) and runs the same CR 614 entry pipeline. Visibility (CR 401.5) is per PLAYER and kept on the POSITION, not on the card — `Card.KnownBy` cannot express "the top card", which changes identity on every draw, mill, shuffle and scry — so `LibraryTopVisibility` (`game/library_top.go`, `Spec.LibraryTopVisible`) is derived from the battlefield and resolved LAZILY at the only two places that need an answer: the view projection and `CastPermissionForLocked`. Nothing has to be invalidated and CR 401.6 falls out. The per-viewer filter is widened by exactly one case: an opponent's library keeps its top card, and only when that viewer knows it AND the standing rule put it there — a one-shot "reveal the top two" does not open the zone. Shipped on Bolas's Citadel (caveat: its ten-permanent sacrifice ability), Oracle of Mul Daya, Courser of Kruphix and Realmwalker. **#1035 made the position rule follow the CARD**: it used to read the permission HOLDER's own library, which scoped every printed clause by accident ("your library", all four cards) and made a permission over ANOTHER seat's library top open nothing at all — silently, before the cast path, the enumerator or the view could ask. Three pieces came with it and are the shape a cross-seat card needs: `CastPermission.ZoneOwner` names the pile a STANDING permission is over (no seat named still means the holder's own, so two Coursers do not read each other's library); `CastPermission.SeesLibraryTop` carries CR 401.5's LOOK, because the per-library derivation cannot express "you may look at the top card of THEIR library" — it is per (library, viewer) and granted by a resolution rather than printed as a static ability; and `LibraryTopVisibleToLocked` is the one predicate both the cast path and the knower stamp read. Xanathar, Guild Kingpin is the printed shape and is not in the catalog; nothing catalogued needs any of it, which is why the hole went unnoticed. Still waiting on something else: Conspicuous Snoop (#394); Korlessa, Scale Singer (#398); Emperor Mihail II (#403); Sigarda, Font of Blessings (#394); Augur of Autumn (#303); Thundermane Dragon (#397, cast provenance #653); Mystic Forge and Vizier of the Menagerie (spend-mana-as-though restrictions); Fortune Teller's Talent (#333, Class levels).

- **Exhaust ("Activate each exhaust ability only once")** (#1181, [ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md) exhaust addendum 2026-09-22) — 41 printed cards, and the shape is ONE record plus ONE bit. **`Game.Activations`** (`game/activation_tally.go`) counts ANNOUNCEMENTS — CR 602.2b, not resolution, because an exhaust ability countered on the stack is still spent — in two scopes at once: `Ever`, never reset, which exhaust reads, and `Turn`, emptied on the turn advance beside `TurnTally`, which the per-source activations row above will read. Both are keyed by `ObjectTallyKey(source, Card.ObjectEpoch, label)`, the key `ResolvedThisTurn` already uses, so CR 400.7 falls out rather than being coded: a flicker refreshes an exhaust ability, a phase-out would not (phasing is not a zone change and must not bump the epoch when it is built), and a copy has its own (CR 707.2). The LABEL and not the ability's index, because an ADR 0071 designation switching an ability on renumbers the list. The card side is `ActivatedAbilityShape.Exhaust bool` and no per-card logic; `effects.Register` panics at boot on a blank label, on two exhaust abilities of one card sharing a label, and on a label that prints the keyword without the bit or the bit without the keyword. **One reader, `Game.AbilityExhausted`**, for the three places the rule has to hold: `ActivateCatalogAbility` refuses with `ErrAbilityExhausted` before anything is paid, `internal/legal` does not enumerate the move, and `ActivatedAbilityView` ships `exhausted` so the client greys the row — #544's rule as a property of there being one reader. It is neither `Condition` (which may hold again tomorrow) nor `ActiveWhen` (which makes the ability absent); Bitter Work prints both and the order is timing → exhaust → condition. `Game.Activations` is classified `carried`, so #1020's round-trip test enforces it, and it rewinds with `Clone` / `RestoreFrom`. **Cards:** Prowcatcher Specialist, Greenbelt Guardian (an exhaust ability beside a repeatable one), Bitter Work and Ba Sing Se (whose earthbend is deliberately NOT exhaust), all four `full`. **The MANA half landed with #1183** ([ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md) exhaust addendum, note of 2026-09-22): `ManaAbilityShape.Exhaust` / `effects.ManaAbility.Exhaust` are the twin bit, `Game.ManaAbilityExhausted` the twin reader over the SAME record and the SAME key, and `ActivateManaAbility` (CR 605.3b — no stack, no priority) writes it at the point every gate has passed and the first payment is about to be made. TWO write sites, which the CR 602 path does not need: the hand click and the AUTO-TAPPER's executor (`materializePlanLocked`), which taps a permanent and mints its mana directly. FIVE readers: the activation path, `legal.manaMoves`, `ManaAbilityView.exhausted`, the auto-tapper's one shared picker (`autoTapAbilityFor`, so the planner and the executor cannot disagree), and CR 106.7's `ProducibleManaLocked` — the one activation restriction that reader asks about, declared as a weaker-than-printed narrowing so a Reflecting Pool is not priced on mana a spent Loot can never make again. `effects.Register`'s label checks run over BOTH ability lists with ONE `seen` set, because the two kinds share one key space on one object. **Card:** Loot, the Pathfinder, `full`. **The cards that read the record from OUTSIDE landed with #1184** ([ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md) exhaust addendum, note of 2026-09-22), and they were never one gap — three mechanisms wear one keyword. (1) **The activation event**: `EventActivateAbility`, carrying the ability's `Label` (the record's own key) and an `Exhaust` bit stamped at the announce, because by the time a watcher runs a `SacrificeSelf` cost may have ended the object; `EventManaAbilityActivated` carries the same two at both of its write sites. Built wider than its two cards so the "whenever an OPPONENT activates an ability" row above closes on it. (2) **The permission**: `AbilityExhausted` / `ManaAbilityExhausted` take the ASKING PLAYER, because "as though they haven't been activated" is not a fact about the object — the record still says spent, and every opponent still reads it that way. Not an entry point that clears the record: the permission is continuous while its conditions hold, and a clear would be visible to everyone and survive its source dying, which "as though" never does (CR 609.4). Activating under it writes the record again, which is what makes Elvish Refueler self-limiting with no code. `Spec.ExhaustPermissions` is the card side, gated by ADR 0071 and CR 613.1f like every other static. (3) **The priced activation**: `CostQuery.Ability` is the ability being priced and `CostModifier.Activations` partitions the board's modifiers into cast-pricers and activation-pricers — without the partition, Sphere of Resistance's nil `AppliesTo` ("every spell") would have started taxing every `{T}` ability in the game. One `AbilityManaCostForEffect`, paid by the activation path and read by `internal/legal`, which is #544's rule with the sign reversed: an enumerator pricing at the printed cost would HIDE legal moves. **Cards:** Rangers' Refueler, Afterburner Expert and Elvish Refueler `full`; Boom Scholar `caveats` (the ability's menu row still lists the printed cost, and mana abilities are not priced through the pass). Still open: those two narrowings, and Avatar Kuruk, which wants extra turns (#753).

- **Activation condition on a non-mana activated ability** (#743) - `ActivatedAbilityShape.Condition` / `effects.ActivatedAbility.Condition`, the same `func(g, controller, source) bool` mana abilities carry ([ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md) activation-condition addendum). Checked once in `ActivateCatalogAbility` right after the timing check, before X, targets or any cost (`ErrConditionNotMet`, nothing paid), never at resolution (CR 602.1b), and mirrored in the legal enumerator. `ActivatedAbilityView` and `ManaAbilityView` both carry `condition_unmet`, and the client greys the row. Builders in `cards/effects/activation_conditions.go`: `DuringYourTurn`, `OpponentControlsAtLeast`, `OpponentControlsMore`, `GraveyardAtLeast`, `NoCardsInHand`, `SourceHasCountersAtLeast`, `LifeAtLeastAboveStarting`, beside the existing `ControlsAtLeast`. Sanctum of Eternity lost its sorcery-speed caveat. Shipped on Tectonic Edge, Weathered Wayfarer, Bonders' Enclave, Cryptic Caves, Endless Atlas, Kelpie Guide, Barbarian Ring, Cephalid Coliseum, Rivendell, Sea Gate Wreckage, Minas Tirith, Idol of Oblivion, Speaker of the Heavens, Luminarch Ascension, Sliver Hive, Inventors' Fair, Leechridden Swamp, Mistveil Plains, Lagomos, Hand of Hatred, Hall of Oracles, The Seedcore, Goro-Goro, Disciple of Ryusei, Lilypad Village and Arch of Orazca (the city's blessing read live, with Tendershoot Dryad's caveat). Unblocked and still to be written: Blitzball (#397), Stella Lee, Wild Card (#455). Still waiting on other rows: Kitsa, Otterball Elite (prowess, #706), Glint-Horn Buccaneer (whose discard-a-card cost closed with #660, so it is now unblocked and still to be written), Barad-dûr (amass), Varragoth, Bloodsky Sire and Broadside Bombardiers (per-source activation count), Hydra Broodmaster and Alpha Deathclaw (monstrosity), Metallurgic Summonings (exile-this cost), Ayli, Eternal Pilgrim (reading the sacrificed creature), and Desert (the engine clears combat as the end of combat step begins, so no creature is attacking in the only step Desert can be activated; CR 511.3 keeps them in combat until the step ends, #785). Goro-Goro declares the same gap as a caveat. `SourceIsAttacking`, the step-window builders and `AllOf` from the addendum's table wait for their first card.

- **Landwalk** (#705) - `islandwalk`, `swampwalk`, `forestwalk`, `mountainwalk`, `plainswalk` and `nonbasic landwalk` are canonical keyword tokens, read in the evasion slot of `Game.BlockPairRefusalLocked` (`game/block_legality.go`, `game/landwalk.go`), the one block-pair check `DeclareBlocker`, the legal-move enumerator and the #328 auto-pass signal all call ([ADR 0045](decisions/0045-combat-restrictions.md) addendum, Decisions 7-10). The check is a method on the game, lock-held and read-only (`TestBlockLegalityDoesNotMutate`), and derives the defending player from the attack (a planeswalker's controller, a battle's protector). Both sides are read after layers: a granted landwalk through `HasKeyword`, a land's types through `IsLand` / `HasSubtype` / the new `Card.HasSupertype`, so Urborg switches swampwalk on. A refused block comes back as the `illegal_block` error with a `reason` token and a server-built sentence. Imported cards that print landwalk are enforced from the same change. Shipped on Lord of Atlantis (both caveats gone), Elvish Champion and Goblin King (their landwalk granted, now complete), Master of the Pearl Trident, Cold-Eyed Selkie and Trailblazer's Boots. The rarer variants (snow swampwalk, legendary landwalk, desertwalk) join the token table with their first card. Protection's blocking half (#662) took its reserved slot in the same function; block rules (#750) still have theirs.

- **Sacrifice cost of more than one permanent** (#747) - a sacrifice clause's count is its `TargetSpec`'s `Min == Max`, built with `SacrificeN(n, label, preds…)` for an activated or mana ability (`.SacrificeOther` for the mana one) and `SacrificeNCost(n, label, preds…)` for an additional cost to cast ([ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md) and [ADR 0021](decisions/0021-additional-costs.md) addenda). One validator serves all three sites: exactly N distinct permanents the payer controls, each matching the clause, never the source when the cost also sacrifices it. One payment leaves as one simultaneous exit, so a dies-watcher paid in with the rest sees every death. The enumerator offers one payment per move for N ≥ 2, tokens first, then lower mana value, then the source last; the view ships `sacrifice_options.min`/`max` as N with the options in that order, and the client's picker is a multi-select that confirms at exactly N with a "Choose for me" button. `effects.Register` refuses variable counts. Shipped on Priest of Forgotten Gods, Kuldotha Forgemaster, Tamiyo's Journal, Teysa, Orzhov Scion, Hedron Detonator, Olivia, Opulent Outlaw, Breya, Etherium Shaper and Whisper, Blood Liturgist, and it cleared the caveats on Savvy Hunter, Samwise Gamgee, Sai, Master Thopterist, Magda, the Hoardmaster and Magda, Brazen Outlaw. Still open: Transmutation Font's "with different names" (the set-level row above) and variable counts (the row above). Not this seam: Liliana, Dreadhorde General (a resolution-time edict), Phyrexian Soulgorger (cumulative upkeep's sacrifice payment — the trigger shipped with #567, the payment shape did not), Jolene, the Plunder Queen (a token-creation replacement), Nemata, Primeval Warden (a reflexive "when you do" trigger off a replacement) and Ulamog, the Defiler (ward's sacrifice cost, `WardCost.Sacrifice`, is a separate shape). Unblocked by the cost and still to be written, with another gap the audit named: Pippin, Warden of Isengard (#448, partner with). Zopandrel, Hunger Dominus shipped with S58 (#2061).

- **A spell's own cost modifier** (#746) - `Spec.SelfCostModifiers`, read through `CardDef` by `game.SelfCostModifiersFor(card)` for the card being priced and never from the battlefield ([ADR 0048](decisions/0048-cost-modification.md) addendum). Self modifiers join the board's increase, reduction and floor passes (CR 601.2f), bound to the card as cast with the caster as its controller, so a reduction still stops at generic mana; they apply from any zone the spell is cast from (CR 113.6d), to an alternative cost (CR 118.9d), and for the face being cast. `CostQuery.Targets` carries the announced targets, shown only to a modifier that sets `ReadsTargets`; the legal-move enumerator prices each target set on its own when `CastPriceReadsTargetsForEffect` says something reads targets, and prices once up front otherwise. `CostModifier.Unit` lets an increase add coloured mana (strive). Constructors: `AffinityFor` (CR 702.41a, one instance per entry, CR 702.41b), `CostsMorePerTargetBeyondFirst`, `CostsLessIfItTargets`, plus counting amounts and conditions in `effects/self_cost_modifier.go`. The X picker prices a target-priced spell at one target and quotes the clause from `CardView.target_cost_notes`. Shipped on Thought Monitor, Myr Enforcer, Thoughtcast, Voyage Home, Ghalta, Primal Hunger, Vanquish the Horde, Fireball, Call the Coppercoats, Winged Words, Wizard's Retort, Into the Story, Hour of Revelation, Mortality Spear, Curtains' Call, Price of Fame, Shadow of Mortality, Witherbloom, the Balancer, Mycosynth Golem, Hamza, Guardian of Arashin, Ancient Stone Idol and Hagra Mauling; Blasphemous Act dropped its caveat. Still open: the auto-tap preview has no targets, face or non-hand zone on its query (#696); coloured reductions (CR 118.7c); cost modifiers on activated abilities (the row above); Earthquake Dragon and Metalwork Colossus also wait on #655. Unblocked and still to be written: Not of This World (#385), Sapling Nursery (#450), Thrumming Hivepool (#455), Diamond Weapon (#457), and the audit's clause-only cards (The Great Henge, Embercleave, Excalibur, Sword of Eden, Rumbleweed, The Magic Mirror, among others), each of which still needs its own check for other gaps.

- **Reflexive triggers, "when you do" (CR 603.12)** (#636) - a trigger CREATED by a resolving effect, written `ReflexiveTrigger{Label, Targets, Optional, Cards, Effect}.Apply(ctx)` (or `WhenYouDo(label, effect)` for the plain case) from inside the parent ability's `Effect`, on the branch where the condition actually held. It goes through `Game.QueueReflexiveTriggerForEffect` into the harvester's own dispatch, so it gets the CR 603.3d target pick when it is put on the stack, the CR 603.3d drop when nothing is legal, a CR 603.5 "you may" if it prints one, and a response window above its parent. The payload rides `StackItem.Payload` (`ctx.PayloadCards()`), which is what keeps the `Effect` capture-free across `Clone` / undo. Cards that folded the follow-up into the parent and declared a caveat are converted: Ziatora, the Incinerator; Invasion of Tarkir; Generous Plunderer; Riveteers Overlook and the four b08 Overlook lands (Brokers Hideout, Cabaretti Courtyard, Maestros Theater, Obscura Storefront). Eden, Seat of the Sanctum was the one card here waiting on a SECOND seam — a free yes/no a resolving ability can ask its controller — and #796 closed it (see below); Eden is now `CompletenessFull`. Still waiting: Caesar, Legion's Emperor (modal triggers); Invasion of New Phyrexia (#626), whose back face needed emblems too until #623 landed them (see Closed seams). See [ADR 0026](decisions/0026-delayed-triggers.md) (2026-09-17 amendment) and AGENTS.md §7.

- **Choose a color** (#742, PR #772) - `PendingChoiceColor` (`choose_color`, CR 105.4) in two forms. Stored: `ChooseColorAsEnters(purpose, label)` / `ChooseColorOtherThanAsEnters(purpose, label, color)` on `Spec.AsEnters` write `Card.ChosenColor` (carried by the snapshot, cleared on leaving the battlefield, the S26 `NamedTribe` pattern), read back with `ProducedChosenColor()`, `ProducedColorOrChosen(color)` and `ChosenColorAnthem(p, t)`. At resolution: `ChooseColorThen(purpose, g, chooser, source, question, then)`, a continuation counted in the census, which chains for "each player chooses". Every prompt declares a `game.ColorPurpose` (#780: `ColorForMana` / `ColorForBenefit` / `ColorForHarm` / `ColorForFilter` / `ColorForProtection`), enforced by `color_purpose_guard_test.go` and carried to the wire as `color_purpose`, because CR 105.4 makes all five colours legal and nothing else says which one the card wants. "N mana of any one color" is one `mana_pick` minting N tokens: the produced-mana grammar takes a per-colour count (`{W3|U3|B3|R3|G3}`, built with `OneColorOfAmount(n)` / `ProducedOneColor(fn)`), and the auto-tapper PLANS such a source (#779): one solver candidate per colour, mutually exclusive, with the booked colour carried through to the executor ([ADR 0040](decisions/0040-mana-pipeline.md) #779 addendum). `resolve_choice` routes `color` by kind. Shipped on Thriving Isle, Thriving Heath, Thriving Grove, Thriving Bluff, Thriving Moor, Sea Gate, Citadel Gate, Manor Gate, Cliffgate, Black Dragon Gate, Uncharted Haven, Crossroads Village, Mirage Mesa, Valgavoth's Lair, Coldsteel Heart, Heraldic Banner, Gilded Lotus, Lotus Field (#332), Nyx Lotus, Mona Lisa, Science Geek, White Lotus Tile, Ilysian Caryatid, Sanctum of Fruitful Harvest, Wash Out, Selective Obliteration, Oona, Queen of the Fae, and Caged Sun (#301, #1600 — its "whenever a land's ability causes you to add mana of the chosen color, add an additional one" is a `ManaTrigger`, #763, same family as Wild Growth and Mana Flare), and Throne of Eldraine (#300, #1600 — its mana spends only on monocolored spells of the chosen colour and its draw ability takes only mana of that colour; see "Abilities only some mana can pay, and mana for one-colour or gold spells"). Still waiting on something else: Temple of the Dragon Queen (#386 — the "Reveal-from-hand entry choice" row closed with #1198, but the Temple prints TWO as-enters clauses on one entry, the reveal being conditional besides ("unless you revealed a Dragon card this way or you control a Dragon"), which that row explicitly did not answer); Sunken Citadel (#466, a "spend only to activate abilities of land sources" restriction); Glittering Stockpile (#383, the sacrificed source's last-known stash counters); Gauntlet of Power (#386 — its "whenever a land is tapped for mana, add one mana of the chosen color" is now writable as a `ManaTrigger`, #763, the same one Caged Sun shipped in #1600, and it waits only on being catalogued); Brave the Elements (#399), Flickering Ward (#450), Benevolent Blessing (#459) and the Mother of Runes family (protection, #662 — Mother of Runes herself shipped with it, declaring `ColorForProtection`). Thran Portal (#451) chooses a basic land type, not a colour. The "any combination of colors" cards (Vivi Ornitier, Cascading Cataracts, Axebane Guardian) are a different shape and still unexpressed.

- **X on an activated ability** (PR #550) - `AbilityCost` reads `{X}` out of its mana string rather than a flag a card could forget: `DemandsX`, `XSlots` (Treasure Vault's `{X}{X}` is two) and `FloorX`, with a `MinX` field for a printed floor ("X can't be 0"). X is announced and validated at activation (CR 602.2b), locked onto `StackItem.XValue`, and read back with `ctx.X()`; the enumerator offers one X per ability, the largest affordable ([ADR 0033](decisions/0033-ai-bot-seat.md)). Shipped on Treasure Vault, Helm of Obedience and Soothsaying. The other cards from the old row were not re-triaged: Blast Zone (#308) and Magus of the Candelabra (#398) no longer wait on the cost, and Gogo, Master of Mimicry (#396) still waits on "Ability-copy".

- **Per-turn tally** (#586) - `Game.TurnTally` counts what happened this turn in one place, reset on turn advance: per player (`TurnTallyFor`: life gained and lost, cards drawn, creatures died, tokens created, permanents sacrificed, lands entered, attackers declared, combat damage to players), per player and subtype (`EnteredWithSubtypeThisTurn`, #743: the subtypes a permanent had as it entered, before statics apply, so Lilypad Village is not fooled by a Maskwood Nexus that arrives later), and per ability (`ResolvedThisTurn(source, label)`, `TriggeredThisTurn(source, label)`), with `EventsThisTurn()` for a filtered scan. It closes the trigger half of the old "Once-per-turn trigger / activation tally" row ("this ability triggers only once each turn" reads `TriggeredThisTurn`). Unblocked and still to be written: Morbid Opportunist (#295); Welcoming Vampire (#296); Terrasymbiosis (#300); Tocasia's Welcome (#300). Monument to Endurance (#301) also waits on modal triggers (#764). The activation half is still open: "Per-source activations-this-turn count" above.

- **Library to battlefield without a search, and a random-order bottom** (#745, PR #773) - `Game.PutFromLibraryOntoBattlefieldForEffect(cardID, LibraryEntryOptions{Controller, Tapped})` and its batch twin `PutCardsFromLibraryOntoBattlefieldForEffect`, sharing one body with the hand move (`battlefield_put.go`): controller stamped in the old zone, CR 614 pipeline before the move, move, then `EventZoneMove` / `EventETB` / `AsEnters`; no `EventSearchLibrary`, no shuffle, not a land drop (CR 305.4), a token refused like a nonpermanent (CR 111.8: `Card.IsToken`), resumable since #1322 (a card's entry question — a shockland's life, a Clone's copy — pauses the whole batch, which lands together once the last answer is in; `PutCardsFromLibraryOntoBattlefieldThenForEffect` is the door for a caller that reads the result). The batch runs every card's pipeline against the pre-entry board before anything moves and announces after everything has landed, so "any number" is a simultaneous entry. `Game.PutOnBottomInRandomOrderForEffect(actor, from, ids)` is the public random-order bottom, one draw on the actor's keyed `random_order` stream (ADR 0054 Decision 8, #744 sub-PR 1): it takes only the cards still in a zone of kind `from` (a saved ID whose card has moved on is skipped); from any zone but its owner's library it routes through the exit path (`ToBottom`, CR 614 / 903.9 apply), and a card already in its owner's library is reordered without a zone change; cascade now calls it. Also `LookAtTopOfLibraryForEffect` (knower = the looker only) and `PutIntoGraveyardForEffect` (not a mill). The card side is `effects.PutFromLibraryOntoBattlefield` over a `choose_cards` prompt with `Zone: ZoneLibrary` (the wire already withholds its options and bounds from every seat but the chooser), with `LookAtTopThenMayPutOntoBattlefield` and `RevealUntilThenPutOntoBattlefield` for the two whole sentences. Shipped on Chaos Warp, Coiling Oracle, Genesis Wave, Risen Reef, Explorer's Scope, Thrasios, Animist's Awakening, The Regalia, Lurking Predators, Atla Palani, Ureni of the Unwritten, Majestic Genesis, Gilgamesh (caveat: no reflexive attach, #636), Armored Skyhunter (caveat: Equipment only; an Aura needs a CR 303.4f entry choice) and Esika // The Prismatic Bridge (caveat on the front face: granted mana ability). Still waiting on something else: Tom Bombadil (a "final chapter ability resolves" event), Famished Worldsire (devour), Kamahl's Druidic Vow (the legendary-sorcery cast restriction), Kinnan, Bonder Prodigy (mana-trigger colour), Winota and Doors of Durin (unblocked by #1227: `ZoneEntryOptions.Attacking` is the non-token permanent put onto the battlefield attacking they were waiting on, and the library door takes the same options struct — what is left on those two is ordinary card work), Aang, at the Crossroads (transform), Lonis (a sacrifice-X cost). Cybership, once waiting on face-down (#656), is registered since #1270. Genesis Ultimatum was on that list for "a spell that exiles itself as it resolves" and no longer is: #489 taught the resolution frame not to re-route a spell its own effect has already moved (`spellMovedItselfLocked`), which also unblocks Ascend from Avernus and every other "exile this spell as it resolves" rider outside the flashback and warp paths.

- **Put-from-hand onto the battlefield** (#654, PR #728) - `Game.PutFromHandOntoBattlefieldForEffect(cardID, HandEntryOptions{Controller, Tapped})`: the CR 614 entry pipeline runs BEFORE the card leaves the hand (a paused pipeline leaves it there rather than stranding it), then the move, `EventZoneMove`, `EventETB` and the catalog's `AsEnters` hook — the same four steps the search, reanimation and exile-return paths take. A land put this way is **not a land drop** (CR 305.4), and since #1322 the entry is resumable: the batch runs every card's pipeline against the pre-entry board one at a time, stops on a card's question, and moves them together once the last answer is in (`game/entry_batch.go`; `PutFromHandOntoBattlefieldThenForEffect` for a caller that reads the result). The card side is `effects.PutFromHandOntoBattlefield` on top of #552's pick-from-hand prompt, with `MayPutALandFromHand(card)` / `MayPutALandFromHandTapped(card)` for the sentence five cards share and a `Then` continuation for a rider on the result. Shipped on Eureka Moment, Broken Bond, Chulane, Insidious Fungus's third mode and Spelunking (its Cave life bonus included). Unblocked and still to be written: Growth Spiral (#294); Archaeomancer's Map (#299); Kodama of the East Tree (#301); Ghalta, Stampede Tyrant (#302); Sneak Attack (#306); Cultivator Colossus (#307); Terrain Generator (#310); Arboreal Grazer (#313); +7 more. Two catalog cards still declare the clause as a caveat and are a small edit each: Stoneforge Mystic (its `{1}{W}, {T}` is an activated ability) and Ugin, the Spirit Dragon (the -10 also searches a graveyard and a library). Uro (#653) and the Obstinate Baloth redirect (#650) wait on their own seams, not on this one.

- **Counter removal as an activated-ability cost** (#625) - `AbilityCost.RemoveCounters`, written `RemoveCountersFromThis(kind, n)` or `RemoveCountersFrom(kind, n, label, preds…)`, with kind `""` meaning "a counter" of any kind ([ADR 0020](decisions/0020-activated-abilities.md) addendum). Paid at announce, not replaceable, and not a loyalty activation. A printed 0/0 that pays with its last counter dies, and an uncoded `*` creature does not, by the toughness rule in [ADR 0007 §7](decisions/0007-stack-foundation.md) (#683, and #690/#691 for the rest of that rule: `game.Card.ToughnessIsKnown` is the one gate CR 704.5f has). Shipped on Heart of Kiran (its "rather than pay" crew, as a second ability entry), Dragon's Hoard, Mikaeus, the Lunarch, Benevolent Hydra and Fain, the Broker. The rest of the counter-cost row above is still open: adding a counter, mana abilities, and removals split across permanents.

- **Generic step-entry trigger event** (#588) - `EventStepBegan` carries a typed `Step` and the harvester watches it; `AtBeginningOfYourCombat`, `AtYourPostcombatMain`, `AtEndOfYourCombat`, `AtYourStep` / `AtEachStep` in `triggers_common.go`. The 15 cards that waited on it still need writing: Black Market Connections (#294); Seedborn Muse (#295); Unwinding Clock (#297); Ripples of Undeath (#297); Bender's Waterskin (#298); Mesmeric Orb (#302); Intruder Alarm (#308); Drumbellower (#310); +7 more.

- **AddMana primitive** (PR #341, batch 01) - gave a resolving spell a path to add mana to a pool; unblocked Dark Ritual.

- **DelayedTrigger.ControllerTurnOnly / until-end-of-turn statics** (#314) - unblocked Return of the Wildspeaker and Boros Charm in batch 01, and later The Wandering Emperor's minus ability.

- **EventAttack** (S22) - "whenever ~ attacks" is now a real trigger condition.

- **EventBecomesTarget** (S22) - "whenever ~ becomes the target of a spell or ability" is now a real trigger condition.

- **CopySpell** (#419) - a spell-copy primitive; unblocked Dualcaster Mage in batch 05.
